# pdfcer-gui — what is built, and what is next

**Updated:** 2026-09-05 (twelfth revision — **re-measured against the build about to ship**, at commit `5c01c83`, by running the commands rather than by editing the prose. Eight tracks landed in the twelve hours before it and each appended its own rows; every ✅ below was re-checked at source, and every claimed ABSENCE was grepped before being believed. Fourteen prose claims were found false; seven of them were operator-visible strings. ⚠ **The working tree was NOT mine alone while this was measured** — a second track was writing into `crates/pdfcer-gui/src/panels/comments/` throughout, and the numbers below are a reading taken at a named moment, not a status). **Scope:**
the new shell only. `pdfcer-core` and
`pdfcer` capabilities live in `D:\Dev\pdfcer\docs\FEATURES.md`, whose
**`gui` column is this project's acceptance criteria** — nothing there may
regress at fold-in.

Two lists. The first is what works **today, in the running binary**. The
second is what does not, **in the order it is planned**.

**A row is ticked only when an operator can reach it in a real build.**
Not when the code exists, not when a test passes. Three panels in the old
shell shipped with a body, a rail entry and a diagnostic step and **no
control anyone could click**, and every verification passed for the whole
of their shipped life. That is what this bar exists to prevent.

**Legend:** ✅ reachable · 🔨 in progress · ⬜ planned · ⛔ blocked, with
the blocker named.

## ★★★ What landed in the twelve hours before this revision — **all of it built, none of it driven**

Eight parallel tracks landed between 2026-09-04 07:48 and 2026-09-05 02:24.
Every row below was **re-verified at source for this revision** — a registered
command id, a reachable control, or a dispatch arm that does the thing — and
not one of them has been exercised in a running binary.

★★★ **That is why every row here is ⬜ and not ✅**, and the distinction is the
whole reason this file has a bar. `FEATURES.md`'s rule is *"a row is ticked
only when an operator can reach it in a real build. Not when the code exists,
not when a test passes."* Twenty driven checks were **written** for this work
and **none was run**, because the operator was at his keyboard; there is no
`evidence/` directory in this tree at all. Under this file's own rule these are
not ticks yet, however green the suite is. The previous revision set the
precedent by marking the encryption row ⬜ **BUILT AND UNDRIVEN**; that
precedent is applied to all fourteen rather than to the one that happened to
remember it.

| | |
|---|---|
| **His sticky note can be read on the page** | ⬜ **BUILT AND UNDRIVEN.** A pop-up with the author, the date and the words; a hover tooltip before you click; `canvas/notepopup/`. ★★ **It works in Read, and that is a structural fact rather than a claim**: the pop-up runs its *own* hit test instead of reusing the canvas one, because the canvas one is gated on `caps.author_markup`, which is false in Read. Drawn from `app/surfaces.rs` with no capability guard, and the click consumes nothing, so nothing else it lands on changes meaning. ★ **Replies are read-only and no Reply control is drawn at all** — `pdfcer-core` v0.38.0 has no verb that authors an `/IRT`, and R9 says an unavailable capability renders nothing rather than greying |
| **The Comments panel can be used, not just read** | ⬜ **BUILT AND UNDRIVEN.** Delete (it had none), filter by author / by kind / by has-words, sort, and a **Go to** that navigates *and* opens the comment's pop-up on the same frame. ★★★ **The panel toggle is on the RAIL** (`markup.comments`, in the rail group), which is present in every mode — so Read can reopen it after closing it; the ribbon toggle lives on the Markup tab, which Read never sees, and the rail is what makes the capability real there. **Comments is mounted in Read's default dock** (`app/modes/defaults.rs`, the `"read"` arm's right column). ⚠ **One claim in the tracking notes is overstated and is corrected here: reply threads are gathered by the canvas POP-UP, not by the panel.** The panel lists every annotation flat and marks a row as a reply; the cross-page walk that assembles a thread lives in `canvas/notepopup/model.rs`. A reader who expected a threaded panel would find a flat one |
| **Sticky notes, stamps, text boxes, links and attachments copy and paste at last** | ⬜ **BUILT AND UNDRIVEN.** The clipboard is the engine's own `copy_selection`, and until 2026-09-05 every one of those five answered `Ctrl+C` with *"that annotation is not one pdfcer authors."* ★★★ **And the "lossless" route turned out to be lossy in the other direction, which is the finding worth carrying.** `pdfcer-core` carries a markup it *models* as a spec and plants it with `add_markup` rather than `add_markup_with`, so it drops `/CA`, `/T`, `/M` and `/Contents` — the author, the date, the note text and the opacity — on **exactly the kinds this shell could already copy faithfully**, and is lossless on exactly the kinds it could not. So `canvas/annotclip.rs` forks: modelled markup takes this shell's spec-plus-options route, the five new kinds take the engine's raw carrier, ce dimensions take the dimension carrier, and `/Widget`, `/Popup` and `/Redact` are refused **by name**. ★★ The fork is read off the payload, never off a subtype list |
| **Redaction stopped costing the undo stack** | ⬜ **BUILT AND UNDRIVEN.** `Pass 250.2`. Applying now **arms the next save** instead of rewriting at the click: the base, the overlay and the whole undo/redo stack survive, and there is a **Cancel** that disarms it. ★★★ **Both ordinary save routes refuse by name while it is armed** — `to_incremental_bytes` and `to_full_bytes` both return `WriteError::RedactionPending` — so the shell **routes rather than gates**: there is no `else` that could fall through, and a build that failed to fork would not leak, it would stop being able to save at all. ★★ **The collapsing route from `Pass 250.1` is DELETED, not kept beside it**, and that is pinned rather than asserted: `redact/sealed.rs` censuses the engine calls and the count for the collapsing verb went **down**. ⚠ Its price inverted: the operator agreed to losing undo, and now pays nothing — but **the page does not change when he applies**, which is the surprise that replaced the price and is disclosed in the same place |
| **pdfcer can say whether a signature's bytes were altered, and whether you trust who signed it** | ⬜ **BUILT AND UNDRIVEN.** `Pass 10.5`. Import the operator's own Acrobat trust list (Settings ▸ Digital signatures — a path, a **Browse…**, a live resolved-state line and an Inspect that reports the count and the store's date), evaluate trust against it, and a **persistent opt-in that is off by default**. Nothing is copied: pdfcer reads the operator's store in place and keeps no snapshot. ★★★ **The panel draws THREE labelled facts per signature and never folds them** — *Intact:*, *Covers:*, *Signer:* — and `NotChecked` renders as itself with which of four reasons it was, because collapsing any pair of them would assert something none of them says. Revocation is disclosed as never checked |
| **A drawing can be given a password, and told what it allows** | ⬜ **BUILT AND UNDRIVEN.** File ▸ Security, both controls Large, on the File tab, which is present in all three modes. See the fuller row under **Shell and chrome** below — it landed a day earlier and is unchanged by this measurement except that it is now one of fourteen undriven rows rather than the only one |
| **The colour of text he clicked on** | ⬜ **BUILT AND UNDRIVEN.** O89. A colour control on a **selected** text object, not merely a swept range — which was the whole of the complaint, since both previous controls greyed on a click. Multi-object recolour carries a real **indeterminate** state that applies to every member on commit, and a run set with no glyph-bearing run reads as mixed rather than as black. ⚠ **"Spot inks are refused by name" is TRUE FOR PATHS AND FALSE FOR TEXT, and the source says so first.** A path's `/Separation` carries its name, so the refusal reads *this stroke is spot ink PANTONE 300* and draws no swatch. The engine's `TextColor::Other` is a **fieldless variant and carries no name**, so the text refusal says how many runs of how many and stops. Both withhold the swatch; only one can name the ink. A document claiming it unqualified overstates |
| **Selecting an object says which layer it is on** | ⬜ **BUILT AND UNDRIVEN.** O126. In the Layers panel, as a plate on the matching row, and on the status line — and the two read the **same** name from the same function, so they cannot disagree. ★★ Three-valued on purpose: *on this layer*, *on no layer* and *could not tell* stay apart. ★ The status clause appears **only** on documents that declare optional content (0.6 % of a 500-file corpus), because *not on a layer* after every click is permanent noise; and **nothing is drawn on the page** |
| **The print preview pops out into its own window** | ⬜ **BUILT AND UNDRIVEN.** O112. A second OS viewport, 560 × 760 pt, resizable, with its own taskbar entry — and **the preview column inside the dialog collapses to nothing while it is out**: the room is *given away* to the options column, not merely zeroed, and the preview is not drawn at all rather than drawn at zero size. Both halves are unit-tested arithmetic. ★ The module's own header states that no window has ever been rendered from it |
| **Embedding pdfcer's own fonts became your choice** | ⬜ **BUILT AND UNDRIVEN.** O47. A checkbox, **off when the window opens**, drawn only when pdfcer's own faces would actually change the outcome. It had been on and unconditional since 2026-08-28. ★★★ **The reason is a licence, not a rendering preference**: the bundled faces are BSD-3-Clause and the attribution condition travels with any file he sends out, so leaving it on was making that decision for him. The consequence is stated in plain words above the box — that the letters will look different to whoever receives the file, and that pdfcer's copies come with a licence asking to be credited wherever the file goes. `pdfcer`'s own CLI spells the same decision `--use-bundled-fonts`, absent by default |
| **An object 0.85 pt across can be moved** | ⬜ **BUILT AND UNDRIVEN.** O57, his own report. The grip **anchor** box is pushed outward by `max(0, (20 pt − extent) / 2)` per side, so the objects with least body to spare stop being floored to a size at which they had none. ★★★ **Above the threshold the push is exactly zero and every grip lands byte for byte where it did**, which is what makes it safe unconditionally; painter and hit test both read the same `grip_rects`, and a `debug_assert` names the invariant rather than a comment claiming it. ★ Move still tests the *real* box, so a 0.85 pt cell does not claim 20 pt of canvas |
| **Text comes out; nothing goes back in** | ⬜ **BUILT AND UNDRIVEN.** `file.export_text`, File ▸ Export. ★★★ **And the absence was PROVED rather than asserted, on all five routes** — there is no import command in the registry, no dispatch arm, no dialog, no `.txt` filter on any of the eleven file pickers, and a dropped `.txt` is refused by the drag classifier, which knows only `.pdf` and six image extensions. The only file reads that reach a document are attachments, form data, images and font programs. ⚠ **One claim in the tracking notes does not survive: the phrase "proven absent across all eleven text-edit modules" is not supported by anything in this repository.** What the source actually records is a scan of five `pdfcer-core` areas, which is an engine-side claim this tree cannot check. The GUI-side absence is proved; the engine-side reading is a citation |
| **A drawing can be pasted into Word as lines, not as a picture** | ⬜ **BUILT AND UNDRIVEN.** `edit.copy_as_vector`, Edit ▸ Clipboard, no chord (deliberately). Four formats in one clipboard transaction, in preference order — **SVG, EMF, PNG, DIBV5** — each with its own writer and its own slot; EMF goes in as a real enhanced metafile handle rather than as bytes. ★★ **It refuses the whole copy rather than placing the raster alone**: a paste that silently became a picture is the failure the command exists to prevent. ★ Windows only — off Windows nothing is placed at all |
| **Pages come out as PNG, JPEG, SVG or EMF** | ⬜ **BUILT AND UNDRIVEN.** ⚠ **EMF is the fourth radio and this file's existing row names only three** — corrected below. PNG carries straight alpha and real physical resolution; JPEG has a worded refusal for a transparent page rather than a silent white. ★ **Under R8 these are one command, not four**: `file.export_image` is the registered capability and the formats are radios inside it, so three of the four have no command id of their own and cannot be reached, greyed or bound separately |

### ⬜ The list the operator actually needs: **shipped, and never once driven**

Fourteen capabilities, twenty new driven checks written for them, **zero runs**.
Named here as one list rather than left implied across fourteen rows, because a
reader scanning ticks will not assemble it and this is the thing that decides
whether the build is trusted:

`comment_popup` · `clipboard_annotation` · `colour_clicked_text` ·
`layers_membership` · `preview_popout` · `trust_store` · `protect` ·
`export_text` · `export_image_emf` · `copy_as_vector` · `print_clip_claim` ·
`properties_tool` · `master_detail` · `left_rail` · `menu_icons` ·
`panel_float` · `layers_search` · `ribbon_mockup` · `scale_sweep` ·
`enter_newline` — plus `redaction`, `object_clipboard` and `embed_bundled`,
which were **modified** for this work and last ran against a different build.

★★★ **This is R1 stated as a list rather than as a principle.** 3,405 tests
pass. Twenty-nine gates run and twenty-eight are green. Neither of those
sentences is a report of working software, and this project has paid for
believing otherwise often enough to write the rule down. The first job of the
next session with the machine to itself is to run these, in slices of six to
eight, with the fixtures named in `RESUME.md`.

## ★★★ Where the engine's `gui` column and this shell disagree — measured 2026-09-05

`D:\Dev\pdfcer\docs\FEATURES.md`'s `gui` column is this project's acceptance
criteria (R6: nothing there may regress at fold-in), so a disagreement is worth
finding in both directions. The engine tree is at `b01964f` with nothing
uncommitted and the lock pins that exact revision, so **for once the pin and the
engine tree agree and no engine row describes anything newer than what this
shell links.** Every row below was decided by reading this shell's source — a
registered command id, a dispatch arm, a call site — not by reading either
document.

### ✅ The reassuring half: **no `[x] gui` row was found to be a false tick**

Every backticked command id the engine names was diffed against the live
registry. Exactly one is absent — `pages.split` — and its box is already
`[ ]`, so the checkbox is right. The 63 boxes flipped on 2026-09-04 all trace
to `shipped` entries; none came from `wanted`, `blocked` or `declined`. R6 is
intact.

### ⚠ Eleven rows where the engine says `[ ] gui` and this shell HAS it

The engine's document is behind ours, and in **four** of the eleven this
project's own `ENGINE_BACKLOG.md` was behind too — which is the case worth
noticing, because it means the shell built something and then told itself it
had not.

| engine row | who is right, and how it was determined |
|---|---|
| undo-preserving deferred redaction | **We are.** All four verbs consumed in non-test source (`redact/mod.rs`, `app/save.rs`), and `edit.redact_apply` is registered and dispatched |
| verify a signature's integrity and coverage | **Neither document was right.** The engine says `[ ]`; our own backlog still filed it as *"half consumed — `verify_all` is called nowhere"*. It **is** called (`trust/mod.rs`), and the panel prints *Intact:*. Corrected in `ENGINE_BACKLOG.md` this pass |
| encrypt / set permissions / remove encryption | **We are.** `file.encrypt` and `file.permissions` registered, dispatched, on File ▸ Security; all three verbs called from `protect/mod.rs` |
| import an Acrobat trust store | **We are, and the engine's row ends with the words "No GUI half."** `trust/mod.rs` calls `trust_store::load_from_path`; the control is in Settings ▸ Digital signatures |
| evaluate trust against those anchors | **We are.** `verify_all_with_trust`, reported per signature |
| the persistent "use Acrobat trust store" setting | **We are.** Settings ▸ Digital signatures, off by default |
| deterministic RFC 5280 path validation | **We are**, with the caveat our own row already states honestly |
| export to PNG / JPEG | **Neither document was right** — ours still filed it `wanted`. `file.export_image` is registered, dispatched and on the ribbon |
| export to SVG | **Neither** — same window, same row of radios |
| export to EMF | **Neither** — and ours said *"stays `wanted` because it has not been driven"*, which is a different bar from the one that file uses everywhere else |
| copy page content to the clipboard as vector | **Neither, and this was the most stale row in either document.** Ours said *"NOT BUILT, AND DELIBERATELY SO"* and named two blockers — a workspace crate that would have to be created, and `forbid(unsafe_code)`. **Both are gone**: `crates/native-clipboard` exists, is a workspace member, and is consumed behind the registered `edit.copy_as_vector` |

### ⚠ Two engine rows whose CHECKBOX is right and whose PROSE is not

Reported to the engine rather than corrected here — `D:\Dev\pdfcer` is
read-only from this tree.

- The split row says `pages.split` *"is drawn on the Pages tab and falls
  through to `command-unimplemented`."* It is not drawn anywhere and there is
  no such fall-through: it sits in the PLANNED register and R9 makes an unbuilt
  capability render **nothing**. The verdict is right; the mechanism it
  describes does not exist.
- The maintenance note calls Flatten the worked example of a scaffolded, inert
  ribbon command. `edit.form_flatten` is registered, has a live dispatch arm
  and is on the ribbon; the row it is the example *for* is already correct. The
  header's parenthesis rotted while the row it points at stayed right.

⇒ ★★★ **The pattern across all fifteen findings is one thing: a capability
list is written by the session that built the thing, and the sentence about
what is *missing* is the half nobody re-reads.** Ticks got flipped; the prose
beside them, in both projects, did not.

## ★★★ Fourteen sentences in this build were false, and seven of them were on screen — 2026-09-05

Re-measuring a capability list means grepping every claim of ABSENCE before
believing it, because *an absence claim is a claim about every route* and this
project has now paid for that seven times. The sweep below is what that
produced. It is recorded in `FEATURES.md` rather than only in a commit message
because these sentences are **what the program tells the operator it cannot
do**, which is part of what is built.

### Corrected in place, dated, in `crates/pdfcer-gui/src/text/**`

Seven were **operator-visible** — a tooltip, a dialog footer, a panel row — and
every one of them denied a capability that had shipped:

- the Signatures panel's own **ribbon tooltip** still said *"pdfcer does not
  check whether they are valid"*, read **before** the panel opens;
- the save-invalidation dialog's footnote said pdfcer *"does not check any
  signature's certificate or its cryptography"* — the sentence its own doc
  comment calls the most important in the file;
- a **signature form-field row** said pdfcer *"does not create or verify
  signatures yet"*, where half the sentence stayed true and kept the false half
  looking true;
- the post-save status note claimed pdfcer *"has not checked any signature's
  cryptography"*;
- **both** redaction command tooltips still said *"applying is not
  [reversible]"* and *"This cannot be undone"*, which stopped being true of the
  default destination when applying began staging the next save;
- the apply dialog's **footer** said this is *"the one action in pdfcer that
  cannot be undone"* while the body of the same window, two hundred lines
  above, correctly said otherwise — the footer does not branch on destination
  and the body does.

Seven more were **dead prose** — false, with zero call sites, in doc comments
and in a catalog the new work quietly orphaned. Dead prose cannot mislead an
operator, but it misleads the next reader auditing what the build claims, which
is precisely this file.

★★★ **The pattern, and it is the only part worth carrying:** every
operator-visible false sentence sat in a **command catalog or a dialog footer**
— a file *away* from the module the feature work touched. Where the work opened
a catalog, the catalog got corrected the same day, carefully, with a dated note.
Where it did not, the sentence stood. ⇒ **A feature lands in the module you are
editing; its claims live in files you are not.** After wiring a verb, grep for
the sentence that used to deny it before closing the task.

⚠ `check-stale-blockers.sh` cannot catch any of these, and says so in its own
header: it catches a row that declares itself blocked on a request the channel
shows as answered, and explicitly disclaims *"a stale sentence in a module
header, a stale operator-facing string, or an absence test that outlived its
absence."* Those three remain a manual procedure. This sweep is that procedure
being run.

### ⚠ Four false claims found OUTSIDE `text/**` — reported, not fixed

Those modules belong to other tracks and one of them is being written as this
is measured. Each is false today, each was true when written:

- `app/actions/annots.rs` — *"This shell does not draw pop-ups at all."* It
  does, since the sticky-note pop-up landed, and a whole Rule-4 argument is
  built on top of the sentence.
- `panels/properties/mod.rs` — a *"what is deliberately not built"* header
  saying editable X/Y/W/H is absent because *"there is nothing to edit."* The
  section exists two files over, with an Apply that moves the selection.
- `dialogs/redact.rs` — a doc comment stating *"`Destination::NewFile` is still
  the default"*, contradicted by the constant 684 lines earlier in the same
  file. Two answers in one file.
- `panels/mod.rs` — the header opens *"Ten panels"* against a catalog of
  twelve, and states that the Comments panel deliberately cannot delete an
  annotation, which Delete has made false.

## ★★ What landed on 2026-08-20 — **several documents at once**, and pages
## dragged between them

The operator's request, verbatim:

> *"make it so we can open multiple PDFs at once and drag and drop pages from
> one thumbnail image sidebar to another or onto the canvas to add pages and
> insert them in between the pages we've dragged to on the canvas or the
> thumbnail preview area."*

| | |
|---|---|
| **Several documents open** | ✅ a **document tab strip** under the ribbon — labels, an unsaved `*` marker, a ✕, middle-click to close, `Ctrl+Tab` / `Ctrl+Shift+Tab` to cycle, `Ctrl+W` to close, an overflow menu past the width. Opening a file that is already open activates its tab instead of opening it twice |
| **Drag a page between documents** | ✅ press a thumbnail, rest on the other document's **tab** until it springs open, drop at a caret between two sheets. The drag survives the document switch because it lives in `egui::Memory` rather than on the panel that started it |
| **Drop onto the page view** | ✅ the same gesture, released on the canvas, with a horizontal caret in the gap between two sheets — every display mode, including single page |
| **It is a COPY, and it says so** | ✅ *"Copy 2 sheets from SW41177.pdf to before page 3."* on the status row before the button is released. A cross-document move would be two commands on two undo stacks with no single `Ctrl+Z` able to reverse it |
| **★ Open and New stopped destroying work** | ✅ and by **removing** a guard rather than adding one. They no longer replace the open document, so the unsaved-edits prompt in front of them would have been stating something false — and a confirmation that says something untrue is how an operator learns to dismiss confirmations unread |
| **Live window title** | ✅ `SW41177.pdf — 3 documents open — pdfcer`, so Alt-Tab and the taskbar say what you left open |

### And what the operator asked for on the second pass, same day

| | |
|---|---|
| **Shift makes it a move** | ✅ Windows' own drag modifier — Ctrl copies, Shift moves — sampled at the **release** as Explorer does, so the caption follows the key under your hand. The source's pages go only if the target's insert actually happened, and if the removal is the half that fails it says so, with the remedy |
| **Tabs rearrange by dragging** | ✅ a caret marks the boundary, resolved by neighbours' centres. **The document on screen does not change** — the active document follows its own tab through the permutation, swept across all 80 combinations |
| **Right-click on a tab** | ✅ Close, and Close others. Close is `file.close` itself with the tab as its operand, not a second command — two gates refused the second command and were right |
| **Drag a tab off into a new window** | ⛔ **not built** — it is a scope question with two different conventional answers. See below |

### ✅ Driven **on 2026-08-20** — 50 verified, 0 failed, 6 skipped

(The suite has grown since; the current figures are in the table at the top,
and the run they come from is `evidence/ui-verify-run-2026-08-27-SUMMARY.md`.)

All four multi-document checks pass against the real binary:

```text
two_documents_get_two_tabs                       PASS
document_tabs_can_be_rearranged                  PASS   document-reorder from=0 gap=2 to=1 active=0
a_page_dragged_between_documents_is_copied       PASS   copied=1 take=0 · 36 → 37 · source untouched
a_shift_drag_between_documents_moves_the_pages   PASS   copied=1 take=1 · 36 → 37 · page-move-took removed=1
```

The run also found two things a green `cargo test` could not, both recorded in
`D:/dev/rag/egui/`:

- **the drag caption moved the grid under the pointer** — a wrapping label above
  a drop target, whose height depends on its own wording, moved the aimed-at row
  by 49 pt and then 34 pt mid-gesture. Moved to the status bar, which has a fixed
  height by construction;
- **`Response::hovered()` is false on every widget but the dragged one**, so the
  spring-loaded tab could never fire. Resolved geometrically now.

### ⛔ Dragging a tab off the strip into a new window — the one open question

Not built, and not because it is hard to *start*. The two applications an
operator would compare it to **disagree about what it means**:

| | what dragging a tab off does |
|---|---|
| **Chrome, Firefox, VS Code** | the document **moves** to a new window, fully functional |
| **Acrobat `Window ▸ New Window`**, Bluebeam's split | a **second view** opens; the document stays where it was |

`CONTINUE.md` §1b makes the convergence of the product class the specification.
Here there is no convergence, so it is a scope decision — and the two cost very
different things:

- **a second view** (canvas + page list + status bar, document stays in the
  strip) is roughly a day, and it delivers the *original* request from another
  direction: two page lists visible at once to drag between;
- **a full move** needs every window to own its ribbon state, dock arrangement,
  mode and panel state — a `Workspace` extraction — because a document that has
  left the strip must still be editable, and today all of that is
  application-scoped.

Building the wrong one is worse than building neither: a window the document
cannot be edited in is a trap, and a second view that pretends to be a move is
a lie about where the operator's work is.

## ★ What landed on 2026-08-19, and why this revision exists

Four of the operator's six standing complaints closed in one day, plus two
defects nobody had reported because neither had a symptom anybody could see.

| | |
|---|---|
| **The I-beam** | ✅ two-tone, legible on white — his #1 |
| **Measure hover** | ✅ entity **and** node, before the first click — his #2 |
| **Dimension groups** | ✅ a **dock panel** with six foldable sections, replacing a window whose content could push its own title bar off the desktop — his #3 |
| **Tool status** | ✅ what the pointer does and what is armed — his #4, **and his #5**. ★ Shipped 2026-08-19 as a **panel**; became a **one-line strip in the right dock's chrome** on 2026-09-04 at his own instruction (`OPERATOR_REQUESTS.md` O123). Its live controls are in Properties; its tool LIST is gone, which is the one thing that changed rather than moved |
| **Revision cloud** | ✅ `markup.cloud`, its own glyph, `/BE /I 1.0` — his #6, blocked on a blocker that had already been removed |
| **⚠ Unsaved edits** | ✅ **Close, Open and New destroyed every edit since the file was opened, silently, while `file.close`'s tooltip promised otherwise.** Found by audit, not by use |
| **⚠ Settings coverage** | ✅ a gate asserting every setting the engine carries has a control — it found `quad_point_order`, which had none |

★ His #5 was *"no text editing or adding text on the canvas"*, and it was
**false in the literal sense**: `edit.text` and `edit.add_text` were registered,
drawn, chord-bound and driven-verified. He could not find them. That is a
discoverability defect, and it was marked green for three weeks because *"the
tests pass"* is not a report of working software. The Tool panel was the fix.

⚠ **And on 2026-09-04 he took the list back** — O123: *"its buttons duplicate
the ribbon and go."* What survives of the fix is the strip, which names what is
armed at frame one, cannot be closed, and is drawn in every mode. What does not
survive is the row that named a tool the operator had **not** armed, which was
the half that answered *"I could not find it."* Recorded as a real subtraction
in `crates/pdfcer-gui/src/app/toolstatus.rs`, not as a wash.

★★ **Two recorded blockers turned out to be false on the same day**, both
naming `pdfcer-core`, both disproved by one `grep`. The revision cloud's cost the
operator three weeks of asking for a tool whose only blocker had already
shipped. A blocker that names a repository this project does not build **cannot
fail a test** — nothing compiles differently, nothing lints, and CI stays green
*precisely because the feature is still absent*. Every external blocker here is
now written as a dated citation rather than as a verdict. See `NO_SURFACE.md`
§1c and `D:\dev\rag\rust\`.

## Complete

### Shell and chrome

- ✅ **★★ A dialog is a real OS window — Print, 2026-08-20.** The operator's report: *"Print dialogue box doesn't pop up in its own movable window. It is locked within the boundaries of the program's window. Like, I just assume you've been trained on a million lines of code and software that pops it up in its own window."* The last sentence is the diagnosis, and the convention corpus says why immediate-mode toolkits get it wrong: the in-viewport window widget is one function call and the real thing needs a newer, more awkward viewport API — the default is wrong and nothing pushes back. Every retained toolkit makes the OS window the **default** and the inline panel the special case, and `dialogs::host` restores that ordering here, so **the other thirteen dialogs are one line each** rather than thirteen implementations. Title bar, taskbar entry, draggable outside the application and onto a second monitor; it degrades to the old in-viewport window on a backend with no multi-viewport support, which is the web target, and egui owns that fallback. **Enter presses Print** and Print is drawn in the theme's selection fill so you can see what Enter will do; Escape is Cancel and is the X. It comes back where you left it for as long as it is open — not across a restart, because a remembered position must be validated against the *current* monitors and a dialog that opens on an unplugged screen is worse than one that opens centred. ~~⛔ **It is not OWNED by the main window and cannot be**~~ — **CLOSED 2026-08-21, see the row below.** Always-on-top was considered and refused — it breaks the driven harness in a way that produces confident wrong bug reports. ★ **Verified without taking the operator's cursor**, through a new headless seam that presses one ribbon command in an invisible window: an offscreen window is genuinely laid out and OS input cannot reach it, so before this a headless run could read the trace and press nothing
- ✅ **★★★ AND SO ARE THE OTHER THIRTEEN — 2026-08-21.** About, Render diagnostics, Export to DXF, Insert image, Insert pages, New document, Recognise text, Apply redactions, Set scale, Keyboard shortcuts, Settings, the note editor and the unsaved-changes question. The operator's report was never about printing; it was about a shell that draws its dialogs inside its own canvas. Three are worth naming because the window is the **feature**: **Apply redactions** lists what will be removed and could not be checked against the page it was covering; **Render diagnostics** is read *while* zooming the document it describes; and the **unsaved-changes** question appears in answer to a close, when the operator has already looked away — a modal question hidden behind the main window with no taskbar entry is the classic *"the program has frozen"* report. ★★ **The host stopped owning state, and that is what made thirteen conversions one line each.** Its first version kept the remembered position in a struct field, so every dialog needed a field, a constructor and a `take()`/put-back dance around the borrow — `Host::show` wanted `&mut` on the host while the closure it was handed wanted `&mut` on the dialog — and `ShortcutsDialog`, a deliberately stateless **unit struct**, could not be converted at all without being given state it does not want. The position now lives in `egui::Memory` keyed on the dialog's id string and `show` takes `&self`, so a `Host` is a *description* built fresh each frame. It fixed G6 properly on the way: the position now **survives close-and-reopen**, which the struct version could not and called correct because it had no way to be otherwise. ★★★ **Nine of the thirteen had no size to convert** — they were `resizable(false)` with no `default_size`, so egui sized them to their content every frame and no number for how big they are existed anywhere. An OS window must be created at *some* size, so a naive conversion means guessing nine numbers, and a guess that is too small does not look wrong: it clips the bottom of the dialog, which on a confirmation is the row with the buttons on it — the exact class the egui RAG records as *"panels that shipped unreachable in real builds with every gate green"*. So a dialog measures its own body and the window grows to fit; the declared size stopped being a promise and became an opening bid. ★ **And that mechanism's first version was the R128 feedback loop, caught in one driven launch.** It padded the measured content by an item spacing before comparing, so the wanted size was always larger than the window, every frame asked for eight more pixels than the last, and the once-per-size guard did not help *because every size was a new one*. About opened at 560 × 480 and was **1,624 × 746** by the time the trace was read. **Monotonic creep is a loop; a guard that only stops repetition does not stop it.** Content is now measured raw with a floor on how much overflow is worth acting on. Driven by `dialogs_open_in_their_own_window` — one process per dialog through the headless invoke seam, so it needs no pointer and is safe to run on a machine somebody is using — eight of thirteen, each at its declared size, none needing to grow. ⬜ **NOT VERIFIED and named rather than implied:** the five reachable only by a gesture — Insert image, Insert pages, Set scale, the note editor and the unsaved-changes question. Converted; undriven
- ✅ ★★★ **SIGN A DOCUMENT — 2026-09-06, DRIVEN, and the signature is read back out of the FILE.** The operator reported on 2026-09-03 that *a document cannot be signed*. `pdfcer-core` answered on 2026-09-05 with `pdfcer_core::sign` — 101 public items whose own module header says it is *"the family the `pdfcer-gui` request of 2026-09-03 asked for"* — and this build **did not contain any of it for three days**. ★★★ **The cause was one line of a Cargo manifest, and it is the JPX incident repeating.** `crates/pdfcer-gui/Cargo.toml` took `pdfcer-core` with `default-features = false` and forwarded `jpx` and `ocrs`; the engine's `signing` feature is default-on, so it was stripped. Nothing failed to compile, no test went red, and the comment warning about the identical omission three weeks earlier — *"forgetting to forward does not fail to compile"* — was forty lines above the block that repeated it. ⇒ **A warning does not protect a code path written after it**, and `tools/gates/check-forwarded-features.sh` now reads the ENGINE's own default feature list and fails when a capability is missing from this build. Falsified both ways. **What he does:** open the drawing, File ▸ Security ▸ **Sign…**, choose a `.pfx`/`.p12`, type its passphrase, press *Open certificate*, then Reason, Location, whether to draw a box, where the signed file goes, and *Sign and save…*. ★★★ **Nothing below the identity is drawn until the certificate has actually opened** — not until one is chosen, not until a passphrase is typed. Signing is an act of identity and the only guard a program can offer is the operator reading whose certificate came out of the file; a form that let them fill in a reason and press *Sign* with the container still shut would put the identity check after the decision, where it is a formality. ★★ **Invisible by default**, because the engine's visible appearance is *"a thin frame only — no text"* in this first cut and an empty rectangle stamped on one of his CAD sheets is indistinguishable from a defect. The option is offered with the box's contents and its exact measurements stated **before** it is chosen. **No Name field**: the engine falls back to the certificate subject, and a free-text name beside a certificate is a second unverifiable claim about who signed. ★★ **Both engine refusals are shown rather than discovered** — an encrypted document, and one carrying an armed redaction, each get a sentence naming the remedy **instead of a form**. Both are reachable in one session, because this shell ships Encrypt… and deferred redaction. ★★★ **The passphrase is held to a stricter rule than the document passwords beside it**: `Secret` from the moment it leaves the field, and — unlike the encryption surface, which traces `user_chars=` — **no trace line carries its length**, nor the certificate's path. ★★★ **THE VERDICT IS TAKEN IN ANOTHER PROCESS.** `a_document_can_be_signed_and_the_signature_is_in_the_file` signs a document, then opens the result in a **fresh binary** and reads the Signatures panel — the verification side that shipped as `Pass 10.5`: `signature-row field="Signature1" integrity=verified`. Falsified by flipping one byte of the output: phase A's trace stayed **identical** (`self_verified=1`) and phase D read `digest-mismatch`. That is the *"traces perfectly and does nothing"* class, caught only because the oracle is a different subsystem. ⚠ Still the engine's: certifying (`/DocMDP`) signatures, signing into a pre-placed field, an appearance stream with words in it
- ⬜ ★★★ **Put a password on a drawing, and choose what it says it allows — 2026-09-04, `OPERATOR_REQUESTS.md` O119, BUILT AND UNDRIVEN.** File ▸ Security, immediately after Export, which is where the approved mockup draws it. **`Encrypt…`** does all three jobs — set a password, change the passwords keeping exactly what the document allowed before, or remove the protection entirely — because they are one subject and three ribbon controls would be three chances to press the wrong one. **`Permissions…`** offers what `pdfcer-core` actually models, read out of its source: **eight bits**, Table 22 order, each rendered from the document's own three-valued answer. ★★★ **Seven are tick-boxes and one is not** — the engine sets the accessibility bit on every file it writes (rule W19, for older readers), so a box you could clear would come back ticked in the result; that row is a sentence saying so instead. It was found by a test that FAILED, not by reading the docs. ★★ **The window reports before it offers**: the cipher, which password opened the file and all eight bits appear under *"This document, as it is now"* above anything editable, because a permissions dialog that opens with everything ticked on a document that forbids printing has lied before it is touched. ★★★ **O119's three disclosures are all on screen and none waits for a press**: the engine's own *"a request, not a lock"* sentence verbatim above the tick-boxes; a **signed document draws no form at all**, naming the count and explaining that protecting rewrites every byte the signature covers (R9's explained branch, not a button that fails on press); and the owner-password requirement written above the box it is about, including that it must be typed again because pdfcer does not keep passwords. ★ **Two password boxes, not one, and the window says why** — and refuses to let them be the same, since the owner password ignores every permission. ★★ **Saving is `Apply redactions`' mechanism part for part** — new file by default, a suggestion that is never the source, replace gated behind one extra acknowledgement and no picker, atomic temp-then-rename — deliberately, rather than a second answer to the question the operator settled that morning. ⬜ **NOT DRIVEN and named rather than implied:** 20 headless tests pass and every one was falsified; `ui-verify`'s `protect_shows_the_document_and_refuses_a_signed_one` is written, registered and **was not run** — the desktop was held by a concurrent track
- ✅ **Ribbon, seven tabs** — File · View · Pages · Edit · Markup · Measure · Tools, plus the contextual **Format** tab that appears on selection
- ✅ **The ribbon is data, not code** — a serializable manifest, which is what makes it customizable *and* reusable by another application
- ✅ **Mode selector** — Read / Review / Edit, right-aligned on the tab row, driving both the tab set and the panel layout
- ✅ **Quick access toolbar** — Open, Save a copy, Undo, Redo, drawn **icon-only** now that a painter exists
- ✅ **Open, Recent, Close** — a second document can be opened. `Ctrl+O` was in the keymap and printed in the tooltip since the ribbon landed and **did nothing**: `DERIVED` held only digits, so the chord could not be spelled
- ✅ **Keyboard chords derive from the manifest keymap** — `keyboard.rs` no longer knows what `Ctrl+0` *means*; it spells the key, looks it up, and returns a command id through the same dispatcher a ribbon click reaches. Rebind it in a customization layer and the keyboard follows
- ✅ **`Ctrl+1/2/3` switch mode**, `Ctrl+0` is actual size — one owner per chord, with a test that fails naming any chord claimed twice
- ✅ **Every ribbon control that can be ON renders pressed** — the last two were the hand tool and marquee zoom, whose armed flag lives in `egui::Memory` rather than on the document, so `conditions()` now takes the `Context`. The obvious alternative, a shadow copy on `PdfcerApp`, was refused: it fails as a ribbon claiming Hand while the canvas selects, and each half would be self-consistent so no test would catch it. Published outside the `Status::Open` arm on purpose — with no document the control is greyed **and** pressed, which is exactly "this is the tool you are in, and there is nothing to use it on"
- ✅ **Group captions, enforced by construction** — one closure draws every group, so a caption cannot be omitted
- ✅ **Band overflow** — reserved-space "⏷ N more", proven hit-testable at a width narrow enough to hide groups
- ✅ **Tab-strip overflow** — active tab pinned; collapses to the affordance below ~47 pt rather than hiding the tab you are looking at
- ✅ **Status bar** — render-notes disclosure (closed by default), Actual size / Fit width / **Fit height** / Fit page, zoom −/%/+, page ⏴ n/N ⏵, and the **Flip pages** wheel toggle beside them (single-page displays only)
- ✅ **Find** — `Ctrl+F` and a status-bar toggle open a floating box at the page's top right: search, `3 of 47`, Enter / Shift+Enter to step, hits highlighted with the current one distinguished, and Match case · Whole word · Wildcards behind an Options menu. Literal by default — the old shell's bar ran through `find_text`, which patterns, so typing `?` matched every character on the page. An edit clears the highlights and says so rather than drawing a mark that may no longer be over the text it names. Measured at **350 ms** on the 5.6 MB benchmark drawing, which is why nothing searches on a keystroke
- ✅ **Editable page box** — type `37`, press Enter. Commits on Enter or focus loss, clamps out-of-range and says so, rejects non-numeric without discarding what you typed
- ✅ **Three themes** with a *rendered-pair* contrast gate over all five widget states — **and, as of 2026-08-14, actually installed.** This row was ticked from the day the theme module landed and was false for all of it: `Theme::apply` was never called, so every colour an operator ever saw was `egui`'s stock style. Found by sampling pixels, not by a test — see `DEFECTS.md` D10. Enabling it immediately exposed a second defect the stock style had been hiding: the active ribbon tab borrowed `egui`'s *selection* visuals, which this theme points at the translucent **canvas** object-selection tint, so the tab drew pale text on a 27 % wash. It now paints `accent` + `on_accent`, as the mode selector beside it always did. ~~**The preset is not yet choosable** — that is the settings dialog, still unsalvaged~~ **★ Corrected 2026-08-19: it became choosable on 2026-08-17 when the Settings dialog was salvaged, and this sentence was never updated.** `Settings ▸ Appearance`, three radios, live-previewed from the draft — you cannot judge a theme from its name. `dialogs/settings/appearance.rs`. The stale line was found by the `pdfcer` session reading this file for the `gui`-column re-base and noticing it contradicted a later entry in the same file; the lesson is the same one `NO_SURFACE.md` §1c draws — **a claim with no falsifier decays**, and a 113 KB document nobody re-reads end to end is exactly such a place

- ✅ **⚠ Closing a document asks about unsaved edits — 2026-08-19, and until that day it did not.** `file.close`'s shipped tooltip has read *"You are asked what to do about unsaved edits first"* since the day it shipped, and nothing asked: `Action::Close` consulted `save_pending`, which is permanently `false` **by design** because the one save this build has is synchronous, and then dropped the `EditSession`. Every edit since the file was opened went with it, with no prompt and no undo. **Open and New did the same, and Open is the worse one** — an operator who marks up a drawing and then opens the next has destroyed exactly as much work, and is far more likely to do it. Why it survived is the useful half: **the guard that should have caught it existed, was well argued, was correct, and was answering a different question.** ★ The first button says **"Save a copy…"**, not *Save*, and it cannot say Save — `file.save` is `PLANNED`, blocked on autosave and crash recovery, so the only writer produces a new file and leaves the open one untouched. A *Save* label would be the same lie as the tooltip: the operator would name a file, press Close, and believe their work was in a document that still holds its original contents. The count is real — *"48 edits have been made since it was opened"* is a decision somebody can make where *"you have unsaved changes"* is not — and cancelling the file picker cancels the whole transaction, because pressing Cancel in a file dialog must never be a route to destroying a document
- ✅ **★ Every setting the engine carries has a control, and a gate says so.** `pdfcer-core` gains settings on its own schedule and this project finds out by reading a note, so the old shell carried a test asserting the coverage — and it was about to be deleted with that crate. It could not be copied: it read the store's *source* through a relative path that only exists when both crates share a repository. The replacement enumerates from **`Settings::write_to_string`, at runtime, against the compiled dependency** — which is strictly better: it describes the engine actually linked, cannot be fooled by a `pub` field that is not persisted or by one persisted under a different key, and needs no path into another repository, so it survives the fold-in in either direction. It found `quad_point_order` immediately. ★ That setting's silence line is **the only one in the window that does not describe a silence**: §12.5.6.10 states a `/QuadPoints` corner order and essentially no producer follows it, so the sentence says pdfcer is choosing between the wording and the tools rather than filling a hole — *"the standard is silent"* would have been the comfortable sentence and a false one

### Panels and dock

- ✅ **Dock** — multiple columns per side, vertical stacks, tabbed groups, draggable splitters
- ✅ **Tab overflow** — reserved-space menu; the old two-panes-per-group cap is retired (nine panels in one stack, tested)
- ✅ **Layout persistence** — `<settings dir>/layout.ron`, debounced 750 ms with a 5 s ceiling, per-item fail-soft loading
- ✅ **Named workspaces**, and **a mode is a workspace** — leaving Edit and returning restores your arrangement, not a default
- ✅ **Scoped reset** — right dock alone, left alone, or all
- ✅ **★★★ Bookmarks — navigation that lands on the DETAIL, not merely the page — 2026-09-01.** `Destination::Page` carries both a `page_index` and a `view`, and the panel used to match `{ page_index, .. }`: the `..` was where the zoom went. On a drawing package, where every bookmark names a detail of a shared sheet with `/XYZ` or `/FitR`, that reduced the whole outline to a page list — several bookmarks arriving in the same place, which is indistinguishable from all of them being broken. All five views are honoured. ★★ **Table 151's null-vs-zero rule is asymmetric and is implemented that way**: a `zoom` of `0` means *retain the current magnification*, and the standard states that equivalence for `zoom` and for nothing else — a `left` of `0.0` is a real left edge. Collapsing them is how a destination at a page's top-left corner silently becomes "no change". ★ A `/FitR` frames through the same code the zoom marquee uses, so a bookmark and a rubber band over one region arrive **identically**, not merely close. ★ **Nothing is clamped into view**: a bookmark pointing off the sheet is a document defect the engine's own census counts, and landing somewhere plausible would hide it. **DRIVEN** on the operator's own A1 sheet — clicking "Drawing View64" takes the fitted 0.382× to 0.766×
- ✅ **★★★ A clickable table of contents WORKS — 2026-09-01 (evening), and the defect it was built to avoid is not the obvious one.** Clicking a `/Link` in Read or Review navigates — page, zoom and scroll, through the same `destination::actions_for` pipeline the bookmark row above uses, so Table 151 has exactly one implementation. In **Edit** a click **selects** the link instead, because there it is an annotation you may want to move, resize or delete; same predicate and same reasoning as the form fill-versus-author split. ★★★ **The failure most likely to ship was never "links do nothing"** — that is loud and gets reported in a minute. It is a viewer that treats all **five** `Destination` variants as navigable, resolves the four it cannot perform to a defaulted page 0, and navigates anyway: the cursor changes, the click lands, a page appears, and the operator concludes their document's links are wrong. So `follow` has five arms and no collapsing catch-all, and each non-navigating one raises **its own sentence**, off-canvas, on the click — a deleted target page, a name table lost when the file was made, another file named with its page, and an action pdfcer **recognises and never executes**. Four causes with four remedies rather than one generic *"broken link"*, which would send somebody hunting for a defect in a document that has none. ★ **The affordance is a cursor and nothing is drawn on the page**: a pointing hand over a followable link, **nothing at all** over one that is not (R9). No border, no tint, no rectangle over the `/Rect` — a screenshot of the canvas is identical to a screenshot of the same file saved and reopened. ★★ **The falsification FAILED first and had to be repaired.** The non-navigation check originally asserted only that the page had not changed; the plausible wrong implementation was planted and it **passed**, because the fixture opens on page 0 and a defaulted jump to page 0 moves nothing. It now zooms in first and asserts page, zoom **and** offset — planted again it fails, restored it passes. A check that cannot fail is not evidence. ★ Cost: the O(document) `DestinationReader` is cached on the edit epoch and the per-page link list on `(page, epoch)`, because the cursor asks *"is the pointer over a link"* on every frame and without that it would walk a 36-sheet drawing's page tree on every mouse move. Engine `Pass 222.0`, requested and answered the same day
- ✅ **★ And a bookmark can be WRITTEN — 2026-08-19, the day `EditSession::add_outline_item` shipped.** pdfcer had read bookmarks since the reader passes and carried **zero authoring verbs opposite them**; this was the first ask of the `insert_pages` request. Type a title, and it points at the page on screen, filed under whichever bookmark you last clicked — the click navigates *and* records the parent, because both are true of the row you pointed at. ★ **Nothing counts anything.** `/Count` is two different quantities and its **sign carries the open/closed flag** (§12.3.3 defines no `/Open` key), so a bookmark added under a **collapsed** ancestor leaves the document's visible total unchanged — a surface reporting *"added N"* by diffing it reports **zero for a correct save**. One call adds one bookmark and that is what is said. ★ And the collapsed case is **disclosed before the press**: the operator's real problem is not the count, it is adding a bookmark, looking at the panel and not seeing it — while the panel is correct. The parent is an `ObjId`, never a position, because a position is invalidated by the very edit this performs — which is the hazard the engine hit **in its own CLI**
- ✅ **★★ …and a bookmark can be RENAMED and REMOVED — 2026-08-28** (`Pass 156.0`). Click a row and a Selected bookmark block appears above the list with its name and a Remove. Renaming is the commonest bookmark edit there is and the panel could only ever create. ★★ **Delete takes the subtree, as Acrobat does** — promoting orphaned children would silently reorganise a document's navigation, so deleting one chapter heading would splice its ten sections into the top level — and the count is disclosed **before** the press (*"this also removes the 11 filed under it"*) as well as after it. ★ The two counts are computed differently on purpose: the panel's is exclusive and the engine's is inclusive, and they are allowed to **disagree**, because `read_outline` truncates on cycles and depth — so a damaged outline becomes visible instead of quietly mis-promised. ★ **No confirmation dialog**: the question a modal would ask carries no information (one press is one undoable command), and the one the operator needs is on the panel, where a modal cannot put it. ★ **Reorder and re-parent do not ship, so nothing renders for them** — no drag handle, no greyed "Move up"
- ✅ **★ Tool — 2026-08-19, and it closes two of the operator's complaints at once.** *"No side bar area showing what tool is active and its options"*, and *"no text editing or adding text on the canvas"* — the second of which was **false in the literal sense**, because `edit.text` and `edit.add_text` were registered, drawn on Edit ▸ Content, bound to `Ctrl+E`/`Ctrl+Shift+E` and driven-verified. He could not find them. ★ **Captioned Tool, not Tool options**, and the two text tools are the proof: `CanvasTool::TextEdit`'s whole state is a draft whose anchor comes from the click, with no font, size or alignment — §5.8 gives all three to the Format tab — so **the two tools whose absence started this have exactly zero options**, and a panel shaped around options would render nothing for them. Three blocks, every line live: **what a press means in this mode** (the identical drag marquees objects in Edit and sweeps text in Read, and no surface had ever said so); **one row per tool family**, capped at seven, each naming its ribbon tab and its chord so the panel teaches the ribbon rather than replacing it; and **what pdfcer last worked out**, which is where the text tools' refusal sentences finally fit — they are 47 words aimed at a status row R128 forbids growing, and *"that click was declined and you could not read why"* is very likely the actual defect behind the complaint. Its own dock stack, at the top, in Review and Edit: it is the only panel in any arrangement that gets one, because a tab invisible until clicked cannot fix a discoverability defect — it has the same shape as the defect. Every row is `Action::Command(id)`, a route rather than a second implementation; the identity row reads the **registry's** label and the operator's own keymap, because two copies of one label drift invisibly
- ✅ **★ Dimension groups — a panel as of 2026-08-19, a window before that, and the move is the operator's.** *"The groups editor popup is too long for some screens so can't close it … should come up in the side bar and be scrollable and each section should be able to fold up like the settings one."* A window taller than the screen can push its own title bar — and its only ✕ — off the desktop: not a layout complaint but a **trap**. It also removes the growth loop rather than tuning it (the body was a `ScrollArea`, the window sized itself to its content, and the two chased each other until the Add button was laid out 300 pt below the bottom of its own window — R128 in a second place). Six foldable sections of which **one** starts open, and the rule behind that is *what does an operator need to READ without asking*, not *what do they most often change* — the second question is the trap that made Appearance, the longest section, open by default. Adding a group moved to directly under the list: the window had hoisted it into a reserved footer to make it reachable, which fixed the reach and left the claim wrong, because **adding is an action on the list, not on the selected group**. One command, `measure.manage_groups`, with no second `view.panel_*` id — so the mode taxonomy does the gating for free
- ✅ **★★★ Attachments — 2026-08-28, and the capability had NO surface at all.** `attach_file`, `detach_file`, `list_attachments` and `extract_attachment` had all existed in `pdfcer-core` with no command, no menu item and no panel — which is a capability that does not exist as far as the operator is concerned. Edit ▸ Insert ▸ Attachments lists what the document carries, both document-level embedded files and page-level file annotations, with **Attach file…** above the list and **Save a copy…** and **Remove** on each row. ★★ **The saved name is sanitised and the rename is disclosed** — the listing shows the raw name and the disk gets a different one, so `..\..\Windows\System32\evil.exe` becomes `evil.exe` and the operator is told *"it tried to climb out of the folder you chose"*; a surface that quietly wrote a different file from the one it named would be the worse of the two failures. ★★ **Removing does not erase**, and the sentence saying so is mandatory copy: the bytes stay recoverable from an earlier revision inside the file until a compacted copy is saved. ★ **Editing a description renders nothing** — the engine takes one at attach time and has no verb that changes it — and the *limit* is disclosed on the attach row instead, which is R9 rather than a greyed box. ★ `RIBBON_IA.md` names attachments **nowhere**; Edit ▸ Insert is argued from Redact's precedent (Read is shown File and View alone, and a surface that embeds and removes whole files does not belong in front of a reading stance) and the cost is stated rather than hidden: Acrobat *Reader* lists attachments and this build's Read mode will not
- ✅ **Layers** — full `/RBGroups` radio semantics, locked-member handling, Reset to the document's own default
- ⬜ **★★★ Signatures — three facts, reported separately, and “not checked” says so — 2026-09-05. BUILT AND UNDRIVEN** (re-measured 2026-09-05: the capability is real and verified at source, but `trust_store` has never been run, so the tick is withdrawn under this file’s own bar). The panel used to report what each signature *covers* and open with *“pdfcer does not check whether these signatures are valid — it cannot yet”*. It can now: `signature::verify_all_with_trust` is wired, and every signature gets **`Intact:`**, **`Covers:`** and **`Signer:`** as three labelled lines, always all three, in that order — which is also the order of increasing uncertainty, since integrity is arithmetic, coverage is arithmetic, and trust is a judgement about the world. ★★★ **There is no badge and no tick, and that is the whole feature.** `pdfcer-core` returns three facts *that never collapse into one bool* precisely because a reader saying *“valid ✓”* about a chain it did not validate is the one failure worse than saying nothing — so nothing in `panels/signatures.rs` combines two facts into a third, and a signature can read intact-and-untrusted, or trusted-and-covering-half-the-file, and be shown as exactly that. ★★ **`NotChecked` renders as itself** — the words *“not checked”*, never a soft “no”, never a grey tick, never omitted — **and this shell supplies the half the engine structurally cannot: WHICH of four reasons applies.** The engine reports `NotChecked` identically whether the operator opted out, has no Acrobat store, typed a wrong path, or has a store pdfcer could not read, because it was simply handed no anchors. Those four call for four opposite actions — turn the setting on, install Acrobat, fix your typo, your store is corrupt — so there are four sentences and a test that refuses the cheap single one. ★ **Where the anchors come from is disclosed above the list, with the store's DATE in the same sentence as its count**, because an anchor set that silently went stale is worse than one never imported: on this operator's own machine the store is 3.4 MB and was last written **2024-05-27**, sixteen months ago, which a *“1,780 anchors ✓”* badge would have hidden. ⚠ **NOT DRIVEN** and named rather than implied: the headless tests pass and `ui-verify`'s `signature_trust_is_reported_as_its_own_fact` is written, registered and **was not run** — the operator may be at his keyboard. And **nothing here verifies the positive path**: no check in this repository has ever seen a signature read `trusted`, because that needs a real AATL/EUTL anchor and somebody's real certificate
- ⬜ **★★ …and the anchors are the operator's own Acrobat trust list, opt-in and off by default — 2026-09-05. BUILT AND UNDRIVEN.** Settings ▸ **Digital signatures**: a permission (*“Use the trust list my Acrobat has downloaded, at my own risk”*) and a location, with a **Browse…** and a live resolved-state line. The permission is the **engine's** `Settings::acrobat_trust_store`, so the same choice governs `pdfcer verify-signatures` at the command line — one answer to *“may pdfcer read Acrobat's trust list?”*, in one file, for both front ends; the location is a shell preference because `pdfcer-core` deliberately models none (*“locating the file is the shell's job”*). ★★★ **“Importing” is a live READ and pdfcer copies nothing** — no anchor file of its own, no snapshot, no cached certificate on disk. That is the design rather than a shortcut: a copy has no way to say how old it is that anybody will ever read, and a live read has one that costs nothing, which is the file's own modification date. ★ **R9, both ways.** The *Show what is in it* button is **absent** when no store was found — an unavailable capability renders nothing — while the path field is drawn **always**, because it is the remedy, and an absent capability whose remedy is also absent is a dead end. ★★ It is also the row that made `every_setting_the_store_carries_has_a_control_in_this_window` go red, correctly: an earlier session registered the engine setting and gave it no control, so the only way to change it was hand-editing `settings.txt`, which is not a user interface
- ✅ **Fonts** — inventory, embed status, byte cost, font-folder resolution, and **the two verbs it reports on**: Tools ▸ Embed fonts and Tools ▸ Remove embedded fonts, both wired and driven 2026-08-28. The panel's per-font verdict and the removal window's refusal come from **one classifier** — `UnembedBlocker::reason` delegates to `Removability::reason` — because a font that refused in the report and refuses in the window must refuse for the same stated reason, and two catalogs for one classifier is how they come to disagree
- ✅ **Objects** — every object on the page, front-most first; 129,758 on the benchmark drawing
- ✅ **Properties** — read-only facts for a selection
- ✅ **Pages** — thumbnail grid, click to navigate, multi-select (click / Ctrl+click / Shift+extend), context menu of the six page verbs. Selection is marked by a *shape* change plus a written count, never colour alone
- ✅ **★ Drag a page to where it goes, and see where that is before you let go** — 2026-08-18. The tile sensed `Sense::click()` and nothing else, so reordering was two ribbon arrows that move one place at a time and putting page 40 in front of page 3 was thirty-seven presses. An **insertion caret** follows the nearest tile boundary during the drag, **dimmed** over a boundary that would change nothing — hiding it there cannot be told apart from the panel having stopped tracking the pointer, and that boundary is where *every* drag begins, because a block starts out hovering over itself. The header says the same thing in a page number, in the Insert-from-file dialog's own vocabulary. The arithmetic is **gap-indexed**, not page-indexed: there are `n + 1` landings in `n` pages and numbering them by the page they precede leaves *after the last sheet* unnameable, which is the commonest drag on a drawing set. A scattered selection dropped at its own first page **is** a real edit — gathering sheets together is the most useful thing a drag does — so the no-op predicate tests contiguity rather than the block's endpoints. A drop that lands nowhere raises **no action**: `reorder_pages` would accept an identity permutation and record an undo entry for it
- ✅ **Comments** — every annotation in the document, page order then `/Annots` order, reusing `pdfcer list-annotations`' ordering by name. `/Widget` excluded because Forms owns it, `/Popup` excluded as an implementation detail of another annotation, and **ce dimensions deliberately *not* excluded** — they are `/Line` annotations, and filtering that subtype would also hide a real `/Line` markup. Every exclusion is disclosed **in numbers**, so nothing is silently omitted; the old shell stated the rule only on the empty case. Reads the session, so unsaved edits show. A markup with no note says so as a fact about the document, not as an error
- ✅ **★★★ …and the Comments panel can WRITE now — 2026-08-28, and it had been a viewer for the life of the project.** Add note / Edit note / Remove note on every row, so the four things `pdfcer-core` calls *what a review IS* all work: comment a shape you just drew, comment a highlight you just swept, fix a typo in your own comment, answer someone else's. ★★★ **The blocker was structural and then it was stale.** `MarkupOptions` is an **author-time** type and a cloud, a rectangle and an arrow are authored on mouse-release from geometry alone — there is no text-entry moment in that gesture and there must not be one, because a dialog on every shape a reviewer draws is the interaction nobody ships. So the conventional model (*draw the shape → it is selected → type the comment in the panel*) needed a verb acting on an annotation that **already exists**; `set_markup_note` shipped in `Pass 154.0` in answer to this shell's own request, and nothing here called it until an audit of every engine verb went looking. ★★★ **Correcting somebody else's typo does not re-attribute their comment**: `/T` is left untouched when the annotation already carries one — the engine called writing all three keys unconditionally *"the easiest way to get this wrong"*, because it leaves a review comment from nobody, dated never, looking exactly like one somebody had mangled. The flag that decides it is computed from the row the panel drew and is the **same expression** that chooses the disclosure sentence, so what the operator reads and what is written cannot disagree. ★★ **The draft is stamped `(annotation, edit epoch)`** — type three sentences, press Ctrl+Z on something unrelated, press Save, and without the stamp those words land on whatever annotation now holds that object id. ★ **Overwriting a note leaves no trace on the canvas** — the shape does not change, and a sticky's words live in a pop-up this shell does not draw — so the previous text is put on the status line **verbatim**, not as a count, which is what lets an operator retype it rather than merely be told it is gone. A ce dimension declines with the reason (its text comes from its measurement) rather than a greyed button, per R9
- ✅ **★★ Markup can be authored SEE-THROUGH — 2026-08-28.** An Opacity control on Markup ▸ Style, applied to every kind including the sticky note, the text box and the stamp. The point on a drawing is specific: a comment sits on top of the thing it is about, and an opaque cloud hides the dimension it is drawing attention to. ★★★ **One verb, one undo entry** — `add_markup_with` rather than author-then-restyle, and the reason is the engine's and is a **defect** argument rather than a convenience one: *"an operator who draws a translucent highlight and presses Ctrl+Z once gets an OPAQUE highlight, not no highlight. That is a state they never asked for and cannot have created any other way."* ★ Fully opaque writes **no `/CA` key at all**, so a build whose operator never touches the control authors byte-identical annotations to the one before it; and the bottom of the range is a tenth rather than zero, because a control whose bottom end authors an invisible mark is a defect report waiting to be filed. ★★ **Its blocker was the seventh stale one this project has found**: the module header said `/CA` was *"blocked on the engine — filed, accepted, not started"*, and it had shipped the previous day in answer to a request this shell filed itself
- ✅ **★★★ Forms can now be AUTHORED, not only filled — 2026-08-26, on the operator's ask.** Five commands on Edit ▸ Forms, one per kind `pdfcer-core` has a verb for: text field, check box, radio button, drop-down, push button. Click one and then **click the page** to place it at the conventional size for that kind, or **drag a box** for an exact one. Neither authors anything: the release opens a dialog, and the field exists once Add is pressed — which is what makes Escape free and stops a mis-drag leaving a stray field behind, and a stray form field is worse than a stray rectangle because it is invisible on a printed page and swallows every keystroke aimed near it. ★★ **The blocker this was parked behind for nine days did not exist.** `shell::commands::reach::register` recorded *"core's STRUCTURAL certification gate"*; probed against a real drawing, `add_text_field` authors a field and returns its id. What the engine refuses is a spec whose tooltip is `TooltipChoice::Undecided` — an **accessibility** requirement, not a permission, because a form control owes a screen reader a name and the engine will not invent one silently. The entire blocker was a field of the very dialog this feature needs. **Fourth stale blocker in this project**; the standing rule that produced the probe is *a backlog row is a record, not evidence*. ★★ **The settings carry to the next field, and the NAME deliberately does not** — two widgets sharing a fully-qualified name are **one field**, so typing in either changes both, and nothing on the page says so. Names auto-advance and fill gaps rather than counting (a document with `Text1` and `Text3` has two fields and `Text2` free). **Radio buttons are the deliberate inverse**: the name *is* the group, so it persists and the export value advances instead. ★★★ **AND THE PUSH BUTTON DOES SOMETHING — 2026-09-01.** It was greyed for the life of the project because `add_push_button` authors a correct widget and pdfcer ran no PDF action, so a button placed then would have looked right and done nothing. `pdfcer-core` shipped `set_button_action` on 2026-08-30 and the condition the greying rode — `enabled_when("forms.push_button_runnable")`, chosen precisely so this would be one line — is now set. **Placing a button asks what pressing it does, seven ways**: nothing, clear the form, go to a page, move through the pages, show or hide fields, open a web address, send the form's data. Every one states what it **reaches**, including the five that reach nothing, because a disclosure drawn only on the risky choice is one an operator learns to skip. The submit carries the four §12.7.5.2 facts nobody can guess and Acrobat's own warning omits: hidden fields are sent, masked fields are sent as plain text, a file-select field sends the named file's contents, and the baseline payload already carries the document's own path and its `/ID`. ★★ **No scheme is refused** — `https` appears zero times in ISO 32000-1, so blocking `http://` would be pdfcer inventing a conformance requirement; it is *stated* and the operator decides. What IS refused is an address pdfcer cannot state unambiguously: relative (§7.11.2.2 resolves it against the document's own location, and ISO issue #256 records readers disagreeing) or non-ASCII. ★ **Nothing is marked on the canvas**: a button carrying a submit is drawn exactly as the saved file will draw it, which is also why a screenshot cannot judge this feature and the driven check reads the trace. **Verified: driven** — `a_placed_button_can_be_given_something_to_do` drags out a button, opens the chooser, clicks a popup row and presses Add, asserting `button-action-applied … kind=ResetForm` reached the engine. ★★★ **The finding is not the feature.** The engine shipped this on 2026-08-30 with a reply saying *"please check your own copy — your surface is now saying something untrue"*; it was consumed on 2026-09-01, because **nothing in this repository failed when a capability landed**. `tools/gates/check-verb-coverage.sh` now does, and found five more the moment it worked. **The one existing-button gap**: `pdfcer-core` can write a button's action and cannot read one, so the Forms panel has no row for a button already in the document — filed, with a tripwire test that names its own deletion
- ✅ **★★ Clicking a field on the page opens its properties — and the greying turned out to be drawn rather than enforced.** In Edit mode a click selects the field; in Read and Review it still fills it, which is how every program that both fills and authors forms behaves. The Properties pane names the field, its kind, its page, its value, which flags are set and how many boxes it is drawn as, and offers **Rename**, **Delete field** and **Delete this box**. ★★★ Driving the release binary with `edit.form_push_button` armed the tool and traced `form-tool-armed kind=PushButton` **while its ribbon item was correctly greyed** — because `egui` refusing a click on a disabled widget is the *entire* mechanism of greying, and a chord, the QAT, a context menu and the harness seam all reach the dispatcher without passing the ribbon. Ninety-nine commands carry an `enabled_when`. The obvious repair — one guard at the top of `dispatch_command` — was written and **the test suite refused it**, because it would have made `Ctrl+Z` on an empty stack do nothing *and* say nothing: **greying is a hint; the worded decline is the answer**, so enforcement lives where the words can, in the arm. ★ **The selectable set is wider than the fillable one**, which is the change: five conditions exclude a widget from being typed into and every one is wrong for selecting, so a drop-down, a push button and an undrawn widget are all clickable. Both sets come from one walk, and moving `classify` to make room broke the panel's "N fields can only be filled here" disclosure until a test caught it. ★ **Required, read-only, the tooltip and the border cannot be changed after placing** — the engine has four verbs for an existing field and none for its flags — and the pane **says so, with the remedy**, rather than leaving an absence that is indistinguishable from an oversight. Filed as `request_field_property_edit.md`. **Verified: driven** — `ui-verify form_field` arms the tool, clicks empty page, watches the field authored, then clicks a published widget and asserts the properties section drew
- ✅ **Forms** — fill, **in all three modes including Read**. Reading the session, so unsaved edits show. Read was the operator's call on 2026-08-14 — *Acrobat Reader fills forms in its default view, and replacing it is the stated goal* — and it cost the taxonomy amendment plus a tab move: a command lives on exactly one tab and Read is shown File and View alone, so `edit.form_fill` became `view.panel_forms` in View ▸ Panels. Edit ▸ Forms keeps create, manage and flatten, which is the line the move draws: **filling is not authoring**

### Canvas

- ✅ **★★★ Annotations and ce dimensions ROTATE — 2026-08-28** (`Pass 155.0`, `Pass 159.0`). A ninth grip, a circle on a stem above the selection box; Shift snaps to 15°. ★★ **A ce dimension gets the circle and nothing else** — no scale squares, because scaling one is *declined* rather than unbuilt: either the displayed value stays fixed while the geometry grows, so the dimension lies about the drawing, or both change, so nothing was measured and the operator has **drawn** a number rather than taken one. A form field gets no circle at all.
