One function answers — `pressing::grabbable` returns a `GripSet` — and that one value feeds the hit test, the painter and the drag, so the three cannot disagree about what is on offer. ★ **No confirmation and no distortion warning**, unlike resize: a rotation is an isometry, and pdfcer composes the angle into the `/Matrix` a producer already wrote, so it works on a stamp Acrobat drew where a resize refuses. ★ **`/Rect` grows** at any angle that is not a quarter turn — §12.5.2 requires it upright — and this shell draws its outline from `/Rect`, so the operator watches a box swell around artwork that did not change size; disclosed, and silent at 90°. ★★ **A `Linear` dimension's axis lock relaxes to *aligned*** and says so, with *"the measurement has not changed"* in the same breath, because that is the question the first clause provokes. ★★★ **And wiring it found the fifth instance of this canvas's oldest hazard, live**: `presspick::covers()` — the function whose own doc comment records the *fourth* — still asked `overlay::grip_box`, which is `None` for an annotation, so a press on a markup's rotate handle in Edit would have selected whatever content sits 20 pt above it and rotated **that**. It never looks broken from a chair, because something moves. ⇒ **A guard that must agree with another module has to CALL it, not resemble it.**


- ✅ **★★★ Clicking an object inside a form XObject selects THAT OBJECT — 2026-08-27**, and it is the operator's largest single report closed: *"There are obviously more than one item on the page, but when I click on one of the objects all I get is the page selected."* He was clicking a real object. A **form XObject** wrapped it, and a form's `/BBox` is a §8.10.1 **clipping extent** — a statement about where painting is *allowed*, not about where ink *is* — so a form declaring the whole `MediaBox` and drawing one line sat in paint order above everything before it and won every click at every point. The outline it produced hugged the page edge, which looks exactly like a state this program does not have. Measured on the files this project actually uses: the industry print-conformance suite's composite page 1 has **28 page objects, 4 forms and 242 objects inside them**; `ncored-benchmark-cad-drawing` page 1 has 129,758, one form, and **10,256 inside it**; his own `SW41177.pdf` has 5,903, no forms, and **nothing changes on it at all** — which is what says the fix is aimed at the right thing. The pick is `hit_test_point_deep` and the **marquee has the same reach**, so the two gestures that both mean *select this* cannot disagree about what is selectable. ★★ **There is deliberately no fallback to the shallow hit test when the deep one finds nothing**: the commonest empty answer is a click on blank paper *inside* a page-sized form, and a fallback would answer it with the form — the original complaint, restored, for the case that produces it most often. The driven check asserts both halves and was falsified against a rebuilt binary with the shallow call put back, where it reports the operator's sentence back verbatim. ★★★ **The safety property underneath it**: a page now has **two** object lists — its own content stream's, and the objects painted from inside its forms — and a leaf's token range indexes *the form's* buffer, so a leaf index handed to a page-stream editing verb is **in range and wrong**, which corrupts a file silently rather than failing. `TargetId` is a two-variant enum and `page_object_index()`, which answers `None` for a leaf, is the only supported way to obtain an edit operand. The compiler found every site that had to choose
- ✅ **★★ "Select the form" — the deliberate second act, 2026-08-27.** Because the hit test now excludes forms outright, a form had no route on the canvas at all — and a form is a perfectly good thing to want: it is one page object with an ordinary paint-order index, and moving a title block or deleting a stamp is *the form*, not the two hundred objects inside it. On the contextual **Format** tab and in the canvas right-click menu, greyed when the selection is not inside a form. It resolves the leaf to its **outermost** enclosing form — the one whose index an edit verb can take — so after pressing it Delete has something to delete, and everything drawn inside moves with it
- ✅ **★★ …and the three surfaces that would otherwise have lied.** The **status bar** reads the selection rather than the edit-operand list — reading the operand list would have made the line go *silent* on exactly the selection it was written for — and says `Selected: Path · 12.4 × 8.0 pt · inside a form`, or *inside 3 nested forms* when it is deeper. **Delete** on such a selection raises nothing and says *"That object is inside a form — pdfcer cannot edit inside one yet"* instead of doing nothing at all: an outline round the thing you want gone plus a dead Delete key reads as a broken program, and it is not one. A **drag** answered *"nothing selected"* while an outline was on screen; it answers `InsideForm` now. ⬜ **What still does not work, said before you find it:** no edit verb can address a form-interior object — `pdfcer-core` writes a paint-order edit to the *page's* content stream and that object lives in the form's, and `FormLeaf::is_editable()` is `false` for every one of them. Select the form and move that. A double-click will not descend into one either, and that is deliberate: the Part and Node rungs exist to act on geometry, and descending into something you then cannot change promises editability
- ✅ **★★ The measure tools see inside a form too — 2026-08-27.** `pdfcer-core` answered the request the same day it was filed, with the identical fix this shell had just made to its own id type, arrived at independently from the other side of the API boundary. The **two-line dimension** was not degraded on a wrapped drawing, it was **inert** — on the benchmark CAD sheet that is 10,256 candidate lines the picker could not see — and it had been so since the tool shipped, hidden behind the selection defect. The **hover highlight** went deep with it, and that was the more urgent half: a highlight is a promise about what the next click will take, so a shallow highlight over a deep pick is a marker on one line and a highlight on another
- ✅ **Render** — off-thread, generation-counted, cancellable between content-stream operators
- ✅ **Zoom ladder** with a per-page raster ceiling that accounts for `pixels_per_point`
- ✅ **Three fit modes — page, width and height — and each PLACES the view as well as setting the scale.** 2026-08-24, O28/O29. Fit page centres the sheet; Fit width and Fit height centre the axis they fit and keep the operator's position on the other, clamped to the page so "kept" cannot mean "still looking at pasteboard". Before O23's pasteboard a fitted page had nowhere to be except the middle, so the button never had to choose; afterwards, *the scale is right and the page is not on screen* became reachable. Fit height is the Acrobat-parity mode and the useful one for a landscape sheet in a tall window
- ✅ **The mouse wheel turns pages, if you ask it to.** 2026-08-24, O30. A toggle beside the page buttons in the status bar, in single-page and facing displays only — under a continuous display the wheel scrolls the whole document by definition and the control is not drawn at all. Off by default, because on a page that already fits the window today's wheel has nothing to scroll and does nothing, which is what the request is about. Persisted, and it takes effect on the very next notch rather than waiting for a Settings apply
- ✅ **Cursor-anchored Ctrl+wheel zoom** — under 0.01 px drift
- ✅ **Middle-drag pan**, wheel scroll, **hand tool and space-to-pan**
- ✅ **Four page-display modes** — Single · Continuous · Facing · Facing-continuous, as a radio whose active position renders pressed. **Single page is provably unchanged**: its strip is one row with no gap, so the scroll range, centring margin, pan clamp and zoom anchor are the same arithmetic, asserted as an equality against the pre-Phase-4 expression
- ✅ **An undrawn page says so** — its real boundary, a fill that is visibly not paper, and a sentence centred in the part of the page **on screen**
- ✅ **Selection as identity, not position** — page + object + subpath + node, four integers and no coordinate
- ✅ **Selection survives navigation** — zoom, pan, fit, view rotation, page-display mode and tab change, all asserted byte-identical
- ✅ **Multi-select**, marquee, level ladder with Escape ascending one rung
- ✅ **★★★ A right-to-left box takes what it TOUCHES — 2026-09-02, `OPERATOR_REQUESTS.md` O88.** His report: *"I can't box select the tables in the left or right top corners — it only picks up the lines of each table, so I can't drag the entire thing and move it somewhere else."* ★★ **It was never a hit test excluding text.** Both tables sit hard against the sheet edge, and this shell asked for `MarqueeMode::Enclosed` everywhere — so surrounding one would mean starting the band **outside the page**, where at fit zoom there is barely a pixel of margin. The only band that can be drawn is one *inside* the table, which surrounds a few short rules and nothing else. ⇒ *"It only picks up the lines"* is what an enclosing band returns when it cannot be drawn big enough. **Built as AutoCAD's direction-sensitive band**, which SolidWorks drawings use too: left→right is a *window* and encloses, right→left is a *crossing window* and touches. No modifier key, nothing new to learn, and it is the conventional interaction rather than an invention — Illustrator touches always, Inkscape puts touch on `Alt`. ★ The enclosing answer is unchanged; decision 011's reasoning is untouched, and what was wrong was that it was the only answer. ★★★ **One hazard, found by a failing test rather than by thinking:** a crossing band **touches a page-sized form XObject wherever it is drawn**, so on a wrapped drawing every right-to-left drag would have silently included the whole sheet and the next gesture would move it — under `Enclosed` that was impossible, because a band that *surrounds* a page-sized form has to surround the page. Dropped by `canvas::marquee::without_page_wrappers` using the shell's **existing** `container_is_worth_selecting` rule, the one the click ladder already applies; no second threshold exists, and **only a hit that contains another hit is tested**, so a drawing border covering the sheet stays selectable. ★★ **DRIVEN and falsified** on his own sheet: `marquee-mode crossing=true mode=touched hits=3 paths=2 text=1`. With `mode_for` stubbed to return `Enclosed` for both directions the check fails; restored, it passes. ★★★ **Getting there took three wrong diagnoses behind one unchanged failure message** — the view had scrolled and the band was driven off-canvas, then no tool was armed, then the origin was **on ink** so the press selected and the drag became a move. The first was fixed and written up, and **the write-up masked the other two**. ★ And the check's own oracle was an assumption: it demanded a count *"well into double figures"*, while that whole drawing decomposes into **25 objects, 19 paths and 6 text** — so three was a large fraction of the page and the threshold was rejecting a correct result. It now asserts the **kinds**, because *"it only picks up the lines"* is a claim about a kind being missing and a count ranks one-path-one-text below nine-paths-no-text at every threshold. ⬜ **The enclosing direction is not driven and cannot be on that sheet** — every corner a surrounding band could start from is on ink, which is his complaint restated
- ✅ **★★ …and that also reaches an object dropped OFF the sheet — 2026-09-02, `OPERATOR_REQUESTS.md` O92.** *"We should be able to select things offside of the page, especially since I sometimes drop objects there, and when I do I can't get them back."* Select All already took them; what was missing was the gesture anybody would reach for. It needed **no new code** — a band started on the sheet and dragged out into the grey touches an object lying entirely off the edge, where an enclosing band over the same rectangle surrounds nothing. ★★★ **Driven on a fixture whose oracle is airtight**: a 200 × 200 page with exactly two squares, one on the page and one entirely left of the media box, and a band aimed to miss the first and to touch — but be unable to enclose — the second. So `hits == 1` can only be the off-page one, with no index or ordering assumption. **Falsified** by stubbing the direction rule: *"THE BAND REACHED INTO THE MARGIN AND FOUND NOTHING"*. ★★ The harness needed a named `doc_to_window_off_page`, because the ordinary conversion refuses every point outside the media box — and its own first version bounded the result against `image_rect`, **which is the page's rectangle**, so it rejected the whole class it existed for while reporting *"not enough margin on screen"*. The bound that means something is the canvas **viewport**
- ✅ **Delete** — click then Delete, verified end to end by `ui-verify` against the real binary
- ✅ **★★★ Ctrl takes things OUT of a selection — 2026-09-03, `OPERATOR_REQUESTS.md` O104.** His report: *"I can't unselect things once I have selected them for redaction."* **The capability existed and the route he reached for did not.** Shift+click already toggled an object out; **Ctrl did nothing at all** — `canvas::interact` read `i.modifiers.shift` and no other modifier — so reaching for it got a plain click, which REPLACES the selection with the one object under the pointer. The thing he was trying to remove became the only thing selected. ★★ He is a SolidWorks user, and in SolidWorks, in Explorer and in nearly every list on this operating system **Ctrl+click is the toggle**; both spellings are now taken, because Shift is the vector-editor convention and Ctrl is the Windows one and accepting both costs nothing. ★★★ **And a band can now SUBTRACT** — no modifier replaces, **Shift adds, Ctrl removes** — which is the half a click could never answer: on a CAD sheet of hundreds of overlapping strokes, picking one object back out of a selection of twenty by clicking it precisely is often not practical, and a band is how the work is actually done. ★ Ctrl rather than Shift for the subtract even though AutoCAD spells that one with Shift, because Shift-adds is already shipped here and in every vector editor these operators also use, and re-pointing it would break a gesture they have — and it would contradict Ctrl+click. One modifier, one meaning. ★★ **An empty subtracting band is a NO-OP, never a clear**, and the asymmetry is the whole safety of the gesture: a plain band enclosing nothing means *select nothing*, but a subtracting band hitting nothing must not destroy the selection the operator was refining |
- ✅ **★★★ Redaction WORKS on your drawings now — 2026-09-03, O103, DRIVEN.** *"every time I've tried the redact feature it tells me it can't because there is objects that weren't redacted."* ★★★ **Reproduced with `pdfcer` alone, so it was the ENGINE's refusal** — it could not destroy image pixels, and the gate was the mark's **rectangle**, not the pixels it would remove, so *mark whole page* could never be applied on any sheet carrying a logo and a title-block value inches from one clipped its bounding box. Apply was all-or-nothing for the document: twelve careful marks and one that grazed a logo redacted nothing. Which is exactly *"every time I've tried"*. **Filed, and the engine answered the same afternoon — twice.** v0.26.0 then v0.27.0. All three of our asks in the order we ranked them: the gate is on the **samples** not the bounding boxes; a covered image has its pixels **destroyed** (decoded, overwritten, its soft mask cleared to match, re-encoded losslessly) and a wholly covered one removed outright; an image it cannot decode retains **that mark alone** while every other mark applies. ★★★ **Plus one we never thought to ask for: vector lines through a region are CUT at the boundary.** The engine found it by rendering his `17036-15` before and after and noticing the title-block geometry had not moved — on a CAD sheet that outline can be as identifying as the text was. Measured on his files: the corner mark cuts 25 path objects, exit 0; the whole-page marks drop 780 and 1,089 objects, exit 0. Destroyed cells come back as **paper**, not black. ★★ **Our half:** the mark-time sentence now reads *"those pixels will be destroyed, not hidden"* — it used to say the apply would be refused, and became the exact opposite of the truth within hours. The report says what happened to each image, names a **shared** image copied so the other pages keep theirs, and counts the geometry cut and dropped. Three new residuals gate the write: a **retained mark** (a region where nothing was removed, under a rectangle that says it was — the engine names this as the number to read before the word *redacted*), geometry that could not be cut, and a clip whose outline had to be kept. ★★ **Driven and falsified BOTH WAYS**: a CAD sheet with no image is marked in **silence**, because a caveat attached to every mark is one you learn to scroll past |
- ✅ **★★★ The tab order list is reordered by DRAGGING, with the marker he asked for — 2026-09-02, `OPERATOR_REQUESTS.md` O99, DRIVEN.** His words: *"the tab order list is supposed to be able to be reordered by dragging and dropping rows around like we can with pages in the page preview, and have clear markers of where the field is going to move to."* Built on `EditSession::reorder_annotations`, which the engine shipped **hours after the request went out** — the row had read ⛔ *"no verb can do it"* for nineteen days. It is deliberately the page rail's insertion caret to the constant: same gap model, same theme colour, same full-strength/dimmed pair for *"releasing here changes nothing"*, same rule that a release is read from raw input so a drag can end anywhere. The one honest difference is that the caret is **horizontal**, because a list flows down and an insertion mark runs across the flow. ★★★ **The hard part is that a row is not an array entry.** The list holds widgets a field claims; `/Annots` also holds unclaimed widgets, anonymous ones, and every `/Link`, stamp and markup on the page — so a list built from the rows is a permutation of a **subset**, which the engine refuses by name rather than silently deleting a page's links. Each row therefore carries its **slot** in the array. ★★ And the widgets move **among widget slots** while every other entry keeps its index, which is a *choice*: `/Annots` order is **paint order**, so carrying a widget past a `/Link` would change what is drawn over what — a visible change to the page from a gesture whose whole subject was tab sequence. The engine's `non_widgets_moved` disclosure is what revealed the trap; this route reports zero by construction and the driven check asserts it. ★ **Ids, not indices**, at the engine's insistence, and it was more necessary than they knew: a row's `position` is 1-based, counts widgets only, and skips entries with no id — wrong on three axes at once, all three cancelling on a well-formed form. ⬜ **`/Tabs` is read and never written**, sourced: `/A` is PDF-2.0-only, PDF/UA-1 §7.18.3 *requires* `/S`, nothing requires a writer to state a tab order at all, and Acrobat's own manual tab order writes none — so on a page that states an order a conforming reader may still follow it, which is what the unconditional per-page `/Tabs` sentence is for. ★★ **Four failures on the way to green and NOT ONE was the drag**: rows published a rectangle while scrolled out of view so the pointer landed below the window; a ribbon press closed the panel; the wheel was eaten by a nested scroll area; and the pane was too short for the content to exist in |
- ✅ **★★ Clicking a form row lights that field on the page — 2026-09-02, O98, DRIVEN.** *"when we have the fill form panel visible and I click on fields in it instead it should highlight the field on the canvas that is being filled."* The panel writes the field's fully-qualified name into a frame-scoped channel while a row has focus; the canvas reads it while painting and outlines that box. **Panel → canvas**; the other direction shipped as O53. ★ On **focus**, not on click, and the difference is his own word *"filled"*: a click-only trigger would put the light out the moment they started typing, which is exactly when they want to know which box they are in. ★★ Rule 4 permits it by name — it is the cursor, not a mark on content: transient, following attention, gone when the row loses focus. ⬜ **It does nothing in EDIT mode**, because the canvas's whole form overlay is gated on the Select tool. For the wash and the click targets that gate is right; for a read-only cue driven entirely by the panel it may not be, and his message named no tool. Filed for his decision rather than changed |
- ✅ **★★ Fillable fields wear a wash, and the display buttons are two rows — 2026-09-02, O96 and O97, both DRIVEN.** *"in our display section we should have an option to shade the form fields like acrobat does"* and *"our display buttons should be on two rows to save space."* The wash defaults **on** — an operator who has never opened the settings should be able to see which boxes are fillable — and takes the theme's **hyperlink** role at low alpha, not `selection`, which would make every field look selected. ★ The two-row group is a new manifest field, `Group::prefer_rows`, so it is a **layout hint in data** rather than a special case in the ribbon code. Its check is pure geometry — four rectangles in exactly two vertical bands, two per band, separated by at least a button's height — and it pins the window to a fixed wide viewport, because at a narrow width the group would wrap for the *ordinary* reason and the check would pass over a build whose hint is dead. ★★ The wash's check had to teach the trace **three** states first: turned off, on with no fields, and on with fields present and none painted. Only the third is a defect, and before the trace carried all three a build with the feature entirely dead looked identical to a correct build run against a document with no form |
- ✅ **★ The title bar says when this binary was built — 2026-09-02, O101, DRIVEN.** *"add the local compilation time to the top bar at the end of the date you added."* ★★ He is closing a loop his own reports opened: **two backlog rows have been closed by "you were running an old build"**, and on a day with several publishes a date alone cannot tell two apart. ★ The zone is shown only when it is **not** local — a packaged build is stamped in local time so its offset is noise, a dev build is UTC and keeps the label, because a stamp that says the wrong hour is worse than one that says a true hour in a named zone. Its check needs **no pointer at all** — the title is traced on change and the window lays out without taking focus — which makes it one of four that can run while the machine is in use |
- ✅ **Context menus** — canvas object, canvas empty, Objects row, Pages row, dock tab; a menu with nothing to offer never opens
- ✅ **Read mode does not edit the document** — 2026-08-14, on the operator's ask. Capability is derived from the **mode's tab list in the manifest**, not from the string `"read"`, so the canvas and the ribbon read one sentence: a mode showing the Edit tab can always edit and one hiding it never can, which makes *"a visible control, silently inert"* unrepresentable rather than merely avoided. Read grants pan, zoom, hand, marquee-**zoom**, Find, a right-click offering the three zoom levels, and **form filling** (unchanged — `canvas::forms` still reads no mode). It refuses object selection, move, resize grips, marquee-select, Delete and markup placement. Review refuses content and keeps markup and dimensions, which is the row that proves the gate is per-capability rather than one on/off. Three things the gesture gate could not fix on its own, each closed separately: a **click is not a drag**, so gating presses alone would have left the commonest gesture on the canvas ungated; an **armed tool outlives a mode switch** (it lives in `egui::Memory`, app-scoped) and left a crosshair promising a gesture already refused; and a **selection outlives it too**, leaving eight resize handles on a page in Read. Entering a mode now retires a tool it forbids, clears a content selection, and cancels a gesture in flight — a selection is not *work* under rule 1, and the undo stack is untouched
- ✅ **Find** — `Ctrl+F` or the status-bar toggle. Case, whole-word (with a configurable word rule, because ISO 32000-1 declines to define "word"), and **wildcards off by default**: `find_text` enables them, which is why typing `?` into the old shell's Find bar matched every character on the page. Exactly one `TextSearchOptions` is constructed in the whole crate
- ✅ **Find never searches on a keystroke** — measured at 331–449 ms per search on the benchmark sheet, because the engine re-extracts the document's text every call. Enter, the step buttons, or an option change after a search has run
- ✅ **Stale hits are cleared and said** — an edit clears the highlights, keeps the query, and the readout reads *Document changed*. A quad recorded before a delete can cover different glyphs afterwards
- ✅ **Move** — drag a selection; live ghost preview with no re-raster; multi-select moves as **one** command
- ✅ **Subpath and node moves** — Part rung → `move_subpath`, Node rung → `move_node`; a text run declines rather than borrowing the Object rung's verb
- ✅ **★★★ Delete now removes ONE line, ONE label or ONE corner point — 2026-09-05, DRIVEN AND FALSIFIED, and it is one defect FOUR times.** ⚠ **This row said ✅ for one commit while the key did nothing**, and the correction is the most useful thing on it: the routing landed at `f9ded5a` and the three verbs were still unreachable from the keyboard, because `canvas::interact` gated the page decomposition on a hand-maintained list of **gesture outcomes** and Delete is a **keystroke**. The trace read `canvas-delete-declined level=Part sel=1 reason=NoObjectModel` — a refusal that is correct for *'the page would not decompose'* and was reporting *'nobody asked for it'*. The **ribbon's** Delete worked the whole time, because `app::dispatch::format` asks for the model unconditionally; only the key was blind. `canvas::modelneed` now owns that question, its gesture half is an exhaustive match (a new variant is a compile error) and its keyboard half is the term a list of gestures cannot hold. Three driven checks PASS — `deleting_a_label_leaves_the_other_labels_alone`, `deleting_a_line_leaves_the_rest_of_the_shape_alone`, `deleting_a_point_leaves_the_rest_of_the_line_alone` — each falsified twice, once by removing the model term and once by routing the rung to `delete_objects`. ★★★ **The label is reached with the Points tool (`A`), NOT with a double-click**, and that is by the operator's own ruling: O70 gave the double-click on text to the caret (*'double-clicking inside the bounding box should edit the text'*), so `canvas::clicking` opens a caret and returns before the ladder is touched. `canvas::clicking`'s node-tool branch is the route that exists — one click, one label selected — and the driven check drives that gesture rather than a gesture the program deliberately does not have. Until this landed, Delete acted at the **Object rung only**: every deeper rung traced `canvas-delete-declined … reason=no-verb-for-rung` and **nothing happened, silently**, which is the operator's own *"nothing happens"* in a third place. The refusal was honest when it was written and stopped being honest the moment `pdfcer-core` shipped the three verbs — each of which had its **move** twin wired here, so for a fortnight a line could be entered, selected and **dragged** and could not be removed. ★★★ **All three bite him on his own CAD drawings**, and the engine built one of them for exactly his file: *"one text object holds all 237 dimension labels, so deleting 'a label' deleted every one of them"* — those are **pdf dimensions**, page content pdfcer reads and must not silently alter. One measured export holds a whole isometric view as a single path object with **1,194 subpaths**, so *"delete this line"* used to offer only *"delete the drawing view"*. ★★ **The decision is one pure function, `canvas::deleting::subject`, and BOTH the Delete key and the ribbon's `format.delete` ask it** — a destructive rule stated twice is a rule that drifts, and it had already drifted: since 2026-09-01 the key deleted an object inside a form XObject and the ribbon command explained why it could not. ★★★ **`delete_node` owes a SENTENCE and gets one**: its disclosure list is non-empty when removing the point discarded a curve — *"the shape now goes straight from the point before to the point after"* — a change re-adding a point cannot undo, and the engine's own doc says rule 4 forbids letting the operator find it out from a diff. It reaches the status row through the funnel that already records every verb's disclosures, not through a second mechanism beside it. ★ **R83 is honoured for the label case**: §9.4.2 says a label with no position of its own starts where the one before it ends, so removing the earlier one slides it — asked **before** the press through `text_run_delete_would_move_next` (built 2026-08-28 and never called until now), which is the only way the operator can be told the remedy, *delete the later label first*, since the engine's own refusal may not be routed to a surface. ★ Three refusals speak and eight stay silent, deliberately: an operator meets those three having done nothing wrong, and a bar that narrates *"nothing is selected"* stops being read. ⬜ **The Part and Node rungs INSIDE a form XObject still cannot delete, and that is the ENGINE's gap**: its six `*_in_form` verbs are five moves and one whole-object delete. Named on the status row with what does work rather than left as a dead key
- ✅ **★★ Several anchors move together — 2026-08-19.** `pdfcer`'s `gui` column ticked this `[x]` and their own sweep corrected it: *"objects move together; nodes one at a time"*. It was false in an unusually quiet way — `pick_within` has added a Shift-clicked anchor as its own entry since the Node rung landed, so a multi-node set was **representable from day one**, and `moving::subject` read `entered_object()`, which is the first entry. Four picked, one moved. Nothing failed; both halves' unit tests passed. A capability the data model supports and no consumer reads is the hardest kind of gap to see, so the accessor now has a name and a doc comment. `MoveSubject::Nodes` sits beside `Node` rather than growing a `Vec`, because `EditSession` has both verbs; one stale anchor refuses the **whole** drag rather than moving the three that still resolve
- ✅ **★★ The anchors are VISIBLE — and until 2026-08-19 they were not, at any rung.** `FEATURES.md` recorded it against `view.show_points` and the consequence was not drawn out: an operator could descend two rungs, Shift-click four anchors, and have no surface anywhere saying which four. Squares for on-curve points, hollow circles for control points, tethers between — the idiom Illustrator, Inkscape, Figma and the old shell share. ★ **Scoped to the entered SUBPATH**, and the first version was not: it drew the object's, capped at 400, and driving `SW41177.pdf` printed `canvas-anchors total=4972` — the cap fired, nothing drew, and the feature was blank on precisely the documents it exists for. A cap that suppresses the answer where it is needed is not a performance guard
- ✅ **★★ Bézier handles drag — Phase 1's last row, 2026-08-19, and the engine had it all along.** `EditSession::move_handle` has existed since Pass 30.1 with a `Handle` enum, a planner, a `v`/`y` re-spelling path and a disclosure contract; what was missing was a way to see a handle and a way to grab one. ★ **The priority rule, and it is the same rule twice in one day**: a handle sits *inside* the selection's box, so `grip_at` answers `Grip::Move` for every press on one — and this morning the corner **anchors** were undraggable because the corner resize grips covered them. One rule covers both: *the most specific thing under the pointer wins, and specificity is depth down the selection ladder.* The eight scale grips are now the Object rung's alone, painted and hit-tested by one predicate. ★ **The disclosure is invisible by construction and is forwarded anyway**: `move_handle` answers with sentences that are empty unless a `v`/`y` had to be re-spelled as `c` — Table 59 gives a cubic three spellings and two omit a control point — and **the curve draws identically**. What changes is that the original bytes are gone and dragging back does not restore them, which is rule 4's surviving half exactly. Deliberately absent: turning a line into a curve (the engine refuses it by name, so no handle is drawn there and the refusal is unreachable from the pointer), and smooth/symmetric constraints (a modelling convention PDF has no notion of, which would make one gesture two undo entries)
- ✅ **Escape cancels a drag in flight** without committing, and never both cancels and ascends a rung
- ✅ **Eight handles — and they commit** as of 2026-08-19, through `move_nodes` with the grip's opposite corner as the pivot. Cursors correct, drag consumed so it cannot fall through to a marquee
- ✅ **Dock-tab context menu** — `egui-shell` hands the tab's `Response` to the application; the built-in Close survives for consumers that do not opt in

### Verification

- ✅ **`ui-verify`** — drives the real binary through the OS, asserts on the diagnostic trace **and** the pixels
- ⬜ **175 checks registered** (`checks::all()` in `tools/ui-verify/src/checks/roster.rs`, counted 2026-09-05), and the founding three still **fail against the old binary** and pass against this one — which is the property that makes the harness a measurement rather than a ritual. ★ Three were rewritten on 2026-08-19 and **none of the three has been run**: the Dimension-groups check now drives a *panel* and presses fold headings, and `markup_shapes` gained a phase asserting one field — `kind=Cloud` on `markup-commit` — because a build whose revision-cloud control armed `Polygon` would pass every other assertion in it, author a legal annotation, render it, and give the operator a cloud with no scallop. ★★★ **Re-measured 2026-09-05: this row said 42 and the roster holds 175 — it was one quarter of the truth.** And the sentence it opens with is now the row's most important content rather than a footnote about three checks: **twenty of the 175 were written in the last twelve hours and NONE of them has been run**, four more were modified and last ran against a different build, and there is no `evidence/` directory in this tree. The named list is in the twelve-hour section above. ⇒ *A registered check is a written intention; only a run is evidence.*
- ⛔ **Nineteen CI gate scripts, six of them self-testing** — ui-strings, theme-colours, strong-text, shipped-assets, string-gaps and engine-backlog each carry a self-test that plants its own violations and proves the gate catches them; file size and shell purity do not need one. With `fmt` and `clippy` the runner reports **29 checks, 0 skipped — and 28 pass**. ★★★ **Re-measured 2026-09-05: this row said eight gates and fourteen checks; it is nineteen and twenty-nine.** The one red **moved between two runs forty minutes apart**, because another track was writing this tree throughout: `check-file-size` on `panels/comments/mod.rs` (1,670 against R2's 1,500) at first, which then repaired itself when that track split the module, leaving `cargo clippy` red on an unused import in an **untracked** `panels/comments/tests.rs`. Neither is this revision's, and neither was repaired here. ⚠ **A gate suite described as "14 checks, 0 skipped" while it is running 29 and failing one is worse than no row at all**, because it is the row a reader consults instead of running the suite. The theme gate was **missing entirely** until 2026-08-13 and found five unmarked colours on its first run; `check-string-gaps` was added after a line continuation lost its backslash and shipped a ten-space hole into an operator-visible sentence, and it fired twice more the day it landed
- ✅ **`PDFCER_DIAG`** — canvas layout, pointer in three coordinate spaces, object counts, named UI rects

A fourth symptom he had not reached: the engine's **hit test** is built on those same boxes, so a press on the middle of a rotated letter misses it — a six-letter sweep selected five, an eight-letter 180° sweep selected nothing. The direction is now **measured** rather than guessed: a chain of three consecutive glyphs each exactly one advance from the last along a common non-horizontal direction is not available to horizontal text, because within a line every step is horizontal and the jumps *between* lines are never consecutive. ★★★ **A page with no rotated text never reaches any of it** — the direction census comes back empty and every branch is keyed on that, which is structural rather than incidental and is asserted against a real drawing sheet. 180° is covered as well as 90°/270° and reaches the defect through a *different* clause (the backward jump, because the advance is a positive magnitude), so a fix for the vertical case alone would have looked complete on his own file. The I-beam turns by generating its own rotated artwork — free, because this shell already supplies its cursor as a bitmap after the white-on-white report of 2026-08-19. **Filed with the engine**, where one fix would serve every caller; the shell-side recovery deletes when it lands |

### ★★★ The selector was invented, and that was the real defect — 2026-08-19

The operator, on week two:

> *"How do I select and edit end points on the canvas? How do I edit text when
> on the canvas? I get a box and the I cursor, but I can't type anything. How do
> I make new text when I click on the canvas and expect to edit there? Same
> problem. … Even though the old GUI was wonky and buggy it at least had these
> features somewhat operational within the first week. … **The selector should
> be predictable like other programs. It seems a lot of ideas are getting
> invented instead of just using the LLM weighting that would have produced the
> most common method expected.**"*

He is right on every count, and the last sentence is the finding of the project
so far. **Both features already worked. Reaching them was invented.**

| to do this | the ritual, before 2026-08-19 |
|---|---|
| type one character | Edit mode → the Edit tab → *Edit text* → click the run. **Four steps.** |
| move an end point | click the shape → double-click to descend to a subpath → double-click again to descend to a node — with **nothing drawn at any stage** saying a deeper rung existed |

The I-beam he describes was the text tool **sweeping** text for copying; the
tool that types was a different tool on a different tab. Neither ritual is
discoverable and neither resembles any program he has used.

**The fix is the one every editor settled on decades ago: the tool is the rung.**

| | | |
|---|---|---|
| ✅ | **V — Select** | click a shape, drag to move, drag paper to marquee |
| ✅ | **A — Points** | click a shape and **every point appears at once**; click one, Shift-click for more, drag to move; a point on a curve shows its Bézier handles |
| ✅ | **T — Text** | click text to edit it, click empty space to start new text; drag still sweeps for copying |
| ✅ | **H — Hand** | pan |

Bare letters, because that is the layout Illustrator, Photoshop, InDesign,
Figma, Affinity and Inkscape converged on, and this shell binds no bare letter
to anything else. Safe because `canvas::keys` gates every keystroke on
`text_edit_focused()` — `DEFECTS.md` D1's guard, the one place this project has
already been burned and fixed.

★ `SelectionState::click_direct` is the new entry point and is deliberately
**not** a flag inside `click`: the two branch differently at every decision.
The single line doing most of the work sets the **Part** rung on a click that
names a subpath, because `draw_anchors` draws from that rung up — so entering it
*is* showing them. ★ `textedit::click` was `resolve_run(click)?`, a bare `?`, so
a click on blank paper refused and did nothing; `NoRun` now becomes an origin
and one tool does both. Only `NoRun` falls through — an encrypted document is
still reported, because that says *this cannot be done here* rather than *there
is nothing here*.

★★ **Two driven checks assert the COUNT**, which is the whole feature: one key,
one click. A check that armed the tool from the ribbon and then clicked would
pass on the old build too — the old build could do all of that, it just took
four steps to get there.

★ **Drag-and-drop**, reported in the same breath, did nothing at all — nothing
read `dropped_files`. A PDF now opens, an image inserts through the same
placement window the ribbon opens, and every refusal says what to do instead.
**It made a working Insert-image button look broken**, because both were tried
in the same minute and only one of them said anything: a silent failure does not
just hide its own feature, it takes the credibility of everything tried beside
it.

### Phase 1 remainder — making selection mean everything it should

| | |
|---|---|
| ✅ | **★★★ A PRESS ON AN OBJECT SELECTS IT, AND THE SAME DRAG MOVES IT — 2026-08-27.** Operator: *"if I add an image I Expect to click on it to resize but dragging doesn't resize […] Editing should work like 99% of the graphics programs out there. It should be intuitive."* Selection happened on the **click**, which in egui means on *release*, so a press-and-drag on anything not already selected never selected anything: the press found no grip, fell through to `(None, None) => Marquee(Select)`, and the operator got a rubber band across the thing they were trying to drag — then a selection, on release, after the gesture they wanted had been spent. **Two gestures to do what every other editor does in one.** ★ The gesture machine's answer was never wrong; the selection had simply not caught up with the pointer. Selecting at press time makes the grip test on the very next statement find `Grip::Move` and produce `DragKind::Move` through the path that already existed and is already tested — **nine statements, not a new `DragKind` and an audit of every arm that reads one**, which is what `INTERACTION_GAP.md` had priced it at. It lives in `canvas::presspick` rather than in `canvas::pressing`, whose header opens with *"nothing here changes anything"*: a mutation in the module whose contract is that it only looks would have made a stated contract false. ★★★ **The first version broke rotation and a DRIVEN run caught it within the hour, with the unit suite green throughout.** `covers()` tested the selection's move box alone, and **the rotate handle sits outside that box** — so a press on it selected the object underneath and the rotate became a select-and-move. A working gesture aimed at the wrong verb, which is the failure this canvas has now produced four separate times and which never looks broken from a chair, because *something moves*. It asks `handles::grip_at` now, the same function the gesture machine asks |
| ✅ | **★★ The application opens in the mode you left it in — 2026-08-27.** It opened in **Read** on every launch, whatever the operator had been doing, because the mode was taken from the manifest's first entry and only the per-mode *layout* was persisted — so the next launch restored yesterday's Edit arrangement and put a Read mode selector on top of it. In Read a click on page content selects nothing at all, and nothing on screen says why. That is the invisible first failure of every session, and it is part of what *"I can't figure out how to click on objects to edit them"* was made of. The mode id now rides in the layout file beside the arrangement it belongs to — one `Option<String>` on `LayoutDocument`, `#[serde(default)]` like every field there, so an older file loads unchanged. ★ A stored id this manifest no longer declares falls back to the first mode rather than adopting nothing: a mode renamed in a customized manifest between two runs must not leave the shell with no ribbon and no way back. `egui-shell` learns nothing about what a mode *means* by holding an opaque id the host wrote and reads back, so R7 holds |
| ✅ | **★★ The status bar says what is selected, and how deep the click reached — 2026-08-26.** *"When I click on one of the objects all I get is the page selected"* was true and precisely reported, and **nothing on screen said so**: the outline was drawn round the page edge, which looks exactly like *"the page is selected"* — a state this program does not have. One line at the left of the bar now names the kind and the size, and appends `1 of 5 here` when the click had a stack under it. ★ It says **nothing** when nothing is selected, rather than *"Nothing selected"*: a bar that narrates the absence of a thing spends a permanent line on the commonest state in the program. ★★ The depth lives in `egui::Memory` and is **self-invalidating** — it records which object it was measured for, so a selection arriving by any of the four routes that are not a click (an edit re-resolving it, Escape, an Objects-panel row, a placement) gets `None` rather than a confident claim about a click that never happened. The first design had a `forget()` for callers to call, which is the shape of a guard that is correct until somebody adds a fifth route |
| ✅ | **★ `Alt`+click walks the stack — 2026-08-26.** `hit_test_all` returns every candidate under a point, front to back, and the pick called `.find()` on it: **anything underneath anything was unreachable**. Each `Alt`+click at the same point goes one deeper and wraps; moving more than 4 pt resets the cursor, so it is a gesture about *this* point rather than a mode. The count on the status line is what makes it discoverable — a cycling gesture with no readout is a gesture nobody finds |
| ✅ | **★ One selection, read by everything — 2026-08-26.** The Objects panel kept its own idea of what was selected, so clicking a row highlighted the row and left the canvas alone, and clicking the canvas left the row alone. Three parallel notions of *the thing I am working on* is what "the most convoluted system" was made of. A row click now raises `Action::SelectObject` and every surface reads `doc.selection`; the Properties panel does the same and no longer takes `PanelsState` at all |
| ✅ | **★★ Resize — 2026-08-19, and the blocker was a category error.** This row read *"`EditSession` has the whole `move_*` family and no scale verb at all"*, which is true and was never the question. **`move_nodes` takes a list of per-node deltas, and a scale IS a list of per-node deltas** — `d = (p − pivot) × (s − 1)`, computed once per anchor from the grip's opposite corner. The engine has had the whole capability since the day it shipped the verb; what was missing was arithmetic in this shell. ★ **Three defects, all found by DRIVING it and none reachable by a test.** (1) The drag became a **marquee**: `hovered_grip` read the *current* pointer, and by the frame `egui` calls a press a drag the pointer is ~20 pt from an 8 pt grip — it now reads `press_origin`, which is what the operator aimed at. (2) It scaled about the **dragged** corner, because `Grip::anchor` is where the handle is drawn and a pivot is the opposite one; `Grip::pivot` is now its own method with a test relating the two. (3) Commit returned `NoObjectModel` — `Resize` was missing from `needs_targets`, so the gesture ran on a canvas that had never been asked for a target provider. ★ **Six refusals, each a sentence rather than a silence** — nothing selected, more than one object, not a path, no object model, a degenerate box, no nodes — on the same status row the text tools use, because *"that grip was declined and you could not read why"* is the defect shape this project keeps finding. **Text runs and annotations decline**: scaling a run by moving its nodes would move the glyph origins and leave the type size untouched, which is a shear, not a resize. **Line weights do not scale and it says so**, once, off-canvas — `/LW` is not a node |
| 🔨 | **Object clipboard — cut, copy and paste land 2026-08-19**, scoped to **markup and comments**, on the operator's report *"also the standard copy/paste and I didn't try cut so possibly that one too aren't implemented"*. ★ The scope is what the engine can express, measured rather than assumed: `annot_author::spec_from_dict` reads a markup out and `add_markup` writes one back, so the round trip is complete for the things this operator actually copies between sheets — clouds, notes, stamps, callouts. `Ctrl+X`/`Ctrl+C`/`Ctrl+V`. ⚠ **This row claimed until 2026-08-21 that `Ctrl+C` over swept text still copied text "because `textsel` reads the key before the dispatcher sees it". That was false the day it was written and is corrected here rather than quietly deleted** — `textsel` read `key_pressed(Key::C)`, which `egui-winit` never produces, so sweeping text and pressing Ctrl+C had never once copied it and the object clipboard answered instead. Defect O18; fixed 2026-08-21 and now gated by `check-clipboard-chords.sh`. A same-page paste offsets 10 pt so the copy is visible; a **cross-page** paste lands in place, because a cloud copied to sheet 12 belongs where it was on sheet 1. The spec translation is an **exhaustive match**, so a tenth `MarkupSpec` in the engine fails to *compile* here — a paste that silently stopped offsetting for one annotation kind would be a defect nobody reports. Edit ▸ Clipboard is back (32 groups); its 2026-08-14 deletion note ended *"the next author of an object clipboard needs the word — which is right here"*, and that is literally what happened. **`paste_in_place` stays absent and is now argued rather than pending**: it would differ from `Ctrl+V` only on the one page an operator would never want it on. ⛔ **Page content still cannot be pasted**, and it was re-measured on 2026-08-19 rather than inherited: `grep "pub fn"` over `edit.rs` returns 157 verbs and **none of them inserts content** — no `paste`, no `duplicate`, no `insert_object`, no `add_path`. `insert_pages` inserts whole pages, which is a different subject. Copy is expressible (the decomposition can be read), so what is missing is exactly the half that writes. Filed as a dated citation rather than a verdict, per `NO_SURFACE.md` §1c |
| ✅ | **Restyling a placed markup — 2026-08-19.** Colour, line width and opacity, through `EditSession::set_markup_style`, on a **selected annotation**. ★ It landed in the **Properties panel** rather than the Format tab, and that is a considered placement rather than a shortcut: Properties is already the surface that appears when something is selected and already reads the annotation, so a second surface with the same trigger and the same subject would be two controls for one job. `MarkupStyle`'s fields are all `Option`, so **only what the operator touched is sent** and everything else is left alone — which matters because `StyleEdit::Clear` and "unchanged" are different edits and a struct of plain values cannot tell them apart. ★ **The reader's view of an annotation is not the author's** and this is where that bites: `annot::Annotation` carries `constant_alpha` but neither `/C` nor `/BS /W`, so the current colour and width are read back through `annot_author::spec_from_dict` — the author's view — and the opacity off the dict. CMYK stroke colours read back as **no swatch** rather than as a converted approximation, because showing a converted colour in a picker invites the operator to press Set and silently rewrite an ink they never touched |
| ✅ | **★ Editable geometry — 2026-08-19**, and it landed the day after the grips out of the same machinery: a position change is `Action::MoveSelection` and a size change is `resizing::action`, so this surface computes two factors and a delta and contributes **no geometry of its own**. A properties panel that reimplemented *"make it 40 points wide"* would be a second scale with a second pivot convention and a second answer about line weights, and the two would drift silently. ★ **Why it is needed even though the grips work**: a grip cannot express *exactly 40.0*, and a drawing is full of *"the same as the one above it"*. It is also the only route for a **small** object — at fit-page zoom on an A1 sheet a 6 mm symbol is four pixels across and its eight grips overlap each other — and the only route that does not require a sub-pixel drag, which `MODES_AND_PANELS.md` §7 asks for by name. ★★ **An Apply button, not live commits**: a scrubbed spinner going 40 → 120 would raise eighty undo entries, which is `MoveNodes`' own argument about looping the singular verbs wearing a nicer face. The draft is stamped `(page, object, **edit epoch**)` and the epoch is the member that is easy to leave out — without it, *type W=40, Ctrl+Z something unrelated, press Apply* divides 40 by a width that no longer exists and the object ends a third size the operator never typed. Bounds come from the object's **anchors**, the same set `move_nodes` moves, so the number shown and the number acted on cannot disagree; a flat object scales only the axis it has, because `h_new / 0` is how a NaN reaches a content stream |
| ⬜ | **Undo/redo** — the action funnel exists precisely so this is possible; the command log is not yet surfaced |

### Deep zoom — the maximum is yours to set, 2026-08-22

| | |
|---|---|
| ✅ | **★★★ On the status bar — click the zoom percentage.** A list of maximums from 800 % to a trillion, and **the shipped default is the highest**, on his instruction: *"Also set the default to be able to hit the maximum zoom."* A capability you have to find a preferences file to switch on is one most of its users never have |
| ✅ | **The readout was already the natural home** — the one control on the bar that is about zoom and did nothing when clicked. A label that turns out to be a button is this surface's existing idiom (the page box is a `TextEdit` that looks like a readout), and a separate control would have cost width on a bar whose height and right-hand cluster are both fixed |
| ✅ | **Also editable in `preferences.txt`** as `max_zoom_percent` |
| ✅ | **★★★ `max_zoom_percent`, up to a trillion percent.** The operator: *"I'm not concerned about the practicality of offering such a high zoom. it is up to the user to determine how much of a performance hit they want to take."* So there is **no guard, no warning and no preflight** — the setting's job is to be honest and to work. Defaults to 800 %, which is exactly what earlier versions allowed, so a fresh install is unchanged |
| ✅ | **It is also the dial for comparing the two rendering paths**, which is what he asked for. pdfcer rasterizes the whole page while it can and switches to the visible region only when it cannot. Set the maximum low and you never leave the whole-page path; set it high and you exercise the region path. **A threshold rather than a mode** — a threshold explains itself |
| ✅ | **★★ Panning keeps full detail, which is the thing he said not to lose.** *"I don't want the affect that other readers have where you always have to wait for detail to render after panning to a new area."* Whole-page rasterizing is what makes panning free — the texture already exists and the view moves over it — so the region path engages **only above the pixmap ceiling, where the whole-page path cannot work at all.** Nothing is taken away to pay for anything, and a test walks every rung of the shipped zoom ladder to assert it |
| ✅ | **The `+` button climbs past 800 %** by doubling once the named rungs run out: eleven presses from 800 % to a million, where a fixed increment would need thousands |
| ⚠ | **Two defects were caught by tests before they shipped, and both would have been silent.** A plain maximum broke the promise that the default changes nothing — a large page at a 1.5× display tops out at 690 %, not 800 %, so the setting would have raised that page's ceiling and asked for a raster the engine refuses. And the `+` button stalled at 800 % however high the setting went, which the request had predicted in its own words: *"the buttons stop working exactly where the setting starts mattering"* |
| ✅ | **★★★ It renders past the raster ceiling now — the failure he hit is gone.** *"I got a requested raster size 14580x18868 is empty or exceeds MAX_PIXMAP_EDGE when I got to 2382% zoom."* Above roughly 1000 % pdfcer rasterizes **only the visible rectangle**, whose size follows the window rather than the zoom — so the picture stays the same size however deep you go |
| ✅ | **Measured on his own `banana.pdf`: 2,118,335 %, page drawn, no failed renders** — against the 2,382 % that failed outright. A factor of 890 |
| ✅ | **Panning stays free below the crossover**, which is every zoom that could render whole-page before. Above it a pan costs a redraw, and the raster carries **half a screen of margin on every side**, so movement inside that costs nothing and the redraw comes at most once per half-screen of travel |
| ✅ | **★★★ A TRILLION PERCENT, with the page drawn** — the figure he named, measured on his own `banana.pdf` with no failed rasters. Against the 2,382 % that failed this morning, a factor of **420 million** |
| ✅ | **Three ceilings fell, and each was found by asking where precision was actually lost.** The raster limit went to region rendering. The 32-bit scroll offset went to a 64-bit anchor. The last one was not a type at all: the drawn rectangle was being derived from **the page's whole screen rect**, a value around 10^12 px where a 32-bit float's spacing is 131,072 px — while the thing being drawn is 1,400 px across |
| ✅ | **★★ So "keep the 32-bit strip or build a 64-bit one?" turned out to be neither.** Carrying a huge intermediate more precisely is worse than not computing it: the fix costs nothing, needs no second layout path, and removed the limit outright rather than moving it. **Not forming the large number beats widening it** |
| ✅ | **Panning below the crossover is untouched**, and the deep path is a hard branch rather than a re-parameterisation — this canvas has twice been broken by a change meant to affect only deep zoom |

#### Confirmed on screen, 2026-08-23

| | |
|---|---|
| ✅ | **★★★ The page is drawn at every decade from 114 % to 999,999,995,904 %**, and that is asserted from **pixels**, not from a trace. `ui-verify`'s `the_page_still_renders_at_every_decade_of_zoom` photographs the window at each tier and requires three things together: the **canvas region** is not near-uniform, the canvas reported `drawn ≥ 1`, and no render failed. Any two can hold while the third fails — a page drawing its state message is not uniform, a completed raster can still be placed off-screen, and a shell that stopped asking cannot report a failure |
| ✅ | **Measured on `banana.pdf`**: at 504,845 % one mitochondrion with the cell wall behind it; at 3,730,330 % mitochondria with cristae; at **41,120,084 %** the mtDNA nucleoid, mitoribosomes and ATP synthase heads along a crista — 10 nm features. At the ceiling the viewport spans 6 × 10⁻⁸ pt and the field is a flat fill, which is correct: it is smaller than an atom and there is nothing left to draw |
| ⚠ | **★★★ And that check found a defect nothing else could see.** From about 10⁷ % the region being requested stopped shrinking and floored at **fifty thousand times** the viewport, its raster painted 19 million points off-screen. `render::strategy::region_for` snaps the region origin by dividing an **absolute page coordinate** (~540) by a **relative extent** (2 × 10⁻⁸ pt at the ceiling) — a quotient of 2 × 10¹⁰, past `f32`'s last exact integer of 2²⁴, so `.floor()` was applied to a number that had lost its integer part. The whole region path is `f64` now |
| ★★★ | **It passed every existing check while doing it.** The raster was produced, `drawn=1` was traced, the position arithmetic was independently correct, and the *window* capture was not uniform because a window always contains a ribbon. **A canvas can satisfy every trace-based assertion while showing blank paper**; only a screenshot aimed at the canvas itself can tell |
| ✅ | **The `−` button is the inverse of `+` again** — it halves above the named rungs instead of returning to 800 % from anywhere above it. Pinned as a **round trip** rather than against fixed numbers, because the property is reversibility |
| ✅ | **A zoom no longer discards where you panned to.** At a fit-page zoom the anchor solve was clamped to `display − viewport`, which is zero or negative when the page is no larger than the window — so every zoom forced the view back to centre. The clamp moved to the one place that knows the pasteboard-extended range |
| ✅ | **The tier hand-over at ~2,118,000 % holds the view.** Three faults met there: the `f64` anchor was seeded from the *previous* frame's scroll offset; nothing ever called `DeepAnchor::zoomed_about`, so a zoom held nothing under the cursor; and the scroll area kept its old offset for one frame, placing the page at roughly **twice** the intended distance. Measured at the crossing: the page belonged 6,676,376 px left of the viewport and was drawn 12,940,650 px left — the difference being the stale offset to four significant figures |
| ✅ | **The readout survives its own range.** It returned a `u32`, and `as u32` saturates, so past 42,949,672 % the status bar showed **4294967295 %** — `u32::MAX` presented as a measurement. It reads 999,999,995,904 % at the top now, which is honest rather than rounded: the zoom is an `f32` and that is the nearest value it holds |
| ★★ | **The recurring shape of all of these**: a limit lifted in one place while a narrower type downstream keeps enforcing the old one, silently. The raster ceiling, the scroll offset, the region snap, the percentage readout — four instances, one pattern |
| ⬜ | **What is out of reach, stated rather than implied**: anything inside an atom. A carbon nucleus 200 px across needs 7 × 10¹⁴ %, about 700× past the ceiling; a proton needs 4 × 10¹⁵ %. At the maximum zoom one screen point is 35 femtometres. **Atoms are comfortable; their contents are not** |
| ✅ | **★★★ An atom at 1:1, magnified until it is legible — 2026-08-23.** A benzene molecule drawn at true scale (aromatic C–C 0.140 nm = 3.9685 × 10⁻⁷ pt, carbon van der Waals radius 0.170 nm) is on screen with bonds, hydrogens and van der Waals spheres at **36,919,476,224 %**, driven through the release binary and photographed |
| ⚠ | **★★ And this row replaces one that was wrong for a day.** On 2026-08-22 the same molecule rendered near the page origin and was **blank** at page (540, 560) — the renderer's path coordinates were `f32`, whose step there is 21.5 nm against an atom's 0.34 nm. It was written up for the engine as a limit of the design. pdfcer had already removed it under a different request (`1d6db9e`, *"Deep zoom holds its CONTENT now, not only its viewport"*), and re-running the probe unchanged against the new engine renders it perfectly. **A measured limit is a fact about a revision, not about a design** |

### Free navigation — the pasteboard, 2026-08-21

| | |
|---|---|
| ✅ | **★★★ Any corner of the page can be brought to any point of the screen.** The operator: *"I should also be able to move the view of the corner of the page to the center of the screen, or even all the way vertically to the opposite corner if I want to."* One whole viewport of empty space on every side — **a fraction of the viewport, never a number of points**, so it keeps satisfying that sentence when the window is resized. His two clauses set the size exactly: half a viewport reaches the centre, a whole one reaches the opposite corner |
| ✅ | **It closed a limitation this shell had recorded and left**, and the note was sitting in `canvas::geometry`'s own header: *"This clamps to the PAGE… The operator asked to 'navigate beyond the page's edges', which needs reserved space around the page rather than a different clamp… That is a UX call, and this function is the one place it would need to change."* He made the call; it turned out to need three more places as well |
| ✅ | **★ It also fixed the rotate handle at the top of a sheet** — `O22`. The handle sits 20 pt above the selection box, so an object flush with the top of the view had its handle drawn nine pixels outside the canvas: clipped away, and a press landing on the ribbon. With a pasteboard there is always somewhere to scroll to, and the driven check no longer reports it off-canvas |
| ⚠ | **It took four attempts, and the first three were misdiagnosed.** The cause was one missing coordinate conversion: the rect deciding *which pages are laid out at all* was built from a scroll offset measured in the content's space and then intersected with the strip's. Before a pasteboard those were the same rectangle; with one they differ by exactly the margin, so the rect landed past the end of the strip and **the canvas drew nothing at all** |
| ★★★ | **The application had been saying so the whole time.** Every failing trace carried `canvas-unavailable reason=nothing-visible`, which states the cause exactly. It was never grepped for, because each search was for the *symptom* — no pointer input, stalled frames, a page rect that looked right — and those sent the diagnosis to the arithmetic, then the allocation, then the seeding, then the offset magnitude. All four were innocent. **Grep the trace for what the program reports about itself before grepping it for what you are seeing** |
| ✅ | **A driven guard came out of it**: `scrolling_far_keeps_the_canvas_its_pointer_input` wheels the view to 1,600 pt and asserts the canvas still receives the pointer. It exists because several hours were spent suspecting that a large scroll offset breaks input — it does not, and nothing had ever asserted so |
| ⬜ | **Objects off the page are still not reachable.** `O23`'s second half. The engine already keeps them — the decomposer applies no page-box culling and the hit test is pure geometry — and the block is on the shell's side: the space around the pages senses hover and refuses clicks, so off-page content can be pointed at and never pressed |

### Phase 3 — viewer conventions

| | |
|---|---|
| ✅ | **Hand tool**, space-to-pan — Space is read by the canvas itself, so it needs no keymap entry and cannot be unbound by accident |
| ✅ | **Cursor-anchored discrete zoom** — the rule was decided once, in `canvas::zoom::anchor_point`, and **all five** paths route through it: wheel, the three commands, and the framing verbs. Armed from one statement in the frame, not from the six sites that raise the actions |
| ✅ | **Zoom to selection** — gated on `selection.bounds`, which is *not* `selection.any`: an identity can outlive the box it described, and framing nothing is a jump to the origin that looks like a bug |
| ✅ | **Marquee zoom to region** — one rubber band shared with marquee-select, branched only at release; a zoom marquee never touches the selection and decomposes nothing |
| ✅ | **Recent files** — `recent.txt` beside `layout.ron`, capped at 10, move-to-front; missing entries dropped at display time only, throttled so a dead network path cannot block the UI thread |
| ✅ | **Rulers** — in **points**, or in the document's own unit at its own scale when its dimension sidecar carries one, through the *same* `pdfcer-core` `format_measurement` a dimension label uses, so a ruler and a dimension across one span agree to the digit. The gutters take a **constant** bite out of the viewport (R128): switching them on costs exactly one re-fit, and the trace shows it. Zero is the current page's own top-left, which is the frame the pointer readout already reports |
| ✅ | **Grid** — **per page, in page space**, clipped to the sheet, so it scrolls with the drawing and the row gaps between sheets carry none. Every numbered ruler tick has a grid line under it, because both come from one 1-2-5 ladder. Its pitch is bounded on the **drawn** step, which a measurement of the running binary corrected: bounding the labelled step put a line every 1.4 screen pixels on the benchmark A3 sheet, and neither a screenshot nor the suite saw it |
| ✅ | **Guides** — belong to a **page**, dragged out of a ruler, moved on the canvas, deleted by dragging off it or by a double-click. A guide's catch band is registered after every page widget, so grabbing one cannot also rubber-band a selection. Persisted per document in `guides.txt` — a fourth store beside `layout.ron`, `recent.txt` and `page-display.txt` — and a document that has remembered guides opens showing them, because the presence of the work is the preference. **Escape abandons a guide drag and abandons exactly that**: it is the fourth claimant on Escape and it sits above an armed region zoom, because a drag following the pointer this frame is the more transient of the two. Nothing is rolled back — a drag holds a *proposed* position and only release raises `SetGuides` |
| ✅ | **A new panel reaches an operator who upgrades** — a layout records which panels *existed* when it was written, so a genuinely new one appears while one closed on purpose stays closed. Verified against a real layout file written before the Pages panel existed |
| ✅ | **Thumbnail grid** — one tile per frame, on-screen only, current page first, 64-texture cap, and a **hard stop past 400 ms** naming the page and its cost. Measured: 918 ms for the benchmark CAD sheet, 238 ms for one sheet of a 36-sheet set against 58–72 ms for its siblings |
| ✅ | **Worded decline** — 2026-08-14. `ZoomOutcome::NoBounds`/`NoCanvas` reach the operator instead of the floor: `app/dispatch.rs` carries the outcome into a new `app/status/decline.rs` and the status bar's left half says so. **Same surface, different store** — the bar, `disclosure_line` and the R128 fixed row are reused; the edit-epoch key is not. A decline changes no document, so the epoch never moves and an epoch-keyed sentence would still read *Nothing to zoom to* forty gestures later; and a decline must be **repeatable**, which an epoch key cannot express because nothing changed between the two presses. The precedent is `page_box`'s clamp note: retired by **the operator's next act** (`decline::retire`, at the dispatcher's one choke point) and additionally filtered while its reason still holds — asked through the *same* predicates that produced it, because `doc.page_texture.is_some()` is a different question by one frame and would have hidden a genuine `NoCanvas`. The wording diverges too — `⊗ Nothing to zoom to`, never `⚑ About your last edit` — because one slot for both speech acts would make a completed gesture and a refused one indistinguishable in the same place, which is worse than the trace-only state it replaced. **The ceiling-clamped region zoom is deliberately NOT worded**: it is a partial grant, the region is still framed centred, and the clamped scale is already stated by the zoom readout on the same frame — so a sentence would word a non-event. `Declined` is a narrower type than `ZoomOutcome` and *cannot represent a grant*, so no later edit can word it by accident. `app/status.rs` split a third time under R2, taking out the **narrator** (`app/status/notes.rs`) — the seam its own header already draws between narration and a fact about the operator's document |
| ✅ | **…and how it is reached was decided rather than left open** — 2026-08-14, under the operator's *match Inkscape/Acrobat/SolidWorks* instruction. **SolidWorks and Acrobat both reach zoom-to-selection by right-click**; only Inkscape binds a key (bare `3`, in its `1`–`6` zoom family). Two of three said menu, so `view.zoom_selection` joined the `canvas.object` context menu — least-destructive first, above Delete. **No chord was invented**: Inkscape's family is *unmodified digits*, this shell's manifest chords are `Ctrl`-modified by construction, and `Ctrl+1/2/3` are the mode selector `MODES_AND_PANELS.md` specifies, so transposing `3` onto `Ctrl+4` would match neither convention nor anyone's muscle memory. **Consequence, accepted deliberately:** a menu on an object implies a selection, so the decline sentence stays reachable only by the race in which bounds evaporate between the frame that drew the enabled control and the frame that applied it. That is the case an operator finds most confusing, so a race-only safety net is the right shape — the sentence is correct to be rare |

### Phase 4 — page display modes

| | |
|---|---|
| ✅ | **Continuous scroll**, and **Read defaults to it**. Single page stays the default everywhere else and is unchanged — the strip lays out one row for it, so its size, scroll range and centring margin are the same arithmetic they were, asserted as an equality rather than as an intention |
| ✅ | **Facing** and **facing-continuous**, cover page alone. Fit and the raster ceiling became per-*row*: a spread fits as a spread, and the ceiling is a minimum over the row's pages because a spread is two pixmaps rather than one |
| ✅ | **A continuous fit does not depend on where you scrolled to** — under a continuous mode the fit is taken over the document's tightest row, per axis; under Single and Facing it is the current row, which the operator chose. `page_index` is *derived* from the scroll in a continuous mode, so fitting the current row made the zoom depend on the scroll and the scroll depend on the zoom: a mixed-size document oscillated between `zoom=1.4773` and `zoom=0.9559` for as long as the wheel moved. `PROJECT_PLAN.md` R128 in a new place |
| ✅ | Per-document persistence of the choice, so a sheet set does not inherit a report's setting. A third store, `page-display.txt`, beside `layout.ron` and `recent.txt` — `recent.rs`'s own header had already refused to become one |
| ✅ | **Only visible pages rasterize**, one at a time, nearest the viewport centre first, bounded by a texel budget. Measured: a scroll across four pages of a 20-page document whose pages cost ~460 ms each spent **four** renders, none cancelled and none evicted |
| ✅ | **An undrawn page says so** — its real boundary, a fill that is visibly not paper, and a sentence naming the page and its state, centred in the part of the page on screen. Both the fill and the placement were corrected from a screenshot of a driven scroll after every test was green |

### Phase 5 — text editing

| | |
|---|---|
| ✅ | **The tool itself** — `CanvasTool::TextEdit(TextEditKind)`, click to place a caret, type, Enter or a click elsewhere commits, Escape abandons, a mode change disarms *and* abandons. Gated on `edit_content`, so Read cannot reach it. It takes a click and no drag, the shape `press_kind` already uses for measure and vertex markup; `textsel::takes_the_press` is untouched because it asks `is_text()`, false for this variant **by construction** rather than by an added exclusion |
| ✅ | **Alignment and rotation fixes** — the two cases D4b called genuinely wrong. One pure `choose(text_matrix, ctm, alignment)` at the single commit site: rotation is rung 1 and outranks alignment, non-left alignment is rung 2, both selecting `FollowerDisposition::Pin` — which the engine has always had *"for a justified / right-aligned tail that must not move"* and which the old shell never once passed, sending `EditOptions::default()` from its only call site. `Pin` is **correct** under rotation rather than merely less wrong: it writes no follower `Tm` at all and its compensating `TJ` acts along the rotated baseline, so this refuses rotation less harshly than `reflow_apply` does, deliberately. **Proved against the bytes**: a new right-aligned/rotated fixture — which existed in neither repository — edited in the real binary, saved as a copy, re-opened **in a second process**, and the untouched line's `412.64 668.00 Tm` found verbatim *in the appended revision only*, since §7.5.6 leaves the original in the base revision and a whole-file scan would pass for every build. Planting `EditOptions::default()` made the check fail; neutralising that assertion made it fail again at the byte scan |
| ✅ | **★★★ TEXT INSIDE A DRAWING BLOCK IS EDITABLE — 2026-08-20, and on a CAD sheet it is 99 % of the text there is.** Click a label, a title-block field or a *pdf dimension* callout, get a caret, retype it. That text lives in a **form XObject** — a block the drawing program placed — and until this evening pdfcer could read it and not write it, which is why every one of the operator's *"text editing doesn't work"* reports was correct while every driven check was green: **the checks drove fixtures this repository authored, and this repository authors page-stream text.** Measured on his benchmark drawing: **1,696 show operators of real drawing text inside the block, against 3,007 metadata glyphs in the page's own stream.** His own estimate — *"I need that editing capability as it is 99% of the text I will want to edit"* — is what made the engine escalate `Pass 119.0` ahead of the transform verbs. ★★ **The shell's cost was ONE DELETED LINE, and that is the whole return on a decision made two days earlier.** When the guard was written, the request filed with it said: *"my shell encodes a fact about your surgery's internals. The day form editing lands, my guard silently keeps refusing until I notice and delete it — a workaround that outlives its bug."* So the engine published `TextRun::editability()` and this shell asked pdfcer instead of modelling the answer; when the capability landed the predicate started answering `Editable`, a `#[deprecated]` attribute pointed at the single arm to remove, and that was the job. A hand-rolled guard would have gone on refusing 99 % of his text with every internal check green. ★ **One thing added beyond deleting it: the commit now NAMES the buffer it measured.** `EditTarget::Auto` is the engine's default and searches the page stream first — and that stream holds 3,007 single-character show operators, so offering a form-relative byte pin to it is a dense field of near-misses whose worst outcome is an edit that succeeds on the wrong glyph with no error anywhere. `plan` takes the target from the same provenance record it takes the pin from, because the two fields are one fact and reading half of it is the defect this shell shipped in the first place |
| ✅ | **★ Shared content is disclosed, in the engine's own words.** A drawing program may place **one** copy of a title block and paint it on six sheets — ISO 32000-1 §8.10.1 names a CAD system's standard component as the illustration — and **no clause in either edition binds a form to a page**. So editing text inside a shared one changes every sheet it appears on, and pdfcer cannot make that untrue: there is exactly one stream holding those glyphs. The status row therefore carries the engine's `"SHARED CONTENT: this text is drawn from shared content that appears in N place(s) on M page(s)"`, verbatim — re-wording it here would be a second account of one fact, free to drift — and it is **absent on the ordinary single-paint case**, because a warning that fires every time is one nobody reads. **Nothing is drawn into the page view**: R8b rule 4 as narrowed by the engine's decision 059, and the operator's own finding that *"the nagging and red flagging in the original GUI made for a lot of extra bugs in the visibility when editing."* Known gap, named rather than implied: the warning arrives **after** the edit, not at the caret.
