Telling him at the caret means asking the document how many places paint that block — one walk of the whole file, cheap once and not per click on text — so it needs a document-scoped cache that does not exist yet. Undo is one press |
| ✅ | **★★ One four-character edit used to move 1,676 labels, and the fix arrived the same evening.** Not a shell defect and worth the row anyway, because the shell is what would have shipped it. Reflow shifts *the rest of the line* by the advance delta, and the engine's walk ran forward through every absolute `Tm` until a `Td`/`TD`/`T*` boundary — **a CAD stream positions everything with `Tm` and never emits `Td`**, so there was no boundary and one edit slid the rest of the drawing sideways: `followers_repositioned=1676`, **34,059 changed pixels across the whole sheet**, against 42 pixels inside one label after the fix. A build published at 20:09 linked the revision without it and was **retracted within the hour**. Two things came back with it: `followers_repositioned` is now on this shell's diagnostic channel at the engine's request — *"if you show one number from an edit report beyond the disclosures, make it that one"* — and a driven check fails above 64, which is a fact about the build rather than about any document. ★ And the **fixture had to grow a fourth block**: the fix left the other three unable to exhibit the hazard at all, so both falsifying assertions in `proof.rs` went quiet — and **a quiet falsifier is a test that has stopped measuring**, which would have let the two `Pin` assertions beside them pass against a build that pinned nothing |
| ⛔ | **Live re-layout while typing** — blocked on the engine. Measured per keystroke: **0.49 ms** on a 3-line fixture, **102.77 ms** on a SolidWorks sheet, **356 ms** on an A3 benchmark. The arithmetic is not the cost: `plan_edit` already computes `advance_delta` before any write, but it is `pub(crate)`, so every public route performs a full incremental save. **Request: `measure_edit(&Document, &EditRequest) -> Result<f64, _>`, or make `plan_edit` public.** Debouncing was rejected rather than overlooked — a re-layout arriving 150 ms after you stop typing is a second surprise. The ghost text is gone regardless: what is drawn now is a caret and an extent bracket, which promise nothing about widths |
| ✅ | **★★ Re-wrapping a paragraph is now a command — 2026-08-28, on *"I think the paragraph reflow was implemented ages ago in the pdfcer core, so we should have that option too."*** He was right; it shipped in the engine and no control in this shell had ever raised it. **Edit ▸ Reflow paragraph**, and a **right-click inside the text you are editing**, which is a third canvas menu the build did not have — the other two are keyed on a selected object and on blank paper, and a caret is neither, so a paragraph command had no canvas route at all until one was added. ★★★ **It is not like the other text verbs and the shell says so rather than hiding it.** `reflow_block` is planned against the document **as you opened it** — it re-extracts the page to get position information the editing buffer does not carry — so it refuses a file this session has already changed. One typed character is enough. The shell asks that question *before* the attempt and answers with the remedy, in words: *"Save this file and open it again, then reflow."* ⇒ A refusal that names a cause and no remedy is a sentence that leaves somebody trying things, and this one has a specific, cheap remedy. ★ Three more refusals, each saying a different thing, because a paragraph command whose operand is the caret has three ways to find no operand: no caret at all, a caret placing *new* text, and a caret on a run that is not part of a paragraph — the last being the common case on a CAD title block, where there genuinely is no paragraph and the message says that rather than reporting a failure |
| ✅ | **★★ Text on a line shared with other runs — 2026-08-19, and this row WAS the operator's bug.** *"Text editing on canvas still doesn't work"*, reported for the third time. It worked: two driven checks pressed a caret into a run, typed, and asserted `edit-text` — **against fixtures this repository generates**, a 924-byte one-run page and a blank sheet. On his own drawings almost every click was refused, because a CAD title block is a row of short `Tj` runs on one baseline and the guard declined *any* line whose extracted range spanned more than one run. ★ **The refusal was guarding the wrong thing.** Editing one run on a shared line is not multi-run editing — it is one edit whose **neighbours must not reflow**, and `FollowerDisposition::Pin` is exactly that and had been available the whole time. The guard now feeds a new `Reason::SharesTheLine` into the disposition ladder at rung 2, so the neighbours are pinned instead of the edit being declined. Genuine multi-run editing — one gesture spanning four runs — is still one edit per run and still needs an engine request; **that is a much smaller claim than the one this row made for weeks.** ★ **The lesson is about the fixtures, not the guard**: a check that drives a document this project authored tests the shape this project imagined, and the operator's documents are the only ones with the shape that broke. `--doc-point` now aims a driven check at a real drawing |
| ✅ | **★ New text gets a font, a size and a colour — 2026-08-19.** Nothing was blocking it: `AddTextRequest` has carried `face`, `size` and `color` since it shipped and the apply arm overrode none of them. ★ **A tool option, not a Format property**, and the reasoning is the split `canvas::markup::pen` already makes — Format describes *the placed thing*, this describes *what the next thing will look like* — so it lives in the **Tool panel**, which is the panel whose stated subject is the next gesture. All fourteen bundled faces, grouped by family the way Acrobat lists them rather than alphabetically (which interleaves four Helveticas with four Times), 4–144 pt, and a colour picker. **The default is the engine's default** — Helvetica, 12 pt, black, asserted by a test — so the release that added the controls changed nothing for anybody who does not touch them. Black resolves to `NewTextColor::Black` rather than `Rgb(0,0,0)`: one operator and one byte instead of four, for the same ink. Parked in `egui::Memory` beside the armed tool, so a panel reaches it through `ui.ctx()` with no plumbing, and **application-scoped** — 8 pt Courier chosen for a note is still 8 pt Courier in the next document. Deliberately absent: a **donor font** (that is a file picker, a subset and a licensing disclosure — two features in one combo) and **alignment/wrap** (both describe a paragraph, and this gesture places a caret) |
| ✅ | **★★ NEW TEXT CAN BE MULTI-LINE — 2026-08-21, on the operator's *"I should be able to make it multi line."*** Arm **Edit ▸ Add text** and **drag a rectangle** instead of clicking: Enter starts a new line, text wraps at the right edge by itself, **Ctrl+Enter** commits. A plain click still places one line at a point and Enter there still commits — two gestures, two behaviours, decided by whether you dragged. ★ **It needs a rectangle and that is not a design choice: a PDF has no paragraph.** Every visual line is a separate instruction at its own absolute position, so something must decide where the second line starts, and the only thing that can is a width to wrap against — which is exactly what `AddTextRequest::with_box` takes. ★★ **The drag belongs to the add-text tool, not to the text tool**, and two unit tests decided that rather than taste: offered from the sweep tool's rung it took the text sweep away in Edit, which a shipped check depends on. Two features claiming one drag is a choice somebody has to make, and taking a shipped gesture away to make room is the wrong way to make it. ★ **And the driven check earned its keep on its first run**: the drag opened the box, Enter arrived, the right branch ran — and the newline was thrown away one function deeper by a filter that strips control characters from *typed* text. Its comment argued, correctly, that a control character has no meaning in a show string; true of typing, not true of a paragraph break. The **fifth** carefully-argued restriction in two days to go false the week it was written. The filter stays and the newline got its own door, because relaxing it would have let a stray tab or carriage return in from a paste. Driven: `add-text page=0 n=2 … wrapped to 2 line(s) at 199.7pt`. Still open and named: turning text **already on the page** into two lines (that is a reflow), and a box's alignment and post-hoc size |
| ✅ | **★★ THE NAVIGATION KEYS WALK THE PAGE, NOT THE FRAGMENT — 2026-08-21.** Operator: *"there was an acrobat feature in the original pdfcer-gui that attempted to reassemble individual lines into paragraphs and the cursor would move to the next block of text using the navigation keys."* ↓/↑ move to the line below/above **including into the next block of text**; End/Home reach the ends of the line **you can see**, however many separate instructions drew it — which on a CAD title-block row is four or five. ★ **This is SALVAGE and the salvaged part was four lines**: the old shell asked `caret_up`, `caret_down` and `line_range_at`'s two ends, and that was its entire contribution. **The reassembly was always the engine's** — `recognize` groups instructions into lines and lines into blocks by column band, and `caret_up` walks *lines*, so a caret on the last line of a paragraph steps into the next without the shell knowing what a paragraph is. This shell had not been asking, and at the time that was right: its caret is a position inside **one** run and a single run has no line above it. What changed is not the caret — the *page* is now the thing being navigated. ★★ **The draft is committed on the way out**, because a caret that walks out of a run with unsaved keystrokes in it silently discards them, which is this project's defining defect class; `commit_into` writes nothing when the text is unchanged, so reading with the arrow keys puts nothing on the undo stack. ★★★ **The first live run failed and the failure was not a defect** — and the trace could not say so. No movement at all, with two possible causes that look identical from outside: the keys eaten on the way in (a bug), or the model finding nothing above or below (a fact about *where the click landed*, because the engine never crosses a column band and a lone label has nothing stacked over it). The fix was a second trace line for the **nowhere** outcome, so the check now skips on the second cause and accuses only on the first. *A trace that can only report success cannot tell a broken build from an unlucky fixture.* Driven on his own drawing: `text-caret-step dir=Down from_run=232 to_run=240`, `text-caret-line end=true from_run=232 to_run=236`. Deliberately absent: Up/Down inside a text **box** (its lines are the shell's wrap, and the page model would throw the caret across the sheet), a remembered desired column, and any drawing of the recognised block |
| ✅ | **★★ THERE IS A SELECTION INSIDE A TEXT DRAFT — 2026-08-21**, closing the conventions sweep's item 11: *"no Shift+arrow, no Ctrl+A, no drag-select."* Shift+arrows, Shift+Home/End and Ctrl+A select; typing replaces the selection, Backspace and Delete remove it and nothing else, and **any movement without Shift drops it** — rule 4, whose absence leaves a highlight on screen after the caret has walked out of it so the next keystroke deletes text the operator is no longer looking at. The highlight is painted **under** the galley that was actually drawn, one rect per row, merged along rows, so it cannot disagree with the characters it is under; that is the same one-derivation rule this module deleted `caret_x` to establish. ★ It is a **cursor**, not content marking — R8b rule 4 permits it precisely because it shows what the *next keystroke* will replace and is gone the moment the draft commits. ★★★ **And the modifier nearly did not arrive.** The first driven run moved the caret and selected nothing; both sources traced on the same frame, three presses, Shift held throughout: `ev=Modifiers::NONE frame=Modifiers { shift: true }`. `egui-winit` stamps a `Key` event with the modifier state it holds at translation time and updates that state from a separate `ModifiersChanged`; key-before-modifiers in one batch gives an empty event and a correct frame. The shell asks **both**, and the justification is an asymmetry rather than a preference — `app::keyboard` is right that a **chord** must use the event's own modifiers, because `Ctrl+S` read from a stale frame saves a document nobody asked to save; a selection modifier is not a command, and read-as-held-when-released costs one character while read-as-absent-when-held **destroys the range being built**. Driven on the operator's own drawing and changing nothing: `text-select from=0 to=2 n=2`, then `text-select none caret=3`. ★ The check reads a trace rather than typing over the selection, because proving a selection by making an edit puts a real change on his document to observe something already observable. ★ And its first version accused the build of the document's arithmetic — it budgeted three presses from the run that was **clicked**, but Home reaches the start of the page's *line* and stepped the caret from an 18-character part number into the 2-character cell beside it. R2: `textedit/mod.rs` hit 1,571 lines, so the keystroke contract moved to `textedit/keys.rs`. ⬜ Still open: **drag-select and double-click-a-word** — the draft is an editor box in screen space and hit-testing into it needs that galley published where the click ladder can reach it |
| ✅ | **★★★ A DIALOG IS OWNED BY THE WINDOW IT BELONGS TO — 2026-08-21, closing G3, which had been filed as impossible.** *"`eframe 0.35`'s window builder has thirty options and none of them is an owner"* — true, and `egui-winit` never passes down the parent relationship egui itself tracks in `viewport_parents`, and there is no way to get the child window's handle back out either. So a dialog could fall behind the application window, which is *the* classic Windows bug: a program that looks frozen because the thing waiting for an answer is behind the thing it is blocking. **Ownership is not a request** — an owned window is by definition above its owner and keeps activation as a property of the relationship rather than as a call that can be declined, which is what every native dialog on the machine already uses and why none of them has this problem. The child is found by title among **this process's** windows and its owner set directly; confirmed on every dialog that opens (`dialog-owned owned=true`). ★★ **The unsafe is QUARANTINED in a crate of its own**, `native-window`, and that is the load-bearing part rather than tidiness: `pdfcer-gui` and `egui-shell` both carry `#![forbid(unsafe_code)]`, `forbid` cannot be relaxed from the inside, and that claim is worth more than the convenience of a module. One file, four declarations, one public function, no state, and a no-op for every non-Windows target — **deleted rather than ported** when a toolkit grows an owner option. It names no PDF concept, so it is R7-clean if the dialog host ever moves into the shell. ★ It also carries the session's sharpest lesson: it was reached while chasing a *"the note dialog does not take the keyboard"* defect that **did not exist** — the check was clicking Accept through the main window's coordinates while the dialog had its own — and along the way a focus constant was raised twice, each raise apparently helping because focus really did follow the requests. **A measurement that moves when you turn a knob is not proof the knob is the subject.** The tuning was undone; the ownership was kept, because it is right on its own terms |

### Phase 1 — the transform verb, and what it retired

| | |
|---|---|
| ✅ | **★★★ Move, resize and rotate ANY object — 2026-08-20, and it closes the request the operator made three times.** *"there was no way to reposition, resize, or rotate it on the screen. Can I please please please have that too?"* and *"can I please please please have the capability to move the text after?"* The whole shell side had been built and waiting since S4 — selection, eight grips, ghost preview — and what was missing was a verb: `move_objects` rewrites numeric **operands**, and a text run and an image carry no coordinate operands at all, which is why it is path-only by name. `transform_objects` (`Pass 113.0`) wraps each object's operator run in `q <cm> … Q`, which **never looks at an operand**, so kind-agnosticism is a property of the mechanism rather than a match arm somebody has to remember to extend. ★ **Three refusals were deleted, and each was true when it was written**: *"pdfcer cannot resize text or pictures"*, *"pdfcer resizes one shape at a time"* (the verb takes a **slice**, so several objects scale about one pivot as one undo entry), and *"this shape has no corners to move"*. Kept as comments where the strings were, because **a refusal is a claim with a date on it** — one carried *"is not built yet"* and was right for a day, the other carried an architectural reason that stopped being true the moment a different verb existed. ★★ **The matrix is PAGE space and nothing else**: `cm` composes into the CTM in force at that point in the stream, so the engine emits `X = CTM × M × CTM⁻¹` per object from that object's own captured CTM. A caller that pre-multiplied "helpfully" would be right only where an object's CTM is the identity and **silently wrong at every scale the producer left in force** — the object landing twice as far as the pointer went, with nothing erroring. **Driven on the operator's own drawing:** `resize_scales_a_shape` and `geometry_fields_resize_a_shape` both PASS through `transform_objects` |
| ✅ | **★ The move drag FORKS rather than switching**, and it is a decision about the file rather than the API. Both verbs move things and both are one undo entry, so the tidy answer is to route everything through the transform — and it would be worse: `move_objects` rewrites coordinates in place and adds nothing, while a transform adds a `q`, a `cm` and a `Q` **per object per gesture**. On a drawing that is nudged dozens of times the wrapping accumulates in a file somebody then sends on. So the lighter verb where it can express the gesture, the general one where it cannot — on the *same* predicate that used to refuse the general case, which is what makes it a fork rather than a second notion of "is this a path" |
| ✅ | **★★ A rotate handle — 2026-08-20, and it is the third word of *"reposition, resize, or rotate"*.** A circle above the selection box on a short stem, which is PowerPoint's, Illustrator's, Figma's, Inkscape's, Visio's and Konva's arrangement — and a **circle** rather than a ninth square, because every square on this canvas resizes and a shape that resized in one place and rotated in another would be a private convention to learn. Drag it and the selection turns about its own centre, previewed as a quadrilateral rather than as a rotated bounding box (which would show a shape that grew as you turned it). **Shift steps by 15°**, which divides 90 *and* 360, so the four right angles and the four diagonals are all reachable. ★★ **A rotation is not a resize with different arithmetic**: it is measured from the CENTRE rather than an opposite corner, it reads a bearing rather than a displacement, and — the row that decides the module boundary — **the pointer's distance from the centre must mean nothing at all**, because an operator swinging a long arc for precision is doing what the gesture invites. There is a test whose only job is that property. ★★★ **Three links would each have produced a working gesture aimed at the wrong verb**, and every one looks deliberate from a chair: `Grip::is_resize` was `self != Move` (the handle becomes the ninth *resize* grip), the routing arm below it was a `Some(_)` wildcard meaning "the body" (the handle *moves* the selection), and `needs_targets` had to grow `Rotate` (the fourth time that list has been the defect). ★ **The negation happens exactly once**: screen y is down so a clockwise drag reads +90, PDF user space is y-up so the commit negates at the page crossing and the ghost is drawn from the un-negated angle — the only thing that can differ between preview and commit is that sign. **Driven:** `rotate-commit deg=-90.22 px=244.22 py=364.54 objects=1` |
| ⬜ | **The transform preflight.** `transform_preview` is `&self` and shares one body with the verb, so `preview(..).is_ok()` *is* the predicate — and the engine's guidance is explicit: an object whose own CTM is singular means **do not offer a handle**. pdfcer offers one and the operator finds out by dragging. Not built because the preview **decomposes the page** — ~4 s in a debug build on the 129,758-object benchmark — so it needs a cache keyed on `(page, epoch, selection)`. ★ A `Refusal` variant for it was added and removed the same evening: `every_refusal_is_still_raised_somewhere`, written in the same hour, failed on its first run because the variant had a sentence and no call site. Inventing one to satisfy the test would have been the failure the test exists to catch |

### Defects found by driving, 2026-08-20

| | |
|---|---|
| ✅ | **★★★ Full screen would not turn off, and it filled the display.** `toggle_fullscreen` read `ViewportInfo::fullscreen` to decide what to ask for next — and a `ViewportCommand` is **queued and answered by the backend**, so a second press before it caught up read the pre-first-press state and asked for full screen *again*. The window filled the screen with no route back except closing it. ★★ **It presented as flakiness**: two of three driven runs, depending on how many frames fell between the presses — and it was written off after the first, because it passed on re-run and `osk.exe` was up on the machine. That is the reading the RAG warns about by name; the last time something here looked like harness noise, the conclusion drawn was *"this machine cannot type"* and it cost the keyboard surface for months. ★ **The doc comment directly beneath the bug already stated the cause**, as a *labelling* concern — why a trace line should say `asked=` rather than `on=`. The fact was known and its consequence never drawn. Fixed by remembering the request with its frame, believing it over a report that has not reflected it, and **expiring it after four frames** — the last is the safety property rather than a tidy-up, because a compositor that declines the command outright would otherwise leave the shell permanently convinced of a state its window is not in. Four unit tests; verified PASS three times running |
| ✅ | **★★ Two driven checks were accusing the build of the fixture's properties.** `insert_image` reported *"THE PAGE DID NOT CHANGE"* about a picture that was demonstrably on the page: its pixel floor was *one in five hundred of the page*, which is a statement about the FIXTURE — on a 1584 × 1224 pt CAD sheet at 0.3× a 64 × 16 pt picture is 19 × 5 screen pixels, so a **correct** insert changes 90 of 169,416. The floor is now derived from the operand. And `a_shift_drag_between_documents_moves_the_pages` reported *"the sheets are now in BOTH documents"* when the source was a **one-page** file the engine correctly refuses to empty. Both now ask what else happened before accusing, and skip rather than fail when the document cannot exercise the feature. ★ *A fact about the fixture is not a fact about the build* |
| ✅ | **★★ The baked-gap gate had a hole the width of a closing brace**, and **four already-shipped defects** were through it. Its pattern enumerated the character before a suspicious run of spaces as `[A-Za-z,.:;)]` — and in Rust a clause very often ends in an interpolation, so `}` is one of the likeliest characters there and was not in the set. Widening it to *any non-space except `(` and `"`* (a gap that BEGINS a literal is deliberate indentation) found four mangled trace lines that had shipped. The self-test grew the case that got past it |

### The selection filter — what a click can land on

**★ Where this came from.** The operator, 2026-08-21: *"On the bottom bar I
want a filter menu that pops up with all the options of what to enable
selecting of — text, points, lines, etc … This is to replace the wonky content
edit text and edit objects menu at the top."* `OPERATOR_REQUESTS.md` O17.

| | |
|---|---|
| 🔨 | **★★★ A `Select` button on the status bar, and eleven classes behind it.** Text, Lines, Pictures, Blocks, Parts, Points, Markup, Dimensions, Form fields, Links and Characters — each with a glyph and a checkbox, plus All and None. Clicks now respect it: a class switched off is not selectable in Read, not in Review, not in Edit. **NOT YET DRIVEN** — it builds, 1,628 tests and 17 gates are green, and by R1 that is not a report of working software. The popup publishes an indexed diagnostic rect per row precisely so a harness can choose from it rather than only prove it opens |
| ✅ | **The diagnosis of *wonky*, because it is about gesture design rather than about two buttons.** Edit ▸ Content asks the operator to **declare an intention before pointing at anything** — *I am now editing text* — and the hit test then obeys the declaration rather than the drawing. So the same click on the same pixel means different things depending on a control that is not on screen while you aim, and making a class reachable costs a mode change plus two levels of ribbon travel. This file's own measurement of the ritual for typing one character was **four steps**. A filter on the status bar is always visible, one click from anywhere, and says what it is doing while you do it |
| ✅ | **★★ It is SUBTRACTIVE, always, and that is what makes it safe.** It can only take candidates away, never make something pickable that was not. So `PickFilter::default()` is *defined* as everything the shell can pick today and **R6 holds by construction** rather than by testing every path twice — a fresh install behaves exactly as the shell behaved before this existed. ★ The tempting alternative was rejected on measurement: *"Points on means a click lands straight on the nearest anchor"* would re-open the defect the rung ladder closed, because one CAD export in the fixture set holds **6,681 anchors in a single path object**. One-click node picking already has a home in the Node tool |
| ✅ | **It sits ABOVE the mode, which is not the same as replacing it.** The composition is `capability_allows(class, mode) && filter.allows(class)` — **both**. Switching a class on cannot grant Read the ability to edit content, and nothing in the filter can reach a capability. Two questions with two owners; collapsing them is how a filter turns into a hole in the mode system |
| ✅ | **The class list is derived, not invented.** Every one of the eleven is something the hit test can already distinguish, routed through `panels::objects::summary::object_kind` rather than re-deriving it — that module's header says a second kind classifier is exactly the divergence it exists to prevent. A row nothing consults would be a lie told once per session; a distinguishable class with no row is a thing the operator cannot reach |
| ✅ | **A form XObject is called a *Block*, and that is the most load-bearing word in the popup.** It is an entire nested drawing the page treats as one opaque object — usually a title block or a border, and the usual answer to *why is the selection box so big?* There is no everyday English word for it and there is a perfect **CAD** one, which is the vocabulary this operator has. *Groups* would collide with layers (optional content groups), which this shell has a panel for; *Form XObject* is correct and undecodable by anyone who has not read the specification |
| ✅ | **Switching everything off is legal, and the bar says so while it lasts.** *"Nothing on the page can be selected"*, on the left with the narration, ahead of the decline note because it outranks it — a decline explains why one gesture did nothing, this explains why every gesture will. The state is a real request on a dense drawing (*do not let me grab anything by accident*) and its symptom is otherwise indistinguishable from a fault. Off-canvas, per rule 4 |
| ✅ | **It survives a restart**, in one readable line beside `settings.txt`. Written immediately rather than debounced, because a filter can only change on a discrete click, so one change is already one decision. ★ Three on-disk states are kept distinct and the third is the one that matters: no file means *never configured*, a file with names means *these classes*, and an **empty** file means *I switched everything off* — collapsing the third into the first would overrule a deliberate choice every restart, and the operator would never see why |
| ⬜ | **The View twin** — a second popup beside it governing what is drawn with a bounding box or node markers while unselected. O17 part B, not started |
| ⬜ | **Select other on right-click** — walking the stack of objects under the cursor. O17 part D. ★ The query already exists and has never had a caller: `hit_test_all` returns the full depth-ordered list and is documented as *"the input to click-through cycling"*. Until it does, **an object completely covered by another is unreachable by any click** |
| ⬜ | **Deleting Edit ▸ Content.** ★ Half of it never worked: `edit.objects` has no dispatch arm anywhere and never had one — registered, drawn, enabled, and falling to the catch-all that traces `command-unimplemented`. The unreachability register already said so — *"P3 — NO RECORDED REASON ANYWHERE"*. Whether the two buttons are deleted or kept as a redundant path during transition is the operator's call |

| ✅ | **★★★ Redact by SELECTING on the canvas — 2026-08-30, `OPERATOR_REQUESTS.md` O60.** Ken: *"am I able to select objects on the canvas and redact them that way yet? … it just told me it couldn't."* He was right, and right about why: there were exactly two marking routes — the search box, which reaches **text pdfcer can read as text**, and *mark whole page*. On a CAD drawing almost everything worth redacting is between them: a title-block value drawn as **vector strokes**, a scanned stamp, a logo, a signature image, a run in an unmappable font. Nothing typed could reach any of them, so *"it couldn't"* was honest about the route rather than a defect in it. **Edit ▸ Redact selection** marks the box around whatever is selected. ★ No engine change was needed — `add_redaction` always took arbitrary regions; what was missing was handing it the selection. ★★ It **marks**, it does not remove: the confirmation says so in those words, because *"3 objects redacted"* would make an operator stop reviewing and save a document still containing every one of them. Bounding box rather than exact outline, deliberately — a redaction that follows a shape tells you what was there. Driven by `a_selected_object_can_be_marked_for_redaction`, which asserts the mark count rose **and** that nothing was applied: a build that quietly applied on marking would look completely correct and be the worst defect this feature could have |
| ⬜ | **Push buttons can be placed and can never DO anything**, and that is a pdfcer-wide policy rather than a shell gap: the engine authors no action of any kind on a button it creates — no submit, no reset, no navigate, no script — because `/A` reaches launch actions, network submits and JavaScript, and pdfcer recognises and preserves those and writes none. Disclosed on every creation. Ken asked for working buttons on 2026-08-30; filed back to the engine as a **policy question** with the narrowest useful version — **Reset only**, which touches nothing but this document's own fields and which pdfcer already performs internally. Submit was explicitly argued AGAINST: its purpose is to send data somewhere and no shell can audit a URL an operator typed |

| ◐ | **Turn a form field's box** — Turn left / Turn right in the field's Properties, beside its position and size. It turns what is drawn INSIDE the box; the box itself stays put. ★★★ The direction was the whole risk: a widget's `/MK /R` is **counterclockwise** while a page's `/Rotate` is **clockwise**, and the standard's two sentences are word for word identical apart from that one word — the engine called it *"the single most likely thing for a shell to get backwards"*, and a shell that missed it would ship two buttons that both work, both write a legal angle, and both turn the box the wrong way with **nothing failing**. The controls say *left* and *right*; the negation happens in one line at the panel, where the engine asked for it. **NOW DRIVEN** — `turning_a_field_right_turns_it_right` passes, asserting **270** after one right turn |
| ◐ | **Say something other than the measurement** — a text box on a ce dimension's properties. ★★ It does **not** change what was measured: the measurement stays underneath, so clearing the box restores *exactly the number that was there* rather than re-measuring, and the receipt names the number both going on and coming off. On a drawing, text that replaces a measured value and text that changed it are a note and a lie respectively. No Clear button — emptying the box **is** the restore. **Built; the driven check for this half is still owed** |
| ✅ | **Deleting makes the object go away at once** — it used to sit on screen for a second or two while the page redrew, with no gesture in flight to explain the wait, so a delete looked like it had done nothing and the natural response was to press Delete again — which deletes something else |
| ✅ | **And when it cannot show you, it tells you** — *"Your change is made — the picture of it is still being drawn."* ★★★ There is no shape to slide when you change a colour, press Bold, delete text, mark a redaction or rotate a page, and no redraw answer either: a **two-pixel** region render costs 691 ms on your site plan. So the honest general answer is not a worse picture, it is saying the picture is not the answer yet. ★ Silent under 0.4 s, so it never flickers on quick edits, and an unedited document never says it |
| ✅ | **The shape follows your hand** — drag a line's end point and the line **bends**. Drag a shape and the shape moves. Resize and the shape scales. ★★★ It never did before: every gesture drew a bounding box and nothing else, following a written rule in the code that said *"a preview shows the cursor, the render shows the document"* — overruled by operator ruling against a named comparison (Inkscape). ★★ It uses the real geometry pdfcer has already parsed, so it costs no engine call and no redraw and runs at pointer speed. For geometry it is not approximate: it is **exact**. Paths only — text and images keep the box, because a rectangle genuinely is all the program knows about where an image is going |
| ✅ | **The old position stops showing** — the picture underneath still has the object where it was, and it cannot be redrawn in under about 0.7 s on a dense drawing, so without this you would see the thing twice. ★★ It erases the object's **own outline** rather than a box over it: a polyline blanks a line's own width, not a title-block cell. Stated plainly because it is the one part that is not exact — anything drawn underneath the object's own ink disappears until the redraw lands |
| ✅ | **It stops snapping back** — the preview stays on screen until the page catches up. Before this, releasing a drag threw the preview away while the picture still showed the old position, so the object appeared to jump back and then forward a second later, which reads as the program refusing the edit and changing its mind. ★★★ Holding it is honest rather than optimistic: **the edit has happened** — only the picture is behind. A refused edit holds nothing, and a picture nothing ever arrives to replace is dropped after four seconds |
| ✅ | **All three driven and falsified** — `dragging_a_node_bends_the_line` asserts the preview is built, that it reaches the painter, and that it outlives the release. ★★ It was then run against the **previous release**, which has none of this, and failed on the first assertion — so the check can tell the two builds apart, which is the only thing that makes a green result mean anything |
| ✅ | **Bold uses a real bold font again** — press Bold on a page that carries a real bold face and pdfcer sets the text in it, rather than thickening the plain face artificially. ★★★ It had stopped: the engine's `Pass 179.0` turned a refusal this shell was **built on** into a success, so the retry that reaches the real face stopped firing and Bold quietly began faking a weight into the saved file, on a page where the genuine face was in the page's own font list. Nothing failed — the verb succeeded, the page changed, the disclosure was written — and one unit test caught it only because it asserts the resulting face **by name**. The lesson kept: *a shell built on a refusal breaks when the refusal is upgraded into a success* |
| ✅ | **And a rung that was never there** — where the real face pdfcer found turns out not to be able to show the text (`Times-Bold` has no `o`, so it cannot set *hello world* on a page where `Calibri-Bold` can), the old behaviour gave up and told you to use a face that had just failed. It now thickens the letters and says which real face it tried and why it could not be used |
| ✅ | **Faking bold and italic is now your choice** — Settings ▸ Fonts. Fake it quietly (the default), fake it and say so plainly, or never fake it. ★ A real face is preferred under all three, and the window says so under the group: *never fake it* reads like *never change my font* and is not |
| ⚠ | **Both of the two rows above are UNVERIFIED BY DRIVING**, and the reason is the machine. At the end of 2026-08-30 every input-driving check reported *"the window could not be brought to the front"* — Windows refusing `SetForegroundWindow` to a background process — **including checks that had passed an hour earlier in the same session, unchanged**. That is the fifth documented way to run the suite wrongly (a busy foreground) rather than a defect in what shipped. Two checks are written and registered and waiting; `turning_a_field_right_turns_it_right` asserts **270** after one right turn, which is the single number that catches a missing negation. Stated here rather than softened, because *"the tests pass" is not a report of working software* |

### Phase 1¾ — the clipboard

| | |
|---|---|
| ✅ | **★★★ Cut, copy and paste of page content — 2026-08-20, and it was the operator's OLDEST open request.** *"can you get cut copy and paste working for objects I select on the canvas?"*, asked in the first week and repeatedly since. Select a line, a shape or a piece of text and `Ctrl+C`; `Ctrl+V` lands it 10 pt down and right so the copy is visible, or in place on a different page. `Ctrl+X` is one undo entry, because only one half of a cut is an edit. Any mixture of kinds, and the clip owns its resources by value — so copy, close the source document, paste into another, and it is the same code path rather than a special case. ★★ **Our own scoping was right and incomplete in one place that was the whole job.** We asked for *"expose the copy engine you already have at object granularity"* on the strength of `import_object`, which is a recursive reference-remapping object-graph copy. The engine checked that reading by reading the function, agreed, and named the gap: **a page's content objects are not indirect objects** — they are byte ranges inside a content stream whose operators name resources **by page-local name**, so on the destination page `/F1` is a different font. Paste the bytes verbatim and you get the right glyphs in the wrong typeface, and **neither failure errors**. *A graph copy does not copy a namespace* is now written into `canvas::clipboard` as the general lesson |
| ✅ | **★★★ …and `Ctrl+C`, `Ctrl+X` and `Ctrl+V` had NEVER ONCE REACHED THE KEYMAP.** The operator reported it twice — *"still no ctrl+c, ctrl+v, ctrl+x"* — and on 2026-08-20 they were bound, which was necessary and **not sufficient**. `egui-winit`'s key handler pushes `Event::Copy`/`Cut`/`Paste` and **returns before the `Event::Key` push**, so the chord matcher saw nothing while the manifest contained the binding, unit tests asserted the binding, and the menu displayed the shortcut. **A keymap lookup is not a keystroke.** ★ `Ctrl+V` was worse: `Event::Paste` is raised **only when the OS clipboard holds non-empty text**, so with it empty the keystroke vanished entirely and whether paste worked depended on what had last been copied *in another application* — not random, not reproducible, and unfixable from inside pdfcer. A copy therefore now also leaves a readable sentence on the OS clipboard, which is what makes the chord arrive and which reads as an explanation rather than as garbage if it is pasted into an email by accident. The translation goes **through the keymap** rather than hard-coding three ids, so a rebinding is honoured. Filed in `D:/dev/rag/egui/`, because every egui project with a data-driven keymap has this and does not know it. ★★ **And the fix was applied to one reader of the signal, not to the others** — see the row below |
| ✅ | **★★★ …and the same defect was still live in TWO more places for a further day. 2026-08-21, on the operator's report.** *"if I select text in read mode, or … in an edit box … and press ctrl+c … I get a notice to paste it back into pdfc."* `canvas::textsel::clipboard` asked `key_pressed(Key::C)` — the identical mistake, one grep away from the paragraph explaining why it cannot work — so **sweeping text on the page and pressing Ctrl+C had never copied it, in any mode, since the day it was written**. And a text box on the canvas had no `Ctrl+C` handler *at all*: a guard had been added to stop the sweep answering while a draft composes, which was correct, and nothing was given the chord instead — **a chord with no owner does not go quiet, it goes to whoever claims it next**, which was the object clipboard. ★ The transferable half is not the `egui-winit` quirk, which was already found, understood and written up in capitals: it is that **a finding recorded in one module is not a fix applied to its siblings**, and the question nobody asked was *who else reads this signal?* Fixed in all three places, drafts gained Cut and Paste as well (both also absent), and `tools/gates/check-clipboard-chords.sh` now fails the build on any file asking about `C`/`X`/`V` as a key — because the only reliable version of "remember to check the others" is a script |
| ⬜ | **Across two pdfcer windows**, and **to another program**. The first needs the clip registered under a private Windows clipboard format — a call this shell does not make yet. The second needs the selection rendered as a standalone one-page PDF, filed with the engine and deliberately *not* the same bytes: a one-page PDF cannot carry which byte range was which object, so re-deriving that on the way back in would make a pdfcer→pdfcer paste worse than a pdfcer→Illustrator one. Two formats, two jobs |
| ✅ | **★★★ Form fields, both senses — 2026-08-29, `OPERATOR_REQUESTS.md` O58.** `Ctrl+V` pastes a copied field as a **new, independent** field with a free name; `Ctrl+Shift+V` pastes it as **another box for the same field**, so typing in either fills both. The operator chose the split and both chords, rather than a dialog. ★★ **Before this there was no path at ALL** — not a lossy one, not a refused one. `canvas::clipboard::copy` reads the object selection, a selected field lives on `doc.selected_field`, and `/Widget` is deliberately excluded from annotation selection, so `Ctrl+C` over a field with visible grips fell through to the content copy and said *"nothing is selected"*. ★★★ **And it went lossless the same afternoon.** The first version re-authored through `New*Field` — a spec of geometry plus a dozen booleans — so eight properties readable on `forms::Field` were writable nowhere and a hand-written fidelity table disclosed the loss. `Pass 167.0` shipped `pdfcer_core::formclip` **within ninety minutes of the request**, and `copy_field` / `paste_field` do not *express* properties, they **carry** them. Now travelling: `/DA` and — unasked for and needed — **the font it names**, installed into the destination `/DR` and renamed there if that name is taken, with the `/DA` rewritten to match; `/Q`; `/DV` (always, even when the value is dropped, because a default is the reset target rather than content); `/AA` with its `/CO` registration; `/MK`'s colours; `/BS`'s styles; `/Ff` as an integer, which brings `DoNotSpellCheck`, `DoNotScroll`, `FileSelect`, `RichText` and `CommitOnSelChange` free; and the baked `/AP` with its whole resource closure. The `Lost` enum, the loss sentence and eighty lines of `forms::Field` → `Draft` translation are **deleted**. ★★ **An unsigned signature field now copies** — which hands this shell signature-field *authoring* it never had, since there is still no `add_signature_field` — and a **signed** one is refused at the copy, because what would otherwise travel is the baked "signed by" artwork into a file nobody signed. Radio groups travel whole, translated rather than rescaled, with the ignored rectangle size disclosed. ★ Every disclosure is the engine's, verbatim: *one fact, one wording*, and two of this shell's own refusal sentences went stale within an hour of being written. **Fifth confirmation that a reply arriving is not a capability landing** — the workaround shipped at 12:49 and was replaced at 14:0x. ★★ **And the chord assignment is a SETTING** (Settings ▸ Display ▸ *Copying a form field*), because Acrobat assigns the two the other way round and both orders have a real argument: pdfcer puts the common intent on the common chord, Acrobat matches muscle memory and its own linking default is a documented user-friction point with a standing request to change it. **It swaps the chords, never what a command means** — `edit.paste` is always "paste as a new field" and `edit.paste_duplicate` always "another box for the same field", so the ribbon, the context menu, the shortcuts dialog and the keyboard agree by construction. Swapping the *commands* was rejected: a button reading **Paste as duplicate** would have pasted a new field. Default is the operator's own order. Driven **both ways in two processes** and **falsified** — with `apply_paste_chords` stubbed the mirror check fails on its first assertion and names the cause. **Naming sourced, not chosen:** `Text1` → `Text2`, `Drawn By` → `Drawn By2` — Acrobat's plain numeric suffix, whose sourced rationale is that a script loops over fields sharing *"the non-number part of the field name"*, which a space would break. ★★★ **And one Acrobat convention is deliberately refused:** a second sourced account has it numbering copies `Text.0`, `Text.1` with a **dot**, and pdfcer must not — `.` is the fully-qualified-name separator (§12.7.3.2), so `Text.2` is a *child field named `2` under a parent named `Text`*, a third shape that is neither of the two the chords mean. ★ **A divergence recorded rather than hidden:** in Acrobat, plain Copy/Paste is the LINKED behaviour and there is no paste-as-independent chord at all — the operator's split puts the common intent (independent) on the common chord, which Acrobat's own users have a standing request for. His ruling, reversible in one line. Driven end to end by `a_form_field_can_be_copied_and_pasted_both_ways`, which was **red first**: `Ctrl+V` did nothing because `egui-winit` only raises `Event::Paste` when the OS clipboard holds text, and the new copy path had not written the marker the existing one does — a documented trap that does not protect a code path written after it |
| ✅ | **★★★ Cut, copy and paste for everything else — 2026-08-29, `OPERATOR_REQUESTS.md` O59.** Three things, in the order he set. **(1) Cut is greyed with a reason** and the chord is refused too — a chord is dispatched through the keymap WITHOUT consulting command enablement, so greying the button alone would have deleted a redaction mark on Ctrl+X while putting nothing on the clipboard. It refuses BEFORE the copy, asserted separately: refusing after would leave the mark on the page and a copy of it on the clipboard, so the next Ctrl+V arms a redaction nobody reviewed. **(2) Whole pages** — Pages ▸ Clipboard, and what pdfcer holds is a complete PDF. The only clipboard controls in the program with no chord, deliberately: the `pages.*` operand rule always resolves, so a chord rung reading it would take Ctrl+C from the canvas permanently. Two disclosures at opposite ends — a form field left behind at the copy (still fixable then), orphaned field boxes at the paste (the engine's own *"looks right and is not"*). **(3) Bookmarks and their subtrees**, in their panel — **the one operation Acrobat cannot do between two files at all**, by Adobe's own documentation. The dropped-destination warning comes before the press, because a bookmark that loses its destination still shows and does nothing. ★★★ **And it found a control nobody could click:** Remove was 53 points below the bottom of its own panel and Copy 81, both drawn, both publishing a rect — the dock gives a body a fixed rect and no scrolling, and this body wrapped only its list. Invisible to every test that did not select a bookmark first, and none did. All three driven and falsified. |
| ⬜ | **Dimensions.** Annotations rather than page content, so these verbs cannot address them at all, and a pasted dimension needs a sidecar record and a group. Filed |

### Phase 1½ — the conventions sweep

**★ Where this came from.** The operator asked *"how can you learn from these
other programs so that you can build the missing parts more effectively?"*, and
the honest diagnosis was that **this was never missing knowledge** — asked
directly, *"what happens when you click inside an unfilled rectangle in a drawing
program?"* comes back right every time. Nothing PROMPTED the question while the
code was being written. `D:/dev/rag/ui-conventions/` is five gesture classes,
each a numbered list carrying where the rule comes from and the failure mode when
it is absent; `tools/gates/check-conventions.sh` makes every registered surface
answer every row **in its own source**. It cannot check behaviour and does not
pretend to — it checks the question was asked, which is the whole of the problem.
Fourteen gaps on its first run.

| | |
|---|---|
| ✅ | **★★ Shift constrains every drag on the canvas, and says so — 2026-08-20.** *The* resize convention, present in every program in the class for thirty years, and absent from all five drags here. A **resize** keeps the shape's proportions, applying the factor the pointer travelled furthest to produce — which for a mid-edge grip falls out as proportional-resize-from-an-edge with **no special case**, because the idle axis sits at exactly 1.0 and can never win. A **move**, a **ce-dimension label**, a **perimeter corner** and a **Bézier handle** lock to an axis. The lock is re-decided every frame rather than sampled at the press, so an operator who starts crooked and commits to vertical mid-drag is followed, and letting go returns to the free path. ★ A handle locks to its **anchor's** axis rather than the press's — a control point's meaning is the tangent it defines, so the line that matters runs through the on-curve point; Illustrator and Inkscape both do this, and it is the one place the four differ. ★★ **Apply and announce are ONE CALL.** The first cut had each site check the modifier, do the arithmetic and announce — and a fifth drag could have constrained silently, which is not a smaller version of the feature but the convention's stated failure mode exactly: *"the operator holds Shift, gets a result they did not expect, and cannot tell whether the modifier did anything."* Driven by a **pair** of drags in one process, because a single locked drag reporting equal factors proves nothing — a build ignoring the key could satisfy it by luck on a diagonal. Not built, and named as decisions: Alt-about-centre, Alt-to-break-symmetry, a 45° lock, and a label held to its standoff rather than a page axis |
| ✅ | **★ A perimeter corner snaps back onto the geometry it came off — 2026-08-20.** The sharpest shape a missing convention can take: the tool that **placed** the vertex snaps, and the drag that moves it did not — so a corner could be put onto a line and never put back. It now uses the same `snap_candidates` query, the same tolerance and the same *"Snap to content"* switch a measure pick uses, through one function, because a drag that snapped by its own rules would honour a different switch from the tool beside it. Alt suspends it. ★ **The snap overrides the grab point**, and the two conventions genuinely conflict here: preserving a three-pixel grab offset would land the corner three pixels off the thing it snapped to — a corner that looks snapped and is not, which is worse than either alternative. The marker is the same glyph at the same size, so an operator who learned that a square means *endpoint* while placing reads the identical square while correcting. ★★ `DimensionVertex` had to join the decomposition list, and **that list has now been the defect three times** — a drag works perfectly without the model, it just never snaps, and a snap that never fires is indistinguishable from one that found nothing |
| ⬜ | Still open from the sweep: ⚠️ ~~no rotate handle (blocked on the engine verb)~~ — **the grip shipped 2026-08-20 in `560280a` and this row was not updated until 2026-08-21; it was neither absent nor blocked, and the note claimed both** — no right-click to add or remove a perimeter point (both engine verbs exist), a zero-travel release still raises an action in three of four drag paths, an unfilled `/Square` still claims its interior, caret indices are characters rather than grapheme clusters, and there is no selection inside a text draft |

### Phase 6 — markup completeness

| | |
|---|---|
~~**★ The substrate comes first, and it does not exist.** This shell can place no markup at all today: the eight `markup.*` commands are registered and drawn, and every one falls through to `command-unimplemented`.~~ **Written 2026-08-13 and true then. The substrate landed 2026-08-14 and Phase 6 is now materially complete**: eight `MarkupKind`s place — Rectangle, Ellipse, Arrow, Polyline, Polygon, **Revision cloud**, Freehand, Highlight — plus the three text-markup verbs on a selection, plus the Comments panel. What is left is **Style** (note text, opacity, fill, line width), the three text-bearing kinds (text box, sticky, stamp), and restyling a **placed** markup, which is the Format tab.

The paragraph is struck rather than deleted because of what it got right: it named the substrate as the blocker where the rows below each named `pdfcer-core`, and it was correct. *The six "engine-ready" rows were blocked on our own substrate, not on pdfcer* — which is the same distinction §1c of `NO_SURFACE.md` now makes for every external blocker in this project.

| | |
|---|---|
| ✅ | **Markup tool substrate, and four kinds placing** — Rectangle, Ellipse, Arrow, Highlight. One `CanvasTool::Markup(MarkupKind)` carrying a kind rather than one variant per shape, so a tool that is two kinds at once is unrepresentable and the four ribbon controls behave as a radio with nothing enforcing it. Rubber-band preview, `Action::CommitMarkup`, an apply arm calling `EditSession::add_markup`. **A click with no drag places nothing** — the old shell dropped a 120×60 default box justified as "a placeholder the operator will resize", and there is no resize verb here, so that box could only be corrected by undo. Arrow keeps **raw** endpoints rather than a normalised rect, verified by driving a reversed drag and getting the same two page points in the opposite order — which a normalising implementation cannot produce |
| ✅ | **Drag origins are the button-down point** — `Response::drag_started()` fires only once egui has *decided* an interaction is a drag, by which time `interact_pointer_pos()` reports where the pointer has travelled to. Measured at **94 PDF points** of error on A1 at 0.21× zoom. The fix went into `PointerFrame::press_origin`, not into markup, because **the marquee and the move drag had it too**: a marquee that starts late encloses less than the operator drew round, and a move under-shoots by the same distance. Neither had ever been reported, because both fail by a plausible amount rather than a visible one |
| ✅ | **Polyline, polygon, ink** — 2026-08-14, three gesture families in one substrate. **Ink** is drag-shaped and reuses the existing path; **polyline and polygon are click-shaped** and get a live click and no drag from `press_kind`, in an early return beside the measure tools' — same shape, same reason. Ending follows `measure/circular.rs` exactly: **a double-click and a registered `markup.finish`, through one commit path**, because consistency with the tool that already solved this beats a marginally better third answer. `Geometry::{Band, Vertices, Strokes}` is **one enum on one** `Action::CommitMarkup` rather than three actions, so the CLI-equivalence claim keeps resting on one `spec()`. **Ink simplifies by Ramer–Douglas–Peucker at 0.5 pt, and the tolerance is derived rather than chosen**: RDP bounds deviation by ε, the pen is 2 pt wide, so ε = half the half-width keeps the simplified centreline strictly inside the stroke the raw trail would have drawn — a test asserts the pen and the tolerance stay tied. Measured 240 → 31 points at that tolerance, and `markup-commit … raw= kept=` carries both numbers because a build whose simplification did nothing emits an otherwise identical line |
| ✅ | **★★★ A shape you drew can have its nodes moved and deleted — 2026-09-05, DRIVEN, and it needed an engine Pass.** His report: *"I also can't edit or delete nodes of a markup shape once it is drawn."* Three separate absences in one sentence; this is the one it literally describes. Select a polyline or a polygon and every node gets a small square you can grab — **drag** to move it, and with the **Points tool** armed (`A`) **Ctrl-drag** to add a node after the one you grabbed and **Ctrl+Shift-drag** to delete it. The gesture is the identical one the ce-dimension corners already use, imported rather than re-invented, because one canvas must have one answer to *what does Ctrl-drag on a node mean*. A **line** shows its two ends and moves either. Nodes **snap** to the drawing through the same query and the same *Snap to content* switch a measure pick uses; Alt suspends it; Shift locks the node to one axis. ★★★ **It could not have been built here before 2026-09-05**, and this is the third time that has been the answer: `pdfcer-core` did not model a markup annotation's geometry at all until `Pass 255.0` — `/Vertices`, `/L` and `/InkList` were unreadable and no verb could rewrite one. It was filed and deliberately not worked around, because a shell rewriting the appearance stream itself would have produced a shape that looks right in this canvas, right in a screenshot, right in `pdfcer`, and comes back **in the old place** in Acrobat. ★★ **Every frame asks the engine first.** `reshape_annotation_preview` shares one body with the verb, so the shape you see under the pointer is the shape the release commits, or it is the shape already on the page — a drag never offers something the release would refuse. **A refusal is a sentence**: a triangle that cannot lose a fourth corner says so on the status bar instead of doing nothing, which is the founding defect shape of this project and is the second half of his complaint. ★ **A rectangle, an ellipse, a freehand mark and a text mark draw NO node handles at all** — not greyed ones (R9) — and, because *nothing* is also what a build that forgot to draw them looks like, arming the Points tool over one says in words why it has no nodes, in the operator's vocabulary: a `/Square` is *a rectangle*, a `/Circle` is *an ellipse*. Per-point ink editing is refused by pdfcer on purpose and the sentence says so rather than promising it later. ★★ **Driven and falsified three times** by `a_markup_shapes_nodes_can_be_edited`, which draws a real polygon, drags a node, deletes one, and takes the shape below its floor. Its pixel oracle is the part that earns its keep: the destination gains ink and the origin loses it, **with the selection cleared**, so a build whose trace is perfect and whose page never repaints is caught — and it was, deliberately, on a plant. ⬜ **Not built and named rather than implied:** the right-click menu the engine describes these verbs as living on. |
| ✅ | **…and the ending disagreement is the second worked example of the reference-app rule** — Inkscape and SolidWorks both close a shape by clicking the first vertex; **Acrobat does not, and Acrobat won on applicability rather than head-count**: `/Polygon` closes back to `/Vertices[0]` by §12.5.6.13, so pdfcer is in Acrobat's position and a click-the-first-vertex rule would author a duplicate vertex and a zero-length closing segment. SolidWorks' Escape-to-end was refused outright — in this shell Escape means *abandon*, and committing on the key an operator presses to say "no" is the least recoverable reading available |
| ✅ | **Underline, strikeout, squiggly** — 2026-08-14, the direct payoff of the text-selection gesture that unblocked them. **Select text, then press the control**: the selection is the operand, so there is no `CanvasTool` variant, no new gesture and no second text-range resolver — which is the same divergence `textsel` already refused Acrobat's `Alt`+rectangle over. Model taken from Acrobat *Reader*, which leads with select-then-mark; Inkscape and SolidWorks have no vote, neither having PDF text markup. The **wash is the preview** — literally the same boxes — so no second affordance was added, and `TextSelection` grew `page_quads` in the *same* walk that builds the canvas quads rather than inverting the projection at the authoring site, which on a `/Rotate 90` page would mirror the mark about the page centre. Gated on a new `selection.text` condition, published only for a live selection, so the control cannot be enabled while the press would decline. **Reachable in Review only** — see the row below |
| ✅ | **…and Edit reaches them too, since the Text tool landed** — the P3 tension is closed rather than recorded. With `view.tool_text` armed, Edit sweeps text and `selection.text` becomes true, so the three controls enable. **Observed, not argued**: `ui-verify`'s `text_tool_selects_and_marks_in_edit` drives Edit through dead control → arm tool → sweep → the *same* control now invoking → `add-text-markup epoch=1`, and then **retires the tool and proves the sweep stops working**. That last phase is the falsifier — every phase above it would also pass against a build that had simply deleted `takes_the_press`'s `!caps.edit_content` clause, which would have silently replaced the only content-selection gesture the product has |
| ✅ | **★ Revision clouds — 2026-08-19, and the blocker had been gone for days.** This row said `/BE` appeared nowhere in `pdfcer-core` and that all three routes were closed. `MarkupSpec::Cloud` had shipped — vertices, border, interior, width, intensity, with a matching error variant beside it — and **nothing in this shell noticed**, while the operator asked for the tool three times. The polygon shipped first rather than the rectangle, and the reason is the gesture: `MarkupKind::Cloud` joins `is_vertex` and **nowhere else** in that impl, `markup::action` takes Polygon and Cloud through **one arm** with one three-vertex floor, and the whole difference lives in `spec` as `/BE << /S /C /I 1.0 >>` — Acrobat's own default cloud, under the standing *make it behave the way the program you are replacing does* tie-breaker. Its glyph is nine outward arcs on a closed loop: nine because an even count is symmetric about both axes and reads as an ornament, and because below nine the scallop flattens at 16 px into `shape-ellipse`. The preview draws the closing segment **straight**, not scalloped — a gesture overlay is the cursor, and approximating a border effect the engine bakes an appearance for would be a second rendering path, which drift |
| ⛔ | **Note text** — markup cannot carry `/Contents`. **Filed and accepted 2026-08-14**, landing as `/Contents` + `/T` + `/M` **together**, on the argument that a note with no author reads as a broken panel rather than an absent field. `/M` is engine-stamped (a caller-supplied date is a caller-supplied lie); `/T` is optional and `None` omits the key — **no placeholder author is invented** |
| ⬜ | Style: **width and fill** *(colour works)* — both engine-ready |
| ✅ | Style: **opacity — built 2026-08-19** in the Properties panel's restyle section, alongside colour and line width; the rest of this row is kept because of what it got wrong. ★ **The blocker was FALSE and this row said so for weeks.** `EditSession::set_markup_style` takes a `MarkupStyle { opacity: Some(StyleEdit::Set(a)) }` and writes `/CA` (`edit.rs:13093`), with `StyleEdit::Clear` removing it and tests for both directions. What is genuinely absent is narrower and is the second finding: `MarkupSpec` carries **no alpha**, so authoring a translucent mark is `add_markup` **then** `set_markup_style` — two verbs, two undo entries, and an operator who presses Ctrl+Z once gets an **opaque** mark rather than no mark. Filed as a boundary observation, not as a blocker. The restyle half is a **shell** gap: this build has no Format-tab surface calling that verb at all. The original question did uncover a live viewer defect — see **`DEFECTS.md` D9** |

### Phase 7 — measure completeness

**★ The substrate landed 2026-08-14.** `measure_tool.rs` came across whole —
2,044 lines split into `canvas/measure/{pick,scale,state}.rs`, all 36 tests
carried, and **no engine API had moved**. The 12.M1 snap primitives came with
it as `canvas/snap.rs`. `CanvasTool` grew a `Measure(MeasureKind)` variant on
the same one-variant-carrying-a-kind argument markup settled.

| | |
|---|---|
| ✅ | **Linear ce dimension** — click A, click B, click where it sits. The third click **is** the commit: the old shell's Accept/Reject property bar is deliberately not carried across, because decision 024 retired *"a separate accept / reject box somewhere on the screen"* at the operator's instruction and `MODES_AND_PANELS.md` makes application-initiated floating default to **Never**. Three clicks is also what SolidWorks does, which is what *"make it work the way other programs do"* asks for; the corrective for a mis-placed dimension is undo, as it is for a markup. A live preview draws the constrained A→pointer segment while measuring and, once placing, the dimension itself through the **same** `dimension_preview_segments` a committed one is drawn from — so what you see is what commits |
| ✅ | **Two-line ce dimension** — pick two lines already on the drawing; `pick_line_in_page` resolves each click against the page's own geometry, so the shell and `pdfcer dimension-add` resolve the same click to the same line. Registered as `measure.two_line`, moving out of `PLANNED`. **Corrected 2026-08-14:** this row used to read *"the canvas gesture has no caller"*, false in five documents at once — the caller that was missing was **ours**, and it now exists |
| ✅ | **Escape is two rungs, not one** — a measure pick is a sequence of *clicks*, so there is no drag for claimant 1 to cancel, yet a dimension with point A taken and B not is unmistakably in flight. One Escape corrects a mis-aimed pick; a second puts the tool down. Without the split, one press would do both — decision 025's L1 violated in a new place |
| ✅ | **Radius / diameter — REBUILT 2026-09-03 as a POINT tool, `OPERATOR_REQUESTS.md` O105–O107, DRIVEN.** Ken: *"selecting a point sometimes makes a big circle, and selecting more points around a hole doesn't always get it to narrow down to the size of the hole."* ★★★ **It was not picking points. It hit-tested for a PDF path OBJECT and fed every anchor of every subpath of that object to the fit** — and on his own drawing (`SW41177.pdf` p1, `pdfcer object-list`) three objects carry **4,405**, **4,972** and **6,681** anchors, the largest holding **1,194 subpaths across 550 × 500 pt**, half the sheet. One click handed Taubin's fit six thousand points scattered over the drawing. Not intermittent: it depends on whether the hole's arc is its own small object or one subpath among 1,194, which he cannot see. ★★ The second half followed from the same design — a second click on the same object **toggled it out**, so clicking twice round one hole added it and removed it, and a click on a different object added another thousand-anchor blob. *"Add more points"* meant the opposite of what it means everywhere else. ★ **And the tool's own instruction had been telling him to do exactly the right thing all along** — *"Click three or more points around the arc"* — prose and mechanism disagreeing, with the prose describing the better design. **Now: one click is one point.** It goes THROUGH the snap machinery it used to be routed around, so a click lands on the drawing's own geometry when there is any and on the operator's own position when there is not — which is what makes a **scan or a bitmap** measurable (O106). Clicking a point already in the set removes it; the **Tool panel lists the set**, one numbered row per point saying where it came from and where it is, each removable by clicking it (O107). ★★ The panel's live line reports the count **and the current radius or diameter**, through the engine's own formatter and the group's scale, so the number can be watched converging — there was none before, and every correction was a commit and an undo. ★★★ **Driven by `three_clicks_round_a_hole_measure_the_hole` on a fixture built to carry the defect** — `hole-in-a-big-object.pdf`, **one** path object holding a 30 pt circle and forty unrelated segments across the page, because on a document whose circles are their own objects the defect *cannot occur* and the broken build would pass. It pins that fixture and ignores `--pdf`. Three clicks give `r=30.000 resid=0.0000`; **falsified** by feeding one stray point in, which reports *"a circle of radius 299.78"* — his sentence, as a number — and again by making the panel row inert. ★ What is deliberately NOT rebuilt is *"click the circle once and be done"*; it is worth having back at **subpath** granularity, because a subpath is the drawn entity and an object is not |
| ✅ | **The measure preview draws in the right place** — found while arming the circle preview and fixed with it. `measure::preview` converted PDF user space through `viewer::pdf_space_to_canvas` and handed the result straight to `ui.painter()`. That lands in **canvas** space (page top-left origin, no zoom) and the painter speaks **screen**, so the snap indicator and the linear preview were drawn offset by wherever the page sat in the window and at 100 % whatever the magnification — invisible at zoom 1 with the page at the window origin, wrong everywhere else. `measure::page_to_screen` now does both hops, the second through `PageMapping::to_screen`, which is the same outward crossing `canvas::overlay` has always used. `HANDOFF.md` defect 4's shape exactly, and no unit test could see it: the picking was always right, only the drawing was wrong |
| ✅ | **Set scale** — the dialog shipped, and with it the round trip this row was waiting for: `measure.set_scale` opens it, *Measure it on the drawing* closes the window and arms `MeasureKind::Scale`, the pick completes, and the window comes back with the measured length in it. Two edges read once per frame, in that order, because neither is a command — one is a button inside a dialog and the other is the canvas noticing its own state machine finished. `ScalePick` reuses `LinearPick` **verbatim**, since a reference line is a linear pick that happens not to be authored |
| ✅ | **Snapping** — the query is wired, so a pick lands on the geometry rather than near it. On a CAD sheet that is the difference between a dimension that measures a line and one that measures *close to* a line, which is worse than none because it is wrong by an amount nobody can see. `snap_candidates` is the **engine's** query, not a second one here; `PageMapping::snap_tolerance` sits beside its selection sibling so there is still exactly one place in `canvas/` that divides by zoom. **The indicator is drawn before the first pick**, which is the point of it — gating it on a gesture already in progress would show it everywhere except where it is needed. **One `Resolved` per frame** feeds both the marker and the click, so the point aimed at and the point committed are the same value rather than two derivations that agree until they don't. Alt refuses a snap for one pick, which is what makes the generous 10 px catch affordable. **Tab cycles candidates**, guarded on the armed tool rather than a mode, so focus navigation is untouched everywhere else |
| ✅ | **A derived candidate is confirmed, never assumed** — an inferred centerline takes two clicks: the first promotes, the second commits. The call site passed a constant `is_derived: false` until the query existed, which silently turned an inference into a commitment — rule 4's fuzzy-never-sneaky line, crossed by a placeholder argument |
| ✅ | **★ The snap indicator has its colour — 2026-08-19, and the absence had no symptom.** This row read *"`Overlays` is never installed by `pdfcer-gui` … `snap_indicator_tint` returns `None` on every frame"*, and it was true for a whole phase: the marker drew, in the selection colour, and the cue distinguishing *pdfcer proposing a point* from *a committed thing being selected* simply was not there. A missing role returns `None`, the caller falls back, and **nothing looks broken**. `canvas::overlays` now publishes the set beside `Theme::apply` — `preview` → `palette.notice` (*"something is worth knowing and nothing is broken"*, which is what a snap marker is), `dimension_selected` → `palette.accent` (what every other selection in this application is drawn in). The roles live in `pdfcer-gui`, not `egui-shell`: **R7**, because `"dimension_selected"` is a pdfcer concept wearing a colour. Both owed tests land, over `Preset::ALL` rather than the default — a preset is exactly where two roles collapse, and the two that are not the default are the two nobody looks at |
| ✅ | **★ Dimension groups are manageable — 2026-08-18, and the bigger half was not the window.** `measure.manage_groups` was registered, drawn on Measure ▸ Scale, listed in `reach`'s `SCAFFOLDED` set and **inert for the whole life of this build**; the operator hit it by name — *"I still can't get to edit dimension groups when I click on it."* Its recorded blocker, *"two of four verbs do not exist"*, had shrunk to two of **six** and was never re-measured. What was missing underneath is sharper than the window: `MeasureState::group` — *"the active authoring group the next dimension joins (ui-spec §2.6 group picker)"* — has existed since the Phase 7 salvage and **nothing in this crate ever wrote to it**, so a second group could be created, carry its own scale, and be joinable by nothing. The window's *Draw into* column is that picker. It also sets the drafting standard, shows or hides the group's layer, and sets the five appearance defaults of the style cascade's middle tier — each with **a count of how many members will visibly MOVE**, computed before the edit from `StyleProvenance::follows_group()` and deliberately **not** `set_group_style`'s return value, which counts every member regenerated including the ones that override the property being changed. `follows_group()` is true for `Factory` as well as `Group`, which is the easy thing to get wrong, and the predicate is the engine's throughout |
| ✅ | **★ A placed ce dimension is editable — the panel `FEATURES.md` has wanted since the style cascade shipped.** Select one and the Properties panel shows its group, what it measured, a radius/diameter switch for a circular one, and **all eleven** properties of the cascade's bottom tier — unit, precision, decimal marker, drafting standard, text height, line width, arrow length, arrow form, colour, **tolerance** and tolerance decimals — each with the override checkbox the operator asked for and a sentence naming which tier supplied the value in force. Nothing here **builds a label**: a limit tolerance *suppresses* the nominal rather than printing beside it and `Basic` prints no text at all, so the editor renders fields and states what each form will do; the measurement comes from `DimensionModel::display`, which is the producer the `/AP` is baked from — including the branch that never applies the group scale to an angular kind. Nothing is clamped or swapped: an inverted limit pair is refused by the engine with its own operator-facing sentence, shown verbatim, and **no action is raised until it validates**. Reachable from the contextual Format tab and the canvas right-click, both through `format.properties`, which raises `Action::Command("file.properties")` — a second route, never a second implementation |
| ✅ | **★ Renamed, deleted, and re-grouped — requested 2026-08-18, shipped by the engine 2026-08-19, wired the same morning.** The window carried a sentence where three controls should have been; it now carries the controls. **Delete asks the orphan question** rather than refusing or guessing: `pdfcer-core` refuses a populated group with the **member count in the refusal**, because *"this group is not empty"* and *"this group holds forty dimensions"* prompt different decisions and only a surface can put that question in front of an operator — so pressing Delete on a populated group offers a destination and a button that will succeed. Two facts travel with it, both stated before the press: the members are **re-measured** against where they land, and pdfcer **will not delete the dimensions with the group** (doing it inside the verb would be a second implementation of `delete_dimension`'s `/Annots` removal, and looping the existing one would make undo take a press per member and be able to stop halfway). **The group is a picker in the properties panel now**, and ★ `set_dimension_group` is **not a field assignment**: a ce dimension's label is derived from its group, so the verb re-measures and the printed number changes — `70.6 mm` becomes `2.00 m` for the same geometry, measured. That disclosure is the one the engine went out of its way to make sure a shell would not miss, and it is under the picker |
| ✅ | **A group's unit is settable, and it was never missing** — the request implied a gap that was not there. `set_group_scale` takes a whole `NumberFormat` and `NumberFormat.unit` is public, so the unit was reachable and merely **undiscoverable**; the engine declined to add sugar for it on speculation and was right. The window's unit combo goes through that path, carrying the group's own scale through **unchanged** — passing anything else would silently recalibrate a drawing while the operator was changing a unit |
| ⬜ | **Area** and **Angular** — the two a takeoff needs. `DimensionKind::Angular` **exists in the engine**, and the two-line tool already authors one when the picked lines are angled, so what is missing is a *direct* Angular tool (pick an apex and two directions) rather than the capability. The GUI's `MeasureKind` has no variant for it: shell-only work |
| ⬜ | Count tool and a takeoff schedule |
