A black core in a white halo is what Photoshop, Illustrator, GIMP, Inkscape and AutoCAD all use, and it is strictly **better** on mid grey, where an inverted cursor becomes another mid grey. The arms stop short of the centre so the pixel being aimed at stays visible. Black and white are deliberately **not theme colours**: a theme colour contrasts with the *application*, and this must contrast with the *operator's document* — content pdfcer does not control and no palette can be right about. ★ **The one feature in this shell a screenshot cannot verify**: Windows composites the pointer separately, so `ui-verify`'s window capture contains no cursor at any price — hence `cursor-crosshair on px=…` / `off`, emitted on change, which is the only machine-readable evidence that the sticky `cursor_image` is both set *and* cleared |
| ✅ | **★ An icon in the executable — 2026-08-18, operator request.** *"make and add a pdf icon to the exe so it shows as the icon when I associate it with pdfs."* Explorer, the Start menu, the pinned taskbar entry and the *Open with* dialog all read that icon **without running the program**, so it has to live in the PE image's `.rsrc` section — `build.rs` puts it there via `embed-resource`, a **build** dependency that ships in nothing. Two halves for two questions: the resource is what the shell reads, and `window_icon()` is what winit reads for the title bar and taskbar, since winit registers its class without one. Both come from **one render pass** so they cannot drift. ★ **The art is a script** — `tools/make-icon.py` draws a page, a folded corner and a red PDF badge from named geometry, so a change is a diff rather than a paint program; P, D and F are rectangles and half-capsules, because there is no font in this repository. Generic, not Adobe's: no Acrobat *A*, no logotype, no brand colour, no typeface of theirs, and *PDF* is an ISO standard's name rather than a mark. `VERSIONINFO` too, because a blank Properties tab reads as an unidentified program — which matters most for something being registered as a file handler. ★ **`PROVENANCE.md` opens by saying the author is NOT the operator**, which no other asset record here has to: the ribbon glyphs are his art and `catalog.rs` forbids a build session adding one. This broke that rule on instruction, and the file says so, names the request, and states both routes to replacing it. It exists because `check-shipped-assets` **failed the build** on the commit that added the icon. Verified by extracting the icon back **out of the built release exe** with Windows' own `ExtractAssociatedIcon` |
| ⬜ | Imposition in the print dialog · insert blank page · push-button field creation |
| ⬜ | Script-driven-field census · unencrypted-wrapper warning |

### Not salvaged yet

Present in the old shell, not yet carried across. All are Class A or B in
`SALVAGE.md` and none is a rewrite.

| | |
|---|---|
| ✅ | **Print dialog** with live preview — split 2,022 → five files at the three questions it answers. Fixed on the way: stale device capabilities after a printer change, `/Rotate` ignored so a rotated page was planned portrait and rendered landscape, and a texture-name collision |
| ✅ | **★ Printing actually reaches a printer — 2026-08-17, and it never had before.** This row was ✅ above for the whole of v0.1.0 while the dialog opened, said *"This build cannot reach a print device"*, and drew **no printer selector, no preview, no page controls and no commit button** — on a machine with **twelve** printers installed. The row was not lying about the dialog; the dialog was lying about the build. `dialogs::print::spooler` is the adapter, it was written as four *holes* returning `NotLinked` while `pdfcer-print` was genuinely unlinked, and its header set out in full the two edits that would fill them. **The manifest line landed; the holes did not.** `pdfcer-print` was compiled and linked into every shipped binary and no source file named `pdfcer_print` outside a doc comment. **The suite was green because a test held the defect in place**: `every_hole_refuses_rather_than_guessing` asserted all four calls refused — correct while unlinked, a lock the moment it was not, since filling the holes would have turned it red. *A test that pins a refusal must name the condition the refusal is conditional on, or it outlives its own premise and starts defending the absence of the feature.* Replaced by its own inverse, plus `print_dialog_reaches_the_spooler` in `ui-verify`, which **fails on the previously shipped build and passes on this one** — and which never presses the commit button, because a harness that can start a print job will eventually start one by accident |
| ✅ | **★ Paper size, tray and the driver's own Properties… — 2026-08-18, the surface the operator asked for three times.** *"acrobat reader has those options, and pretty much every program I have ever seen lets you press a properties button beside the selected printer in the drop-down menu to open the printer options."* All three asks were **one engine defect**: `build_devmode` synthesised a zeroed `DEVMODE`, and tray, paper and orientation all resolve through it. `pdfcer-print` fixed it and shipped `printer_forms`, `PaperSelection`, `printer_caps_for`, `edit_printer_configuration` and `spool_with_config`; this is the shell half. **Paper** is a combo beside orientation in Pages & Layout, populated from the driver's own list, and the job is **re-planned** through `printer_caps_for` — without which a job asking for A3 on a Letter-default device would be laid out for Letter and printed on A3, with no clip reported and nothing to explain it. **Properties…** sits beside the printer combo and opens the *driver's* dialog, whose result is carried into the job — up to **97 % of a real `DEVMODE` is driver-private tail** holding media type, quality and finishing that pdfcer cannot name and must not drop. **Tray-by-sheet-size is back**, deleted the day before because the engine read the field nowhere, and it is drawn in **all three** capability states: `DC_BINS` on Microsoft Print to PDF returns nothing while that device already picks by form, so a bool would have hidden a control from a device that was doing the thing by default. Two disclosures the operator could not otherwise learn: a chosen paper is a **request** two measured drivers were found silently ignoring, and a job whose driver would not report its settings prints with the rest **fallen back**. Driven by `print_paper_changes_the_plan`, which reads `paper=` and `sheet=` off one trace line and requires both to move — it never presses Properties… (a vendor's modal, unpublishable and un-dismissable by a harness) and never presses commit |
| 🔨 | **Measure tools** — **salvaged 2026-08-14**, whole: `canvas/measure/{pick,scale,state}.rs` + `canvas/snap.rs`, 45 tests carried, no engine API had moved. Linear, two-line **and radius/diameter place dimensions**, and the snap query is wired. `canvas/measure/circular.rs` joined them when the radius/diameter tool was armed — the one measure gesture that does not end itself, so the machinery for *saying when it is over* is a subject the other two have nothing corresponding to. ★ **The scale model is reachable as of the Set-scale window**, and the whole group model with it: `panels::dimension_groups` (2026-08-19) creates, renames, deletes, recalibrates and restyles a group, and carries the ***Draw into*** picker — which is the control the feature had been missing entirely. `MeasureState::group` had existed since the Phase 7 salvage, documented as the group picker, and **nothing in this build ever wrote to it**: a second group could be created from the CLI, carry its own scale, and be joinable by nothing |
| ✅ | **Redaction — mark, review, apply, with the true-removal proof salvaged** — 2026-08-15. Mandatory rather than convenient: core still has **no verdict type**, `apply_redactions` returns a *report*, and `pdfcer` exits SUCCESS on a file it never verified — so the proof came across from the old shell with the code. **Made unskippable four ways**: the prepared bytes are private with no accessor and a `Debug` that reports only a length; the single exit re-proves over the exact buffer between it and the syscall, so the guarantee belongs to the *write* rather than the constructor; the residual acknowledgement is a required argument rather than a convention, so a forgotten checkbox yields a refusal and not a partly-redacted file; and `redact::sealed` parses every `.rs` with `syn` to assert the engine's removal is called from exactly **one file** and exactly **twice** in it — failing closed on zero as well as on three. ★★★ **2026-09-04: applying is now an EDIT, and Save decides where it lands** — `OPERATOR_REQUESTS.md` O125, *"why can't it just wait on saving until I choose to save over the existing file or save as a new file?"* The default destination applies the removal into the open document and writes nothing; a new file and replace-the-original stay for when he wants it written now, the second behind a checkbox naming the file and an atomic temp-then-rename. This row used to read **"Always a save-as"** on the argument that overwriting the source destroys the only remaining copy of the content being removed. The premise is still true and the conclusion was his to make, not ours. ★★ **The property that had to survive the change was measured, not assumed**: a redacted session's *incremental* save cannot leak, because `EditSession::apply_redactions` (`Pass 250.1`) collapses the session onto the redacted bytes as a new base — no `/Prev` at all straight after the apply, and after a further edit a `/Prev` pointing at the redacted base. ★ It FINALIZES: the whole undo log goes, so the dialog names the step count above the confirm control, and the unsaved-edits predicate grew a third term because a collapsed session reports itself unmodified and would have been closed without a prompt. The harness asserts byte-for-byte that the write-now destinations leave the opened file untouched. Proven by a **second process** extracting nothing from the redacted page, with the falsifier running on every invocation — the secret must be *found* in the fixture first, so a scan that cannot see it is a failure rather than a pass |
| ✅ | **★ Markup ▸ Style — the group that was a caption over an empty band.** `RIBBON_IA.md` §5.5 specifies *Colour · Line width · Fill · Opacity* and the manifest has declared `Item::custom("colour_swatch")` since S2 — **and no renderer ever matched that kind**, so the shell reserved the item's space, the application declined to draw it, and the group rendered a caption over nothing for the whole of v0.1.0. The pen was two hard-coded constants, so every mark this shell has ever authored is red at 2 pt with no way to change either; §5.5 predicted the operator's report in advance — *"which is why a placed markup feels final"*. Now: **two colour swatches** (ink and highlighter, separate because setting a pen colour must not change your highlighter) and a **width** in points over the pen's own 0.25–12 range. The pen rides **in the action**, not read at apply time, so a mark is authored with the pen the operator had when the gesture completed and the action stays replayable. **Fill and Opacity are still ⬜ and for different reasons**: fill is a design decision on record (*"a filled comment shape hides the drawing it is a comment about"*), opacity is `/CA` and blocked on the engine — absent rather than greyed, because no setting in this build makes it reachable. **A third shape of invisible wiring**, and the quietest of the three: the manifest test asserted the item was *declared* and passed correctly, and the reachability check cannot see a `Custom` item at all because it carries no command id. Caught by `ui-verify --check markup_style_group_is_drawn`, which fails on the previously shipped build |
| ⬜ | **Canvas drag-to-mark for redaction** — panel marking (whole page, literal text, pattern) covers all three routes the tooltip promises, none of which is a drag. The gesture would need a `CanvasTool` variant, a capability entry, an Escape rung, an overlay preview and an `Action` carrying page-space quads — the tool-substrate warning applied to the one irreversible verb in the program |
| 🔨 | **Forms** — **fill** ✅ (all three modes since 2026-08-14), and ★ **Reset** and **Flatten** are reachable too, *through the panel rather than through the ribbon*: both are buttons in the Forms panel's own collapsed sections, calling `reset_form` and `flatten_fields`, while `edit.form_flatten` on Edit ▸ Forms is still `SCAFFOLDED` and inert. **That split is a reporting hazard worth naming**: a register that records the *command id* reports Flatten as unreachable and Reset as absent, when an operator can do both. Record the **surface**, not the id. Create field, rename, delete and FDF/XFDF/CSV are still ⬜. Filling found a genuine `pdfcer-core` defect: `fill_refusal()` omits two of the three guards `fill_guards()` applies |
| ✅ | **Fill a field on the canvas** — click the widget on the page and type; Enter or clicking away commits **one** `FormEdit::FillText`, Escape abandons the draft without also ascending a selection rung. Check boxes toggle, radio widgets select *that widget's* on-state. **No `CanvasTool` variant**: a widget's `/Rect` is a region the file itself marks interactive, so a press inside it is unambiguous — markup needed a variant precisely because a drag on blank paper is not. An *unfocused* widget registers **no interactive rect**, so pans, marquees, hover and Ctrl+wheel over a field are untouched; only the focused field is a real `TextEdit`, and it therefore owns its own drags. The hit test takes **no tolerance** — a tolerance exists so a hairline can be hit, and form fields sit a point apart, so a 6 px catch radius would silently focus the neighbour of the field aimed at |
| ✅ | **What a fill inferred is in the status bar** — `applied_autosize` and `unencodable_chars` are the only two facts of a fill **not re-derivable from the saved document**: afterwards they look exactly like the author's decision. The Forms panel showed them, which sufficed while the panel was the only way to fill; canvas filling can happen in **Read mode with the panel closed**, so the bar — present in every mode — now carries them too. Keyed on the edit epoch, so any later edit or an undo retires the sentence with nothing having to remember to clear it, and asserted not to change the bar's height (R128) |
| ⬜ | **Highlight fillable fields** — a View ▸ Display toggle tinting widget rects, Acrobat-style. Today the *cursor* is the whole discovery mechanism, which is honest but weaker; it is also the only thing that would make an **undrawn** field discoverable |
| ✅ | **★★★ …and the LAST of it is gone too — 2026-08-28, three Passes after it was first questioned.** `Reading::find` — the longest-contiguous-glyph-stretch walk — is deleted. Its story in three acts, because the shape is more useful than the code was: **(1)** built on a mechanism this project invented and never measured (*"the extraction synthesises a space where a `TJ` offset exceeds the word-gap threshold"*), which `pdfcer-core` refuted over 256 fixtures — **zero** such runs; **(2)** correct anyway, which is the least comfortable outcome of the four, and only knowable once the invariant it rested on was measured — 4,289 files, 29,246 operator spans, zero exceptions, sabotage-checked; **(3)** kept alive one extra day solely to feed `preview_font_resources`, whose coverage gate took the text as a *parameter*, so an empty `find` tested **zero characters and reported every face as accepted**. That trap was filed rather than absorbed, `Pass 147.0` made the pre-flight resolve the pin through the same `effective_find` the commit path uses — **and the engine's own note says our alternative was needed too**: they fixed the pinned case and assumed an unpinned empty `find` already errored, and a test proved `s.text.contains("")` is true of every string. Both halves shipped. ⇒ Nothing feeds the field now, so it is **deleted rather than kept "in case"**: a mechanism with no caller rots, and the next reader cannot tell a deliberate fallback from a forgotten one. Driven after the deletion: `applied=19 runs=14` on the operator's SW41177, identical to before |
| ✅ | **★★★ The shell's second text locator is DELETED — 2026-08-27, and the engine measured the invariant it had been resting on.** A pinned restyle used to need a `find` string naming a contiguous sub-range inside the operator, so this shell sliced each run's text into per-operator pieces to build them. That slicing was a **second locator living beside the engine's**, and its stated justification had already been refuted. `Pass 145.0` made `FormatRequest::whole_operator(page, span)` mean *the whole pinned operator*, filed as its own one-topic request and shipped the same day; `pin::Operator` no longer carries a `find` or the byte cursor that extended it. ★★★ **The measurement that made deleting it safe rather than hopeful**: 4,289 fixture files, 18,559 runs, 669,436 glyphs, **29,246 distinct operator spans — zero non-contiguous, zero whose slice did not index the run's text cleanly**, and the detector sabotage-checked so a green result is not vacuous. It is now a documented guarantee with a test on every `cargo test`. ★★ **The same probe confirmed the third claim this project made and could not verify: 2,420 of 18,559 runs — 13 % — carry glyphs from more than one show operator.** Common, not exotic, which is why *a run is not an operator* is a rule rather than a curiosity. ★ Driven before and after: `applied=19 runs=14` on the operator's SW41177, identical. **A trap was found on the way and filed**: `preview_font_resources` still takes the coverage text as a parameter where `FormatRequest` now resolves it, so an empty `find` there reports **every face as accepted** — worse than the superset it replaced, and silent. One field is kept alive solely to feed it, and says so |
| ✅ | **★★★ The font list stopped being a guess — 2026-08-27, and it was wrong in a way nobody had reported.** The face chooser (both of them — the Properties panel's and the Format ribbon's) now lists exactly the faces `EditSession::set_font` has already said it would accept **for this run**, through `preview_font_resources` (`Pass 142.1`, asked for by name that afternoon and shipped that night). ★★ **Two holes closed, and only one had been filed.** The old list came from `fontinfo::FontInventory`, which is keyed on the font **dictionary** while `set_font` matches on `/BaseFont` and then asks whether the face can encode *these characters* — so it offered entries that could not work, which is the reported half. The unreported half is worse: one page can carry **two dictionaries sharing one `/BaseFont`** — two subsets of one face, which the Fonts panel's own survey found in **87 % of embedding files** — and a name match reached one of the twins **arbitrarily**. That is not a refusal an operator can see; it is the wrong font, applied. `FontResourceEntry::selector` reaches the resource the row is about, and a shared name is disclosed on hover. ⇒ The lesson, recorded on both sides: **a superset is not a mild error when its elements are indistinguishable.** ★ Refused faces are **absent, not greyed with their reason**: the engine hands over a good per-character sentence, and a combo of twelve faces with nine greyed rows each explaining a character is a control nobody can read — R9's not-applicable case. A page where nothing can show the run says so in one line rather than showing an empty combo. ★★ **`faces_on_page` is deleted, not kept as a fallback** — a workaround whose cause is removed rots, and the next reader cannot tell a deliberate fallback from a forgotten one. ★ **Costs nothing measurable**: a fourteen-run Bold on the operator's SW41177 ran 1,075 ms before and 1,083 ms after, because the pre-flight shares the draft's existing extraction. That is only true because the first draft of the accessor re-ran `inspect()` — 392 ms, already paid by its only caller — and the doubling was caught before it shipped |
| ✅ | **★★ Bold reaches the covering face on a page that has one — `Pass 144.0`, filed and fixed the same day.** A driven Bold press on the engine's own `format_family.pdf` found that `gate_synthesis` refused synthesis *"a REAL bold face is available"* while naming `Times-Bold`, which remaps `o` to a bullet and cannot show the run — and `Calibri-Bold` on the same page could. **Bold was unreachable there through either verb.** The engine reproduced all three commands on the release binary before accepting the report; the cause was a predicate deciding *"a real bold face is available"* from two string tests on `/BaseFont`, never asking whether the face could show the run's characters. ★ This shell's characterisation test had **predicted its own failure** — *"when the engine picks a covering face, this test starts failing on assertion 1, and that failure is the good news"* — and it failed eight hours later. Rewritten to assert the face **by name**, because a build falling back to synthesis would also move the run and bump the epoch and would be the second-best answer |
| ✅ | **★★★ A box's border and where it is shown — 2026-08-27, filed at 22:40 and shipped within the hour.** The *This box* section now carries all four of `WidgetEdit`'s properties: position, size, **border style and width**, **where it is visible**, and the caption. ★★★ **The two new ones were deliberately ABSENT an hour earlier, and the reason is the whole point.** They were writable and not readable, so a control would have had to show a value it had invented — *"a properties control has to show the current value; one seeded from a default would display Solid 1 pt over a widget whose file says Dashed 3 pt and write the invention back on the first press"* — which is the failure the text colour swatch already refuses for CMYK. Filed under decision 058 rather than worked around; `Pass 146.0` added `Widget::border`, `Widget::visibility` and `Widget::annot_flags`, and the engine quoted that sentence into the field's own doc comment, into `docs/core-api/` and into their test header *"because the next person to touch this will be tempted to simplify it"*. ★★ **`None` is a fact this pane displays, never a default it substitutes.** A file that states no border shows a **dash** and offers no width at all — choosing a style is how a widget gets one, which is an act rather than a default. A border of **width 0** is a *value* (Table 166 states it as "no border") and shows as `0 pt`; collapsing it to absent would say the file is silent when it has spoken. And `visibility: None` means the file carries flags pdfcer cannot set — `Print | NoZoom`, say — so the pane names them in hex and says they are left alone, because an absent `/F` is `0` which IS one of the four settable values, so `None` can never mean *absent*. ★ Both `match`es are **exhaustive with no catch-all**: a sixth border style added to the engine must fail to build here rather than silently render as "Another style" |
| ✅ | **★★★ A form field's BOX can be moved and resized — 2026-08-27.** Click a field and the Properties pane's *This box* section gives X, Y, Width, Height and a caption, with an Apply. ★★ **Moving is free and resizing is not, and the pane says which you are about to do**: §12.5.5 derives the appearance matrix from the appearance box's corners and the `/Rect` corners, so a pure translation moves baked artwork exactly and for nothing, while a changed **extent** makes the same algorithm *scale* it — a text field dragged twice as wide would render its text twice as wide rather than gaining room for more. `edit_widget` compares the extent, not the corners, and rebuilds only when it changed; the Apply hover names the act before the press and the status line reports which happened after it. ★ `appearance_stale` is surfaced because it is the one an operator SEES and misreads: a resize that could not rebuild the artwork — a push button's baked caption, a signature — leaves the widget rendering **distorted**. ★★ **Two of `WidgetEdit`'s four properties are absent, and it is a boundary report rather than a deferral.** Border (`/BS`) is writable and **not readable** — `annot_author::read_border_width` is private, `border_style` is a *writer*, and `forms::Widget` models no border at all; visibility (`/F`) is readable only by matching a widget to its annotation by `ObjId` and deriving the engine's enum from raw flag bits ourselves. A properties control has to show the current value, and one seeded from a default would display *Solid 1 pt* over a widget whose file says *Dashed 3 pt* and write the invention back on the first press — the same failure the text colour swatch refuses for CMYK. Filed. ★★ **DRIVEN**: place a field, click it, scroll to the box controls, confirm Apply is **dead** until a number moves, scrub X, press Apply, and `edit-widget … disclosures=The box was moved.` reaches the document — *moved*, not *resized*, which is the extent comparison proving itself. Falsified by cutting the section out |

It scrolls, and *"I clicked the field and there is nothing there"* is what three scroll-lengths look like from a chair. **The remedy is a layout decision and it is the operator's**: collapsible groups (which cost every collapsed control its published rect, so the driven checks would have to open them), a taller default slot at the cost of Objects, or the dialog route O39 already uses for *placing* a field. Recorded rather than absorbed — the driven check works around it by driving at a taller window, which is right for a check and is not an answer for the product |
| ✅ | **★★★ …and imports back, so the round trip closes — 2026-08-28.** File ▸ Export ▸ Import form data reads an FDF, XFDF or CSV and sets this document's field values from it, with the **extension deciding the parser** exactly as it decides the format on the way out — one convention for both halves, so a file exported as `.csv` and imported as `.csv` cannot land in a branch nobody chose. ★★ **One `Ctrl+Z` takes the whole import back**, and that is the engine's doing rather than this shell's: `import_form_data` is a single `EditSession` command however many fields it sets, and it asks the document-wide gate **once, up front**, so a certified document is refused before anything is written rather than half-imported. The tooltip says both, because *"replacing any values it names"* is the fact an operator needs before pressing. ★ **Three failures, told apart**: unreadable (the path or permissions), unparseable (wrong file, or a format pdfcer does not read), and refused (the **document** will not take an import — no form, certified, encrypted). A single "import failed" would send an operator whose document is certified off to re-export their data, twice. ★ **`skipped` is disclosed when it is non-zero**: a data file may legitimately name a superset of this document's fields, so importing forty values into a thirty-field form leaves ten untouched and nothing else would say so. ★★★ **DRIVEN AS A ROUND TRIP, in two processes** — export, then reopen the document clean and import what was just written. Two verbs in one check deliberately: the only defect class that matters about a pair is an **asymmetry**, a writer and a reader that each pass their own test and disagree with each other, and a separate import check fed a hand-authored fixture would test the parser against bytes the harness author chose rather than bytes pdfcer chose. ★★★ **And the first run found one — in the check.** It reported *"the import applied NOTHING"* over a trace whose very next line read `applied=1 skipped=0`: `vector_edit`'s funnel label and the module's own summary shared the trace name `import-form-data`, so `.last()` read the wrong one. **The identical defect `text-style` had one day earlier**, made again by the same session that had written up the first — so the fix is a **naming convention** (`-applied` suffix on a module's summary, bare name for the funnel) rather than a third note. Filed to `D:/dev/rag/egui/` with the general form: an incident does not generalise itself |

Like `edit.form_flatten` two hours earlier it cited a `FEATURES.md` row that was itself stale: **a citation of a citation, with nothing re-reading either.** ★★ Both were found by the rule written on the allow-list's own count assertion when the *fifth* was found — *when you touch this list for any purpose, re-derive the reason of the entry beside the one you came for* — which has now paid for itself twice on the day it was written. No test can catch this class: the assertion counts entries, and an entry whose id has no arm and whose reason is nonsense is indistinguishable from a correct one. ★★★ **The CSV disclosure is the sentence this feature exists to say.** `formcsv::to_csv` neutralises values beginning `=`, `+`, `-` or `@` so a spreadsheet does not execute them as formulas — correct, and **silent** unless somebody says so, because a neutralised value looks completely ordinary in the file. The count is stated and the fields are named, worded as an act rather than an alarm (a test forbids "error", "failed" and "warning" in it) because an operator who reads it as a fault will go looking for the switch that turns a protection off. ★★ **DRIVEN, and the oracle is the FILE.** A build that computed the bytes, traced `export-form-data fields=3` and wrote them nowhere would satisfy a trace-only check completely and ship an Export button that exports nothing — so the check reads the disk, asserts the `%FDF-` header, and asserts the file names a field of the operator's form. **Falsified twice**: with the dispatch arm removed it reports *"invoked and nothing happened"*, and with the write replaced by `Ok(())` it reports *"traced success and wrote no file"*. ★ Import is the mirror and is still unwired |
| ✅ | **★★★ Field list in tab order — and since 2026-09-02 it REORDERS by dragging.** A per-page, widget-level view beside the fill list, which stays in `/AcroForm /Fields` order because that matches the printed form. Each row therefore carries its **slot** in the array, and widgets move among widget slots while every other entry keeps its index. ★★ That second rule is a **choice**, and the engine’s `non_widgets_moved` disclosure is what revealed the need for it: `/Annots` order is **paint order**, so carrying a widget past a `/Link` would change what is drawn over what — a visible change to the page, from a gesture whose entire subject was tab sequence. Zero from this route by construction. ★ **Ids, not indices** — the engine asked for that by name, and it was more necessary than they knew: a row’s `position` is 1-based, counts widgets only, and skips entries with no id, so it is wrong on three axes at once and all three cancel on a well-formed form. ⬜ **`/Tabs` is read and never written**, sourced: `/A` is PDF-2.0-only, PDF/UA-1 §7.18.3 *requires* `/S`, nothing requires a writer to state a tab order at all, and Acrobat’s own manual tab order writes none — so on a page that states `/S`, `/R` or `/C` a conforming reader may still tab in the stated order, which is what the unconditional per-page `/Tabs` sentence above the rows is for. It must report an absent `/Tabs` as **absent** and must not translate that into a mode name: what Acrobat’s *Unspecified* state mechanically denotes is recorded as **unsourced after two attempts**, so naming it would assert something nobody has been able to source. ◑ **NOT YET DRIVEN** — the check is written and needs a window in which the operator is away from the machine |

The three things agreed in advance for the day it landed all hold: a non-permutation is refused as `reorder_pages` refuses one (with `missing`, `unknown` and `repeated` named, so the UI can point at the row), it is **one undo entry**, and **paint order moving with it is disclosed** — `non_widgets_moved`. ★★ The disclosure turned out to matter more at **design** time than at run time: reading it is what showed that the obvious implementation would silently change z-order, so the shell’s permutation is slot-preserving and reports zero on every call. A counter that reads zero forever looks like dead weight from the engine’s side; its whole value was delivered before a line of the gesture was written. ★ The request’s reasoning was an *extension* of the engine’s rule R104, whose stated scope is field **insertion** — the paint-order consequence is identical but the inference was ours, and the engine’s reply confirmed it. ⬜ Two things deliberately **not** taken: `set_page_tabs` (offered, not asked for — the trigger would be an operator reporting that an arranged order did not survive into another reader), and cross-page reordering (a different edit, which the verb cannot express) |

| ✅ | **★★ The page cache was a frame buffer with extra steps — fixed 2026-08-19 on the operator's report** *("increase cache to maximum for page view so they don't constantly redraw with larger files")*. `StripRasters::retain` took the **visible** page set and dropped everything outside it, every settle, and the 48 MB budget it also enforced almost never bit because the set it was handed was already tiny. So scrolling one page and back re-rastered — on `SW41177.pdf` that is a full strip decode for a page whose pixels had been thrown away a second earlier, and it is exactly the *"constantly redraw"* the report names. **A cache keyed on what is on screen right now is not a cache**; it is a frame buffer, and the LRU underneath it had nothing to do. `retain` now takes only the budget, so the eviction order is genuinely least-recently-used and a page that leaves the viewport stays until something needs its texels. ★ **A test was pinning the defect** — `pages_that_left_the_viewport_are_dropped` asserted the behaviour being removed, which is what a test does when it is written from the implementation rather than from the requirement; it was reversed in place to `…_are_kept_until_the_budget_bites` rather than deleted, so the file records that the property flipped deliberately. The ceiling went 48 MB → **256 MB**, and it is now a **preference** — Settings ▸ Drawing the page, four steps (Small · Medium · Large · **Maximum**), with the megabyte figure derived from the texel count rather than stored beside it, because two numbers that must agree eventually will not. Driven by `pages_stay_drawn_when_you_scroll_back`, whose oracle is a **duplicate page number in `strip-raster-requested`**: scroll forty notches down and forty back, and a page asked for twice is a cache that dropped it |

The two that were real — **render quality** (a raster-scale multiplier; `viewer::raster_scale` had none, so this is new capability rather than an exposed constant) and **zoom settle** (`ZOOM_SETTLE` was a compiled-in 150 ms) — became Settings ▸ **Drawing the page**, because a value set once and forgotten is not an activity, which is what P2 says a ribbon tab picks. Every control, label and icon has been drawn at one size on every machine for the life of the project. **Why nobody noticed is the reusable part** — `egui` ships a built-in `Ctrl` `+`/`-`/`0` handler for exactly this, so on a stock `eframe` app the capability appears to exist without anyone building it, and this shell switches that handler off *for a good reason* (those chords mean **page** zoom, as in every browser and in Acrobat). Closing the one path that would have surfaced it removed a feature nobody had decided to have. Now `Settings ▸ Appearance`, 0.8–2.0 in twentieths, shown as a percentage: a **multiplier on the OS display setting**, never a replacement, so an operator who fixes their scaling once does not have to fix pdfcer separately. Live-previewed from the draft **like the theme and for the identical reason** — you cannot judge a scale from a number, you choose it by seeing whether you can read the ribbon — which makes Appearance the group of the two settings that take effect before Save. A settings control and **no chord**: Inkscape and SolidWorks both put it in preferences and neither binds a key, Acrobat has no such control, and `Ctrl+` digits here are the mode selector |
| ✅ | **★ The freehand tolerance follows the pen again — a defect the Style group introduced and this module had predicted.** `ink::SIMPLIFY_TOLERANCE_PTS` was a `const` = `PEN_WIDTH_PTS / 4`, and the derivation is not decorative: Ramer–Douglas–Peucker bounds how far the drawn centreline can move, so setting ε to half the stroke's half-width is what guarantees *no pixel of the mark moves outside the mark*. §3.2 wrote down the rule for the day the input changed — *"if the pen ever becomes an operator control, the tolerance follows it"* — the pen became one on 2026-08-17, and the `const` went on deriving itself from the **default** 2 pt. At the 0.25 pt pen that exists to match a CAD sheet's own linework, a fixed 0.5 pt ε is **four times** the half-width, so the simplification could author a curve the operator did not draw, silently, on the thinnest strokes. Now `Pen::simplify_tolerance_pts`, beside the width it derives from, and asserted at **both ends** of the operator's range — the old test compared two constants and so kept passing through the whole regression. **The lesson is not about ink:** a constant derived from another value is safe exactly as long as that value is also a constant, and when the input becomes settable nothing about the expression looks wrong |
| ✅ | **★ The opening view — two more preferences, 2026-08-17, and the first two from the `NO_SURFACE.md` sweep rather than from the commissioned list.** `ViewState::default` held **fit-page** and **all three View ▸ Display overlays off** as compiled-in constants, and the sweep's note against them was *"toggles exist; the default is not settable."* That is worse than it sounds, because there is no per-document memory of them either — `viewer::remembered` persists the page-display arrangement and nothing else, deliberately — so an operator who works with rulers on flicked the same switch on every document, forever, and the program never learned. Now **Settings ▸ Drawing the page** carries *How a page is sized when a document opens* (whole page · full width · actual size) and *What is switched on when a document opens* (rulers · grid · guides). The trio is **one setting with three switches**, not three settings, because they interlock: a guide is dragged out of a ruler gutter, so guides-without-rulers is a switch that appears to do nothing, and saying that once beats printing it three times. Read **once per open**, in `PdfcerApp::adopt` — not in `adopt_settings`, which also runs on every settings Save and would have snapped the operator's current page back to fit and switched their rulers off. Guides **OR** rather than assign, so a document that has remembered guides still opens showing them: work the operator did outranks a default about documents in general |
| ✅ | **★ …and the copy contract had stopped covering the newest group.** `every_setting_states_its_silence_and_its_radius` listed **thirteen** triples — exactly the `pdfcer_core::settings` entries — and neither of the 2026-08-17 preferences had ever reached it, so a test reading as though it covered the window was checking a subset of it. The `header` helper's required arguments still forced three answers; nothing forced them to be non-empty, which is the whole failure the test exists for. Fixed both ways: the list is now **17**, and a new `syn` check parses the eight group modules and counts the `widgets::header` calls the dialog **actually makes**, asserting it equals what the catalog describes. A catalog cannot notice a setting that is not in it, so the guard had to count from the other end. `include_str!` makes a moved module a compile error, so "scanned nothing" cannot pass as "found nothing" |
| ✅ | **`userdata/preferences.txt`** — the shell's own store, beside `settings.txt` under the same roof so the update instructions cover it unchanged. Separate from `pdfcer_core::settings` because that file's every entry cites a clause the standard leaves silent and *how sharply a page is drawn* cites nothing; filing it there would make the settings window's own opening paragraph dishonest. Same flat `key = value` grammar and the same **per-key recovery**: an unknown key is kept and reported, a bad value falls back for that key alone, one bad line never discards the rest |
| ✅ | **Icons** — **72 glyphs**, rasterized at physical pixel size, tinted from the theme. All **65 keys** named by commands resolve, asserted against the live registry. An unknown key draws a **visible slashed mark**, never a blank: the label fallback is decided upstream of the painter, so drawing nothing silently reproduces the blank boxes it exists to prevent |
| ✅ | **Every ribbon control is a glyph or a recorded refusal** — 2026-08-14. It was 47 named and 41 bare with **no rule behind which was which**, so a band drew pictures and words side by side and the ribbon read as half-finished because it was. Now **77 of 88 named, 11 refused**, each refusal argued at its own registration: `view.zoom_actual` because the icon spec argues against it by name, the five `view.render_*` because their labels *are* the control's content, `view.app_initiative` because every honest drawing of it pictures what its default forbids, and the three mode segments because the renderer draws text there and a key would be art nothing can draw. Three glyphs were redrawn after being rendered at real size — `reset-layout` read as an umbrella, `form-flatten` was indistinguishable from `download`, and `zoom-region`'s lens filled to a disc |
| ⬜ | **Text editing** — the whole tool |
| ✅ | **★★★ A refused text edit says WHY, in his words — 2026-09-05, `OPERATOR_REQUESTS.md` O140, driven on his own file at his own typo.** His report: *"on page 2 there is a spelling mistake — clien instead of client. if I try to edit the edit is not accepted. **the lines I added below `price)` are editable, but everything else that existed when I got the pdf is not.**"* ★★★ **The diagnosis everybody reached first was wrong, including the feature request filed at the engine that morning**, and it is retracted: the cause is not `Identity-H`. `AAAAAA+Arimo-Bold` — one of the three faces that request named as blocked — **edits end to end on that page** (`edit-text --page 2 --find "n" --replace "t"`, read back), and so does `pdfcer-core`'s own `composite-editable.pdf`, which carries the identical `verdict=blocked-identity` line. **The real cause is that his producer emits one show operator per glyph** — page 2's stream is `(letter) Tj  (move) Td` repeated — and `edit_text` replaces text inside *one* operator, so a five-character `find` can never match. His own added lines are one operator per line, which is exactly the contrast he noticed. ⇒ **The lesson: his editable and uneditable text differed in TWO ways at once, and we attributed the difference to the one that has a column in a report.** `list-fonts` prints a verdict; nothing prints a producer's batching. The tell we ignored was that the engine answered `NoMatch` and not `R-INV-*` — a font refusal names the font. ★★ **What shipped**: `pdfcer_core::text_edit::RefusalKind` is **consumed** — the coarse four-bucket discriminant the engine shipped at `b1033ab` in answer to this project's own 2026-09-04 request, which had sat unused for a day because `check-verb-coverage.sh` and `check-engine-backlog.sh` are both keyed on `EditSession`'s **verbs** and *a new public type is invisible to both*. `crate::text::status::edit_declined_by_engine`'s documentation had said in as many words that it was *"written to be deleted"* the day `EditError` gained a coarse kind; that day had come and nothing noticed. ★★★ **And the shell adds the one fact the engine cannot have.** `NotFound` is the engine's honest answer and *"pdfcer couldn't find what the edit named"* reads, from his chair, as *"your search string is wrong"* — he typed no search string. `Plan::one_operator` carries `pin::spans_one_operator`'s measurement out of the planner (it was traced and thrown away before), and `EditRefusal::of` joins the two: `NotFound` **plus a split run** is `SplitAcrossPieces`, `NotFound` on a single-operator run is `TextMovedAway`, and the other three buckets ignore the measurement. Five sentences, matched exhaustively, the compiler proving the set complete because `RefusalKind` is deliberately not `#[non_exhaustive]`. ★ **The caret is still offered**, against R9's first reading and deliberately: it anchors `edit.reflow_block` and `format_text` as well as the replace, and both of those work on a split run — withdrawing the anchor would take three working features away to save one wasted keystroke. ⚠ **And a forecast on the font was written, tested and DELETED before it shipped**: `Identity-H ⇒ refuse the caret` would have blocked editing on text pdfcer can edit, on every document a modern producer makes, silently — `Refusal::InsideForm`'s episode with a different predicate. ⚠ **No remedy is offered, because none exists and it was checked rather than assumed**: `delete_text_run` removes one show operator, which here is one letter; `add_text` writes bundled Helvetica at a position the operator would place by eye; and the one-character edit that *does* work at the command line has no gesture that names *which* letter. Both filed. ★★ **Driven**: `a_refused_typo_fix_says_why_it_was_refused`, PASS, and **it takes its negative control through the same instrument in the same process** — it commits an edit that succeeds and asserts the `⊗` slot stays silent, because a probe that only watches something appear cannot tell a working build from one that shows the sentence always. Falsified three ways, each planted and restored: classification removed → *THE REFUSAL NAMES NO CATEGORY*; category forwarded instead of resolved → *THE CATEGORY AND THE MEASUREMENT DISAGREE*; the slot published unconditionally → *THE DECLINE SLOT DREW AFTER AN EDIT THAT SUCCEEDED, so the positive half of this check is NOT a verdict*. ⚠ **One limitation, named**: `disclosure_line` truncates and hangs the rest on hover, so only the first clause is read at a glance — the sentences are written front-loaded for that, and the truncation is the slot's existing behaviour rather than something this change introduced |
| ⬜ | **A click on a page that has ANY text cannot start new text** — found while looking for bare paper for O140's negative control, and it is his own 2026-08-19 request quietly not working. `place::click`'s `Edit` arm turns a `Refusal::NoRun` into an origin draft — *"click in text to edit it, click in space to start some"*, his words — but `pdfcer-core`'s `EditableTextModel::hit_test` ends with *"fall back to the nearest line by baseline distance"* and applies **no distance bound**. So on a page carrying any text at all, `NoRun` is unreachable and that arm is dead. Driven on his file: a click **215 pt to the right** of a run's bounding box and one **99 pt below** it both resolved that same run. `edit.add_text` is unaffected — it never asks the hit test — so the capability is reachable, by the other of the two doors, and nothing says which door you are at. Filed rather than worked around; the fix is a distance bound and it is the engine's to set, because the shell cannot tell a deliberate snap from an unbounded one |

---

## Not planned, and why

| | |
|---|---|
| ❌ | **A Home tab** — would mirror commands across tabs and re-create the Pass 47.1 defect |
| ❌ | **Automatic reflow on edit** — R75: reflow invents line breaks the file never stated. It is a **command** as of 2026-08-28 and will not become automatic |
| ❌ | **Tiled rendering** — measured as a 9× regression |
| ❌ | **JavaScript execution** — standing refusal |
| ❌ | **Provisional styling on the canvas** — Rule 4. Disclosure lives off-canvas; a second rendering path for the same content drifts |
